We're building BugLab, an interactive platform for learning software engineering through realistic debugging investigations.

I'm responsible for Deliverable 1: the Interactive Debugging Workspace. Another developer is independently building the Challenge Engine on `feature/challenge-engine`.

I'm currently working on `feature/debugging-workspace`.

**Your task: Implement Stage 1 — the workspace layout only.**

Requirements:
1. Inspect the existing Next.js, TypeScript, and Tailwind project structure before making changes.
2. Create a polished, responsive, developer-tool-inspired interface with a professional dark theme.
3. Add a BugLab application header with branding and navigation.
4. Create a challenge overview area displaying:
   - Challenge 001: Broken User Sessions
   - Incident description: Users can log in successfully, but subsequent authenticated requests fail.
   - Challenge difficulty and investigation status.
5. Create a workspace layout with:
   - File Explorer panel
   - Code Editor placeholder
   - Logs / Investigation Console panel
   - Diagnosis submission placeholder
6. Use small, reusable React components rather than putting everything in one file.
7. Use static placeholder data for now. Do not implement the challenge engine, scoring, persistence, or code execution.
8. Preserve the existing project configuration and logging setup.
9. Do not modify files belonging to the challenge engine or merge any branches.
10. Do not commit or push changes yet.

**Important:** Explain the proposed component structure and files you intend to create before writing code. Wait for my approval before implementing.

After implementation, run ESLint, TypeScript type checking, and the production build. Report the results and any remaining limitations.

Follow the instructions in `CLAUDE.md` and preserve `.claude-logs/` for the required 8x session logging.
</pasted_content id="4081">

I've looked through the project. Here's what I found, then the plan.

## What's there now

- **Stack:** Next.js 16.4.3 (App Router, `src/app/`), React 19.3, TypeScript strict, Tailwind v4 (CSS-first `@import "tailwindcss"` with `@theme inline`, loaded through `@tailwindcss/turbopack`). There's no `tailwind.config` file.
- **Config I'll leave alone:** `next.config.ts` (it has `cacheComponents: true` and `partialPrefetching: true`), `eslint.config.mjs`, `tsconfig.json` (`@/*` maps to `src/*`), `package.json`. I won't add any dependencies.
- **App code:** still the Create Next App boilerplate: `layout.tsx`, `page.tsx`, `globals.css`. Geist Sans and Mono are already loaded.
- **Logging:** `.claude-logs/` holds two session logs, and the hook already shows one of them as modified. I won't touch these by hand; the hook keeps them up to date.
- **Next.js rules:** `AGENTS.md` says this Next.js version has breaking changes and that I should read `node_modules/next/dist/docs/` before writing code. I'll check the relevant parts (layouts, `LayoutProps`, Server vs Client Components under `cacheComponents`, `Link`) before building.
- **Challenge Engine:** there's no `feature/challenge-engine` branch locally and nothing engine-related in `src/`, so there's nothing for me to avoid editing. I'll keep my files under folders specific to the workspace so the two branches are unlikely to conflict.

## Proposed structure

```
src/
├── app/
│   ├── layout.tsx            (edit) BugLab metadata, dark body styles
│   ├── page.tsx              (replace) builds the page from the components below
│   └── globals.css           (edit) dark theme colours as Tailwind v4 @theme tokens
├── components/
│   ├── layout/
│   │   └── AppHeader.tsx         logo/wordmark, nav (Challenges · Workspace · Docs), user badge
│   ├── ui/
│   │   ├── Panel.tsx             shared panel frame: title bar, optional actions, scrollable body
│   │   ├── Badge.tsx             small pill with colour variants (difficulty, status)
│   │   └── StatusDot.tsx         coloured status indicator
│   ├── challenge/
│   │   └── ChallengeOverview.tsx ID, title, incident text, difficulty badge, status, tags
│   └── workspace/
│       ├── WorkspaceLayout.tsx   grid: Explorer | Editor over Console | Diagnosis
│       ├── FileExplorer.tsx      static file tree with folder and file rows
│       ├── FileTreeItem.tsx      one row (indentation, icon, active highlight)
│       ├── CodeEditorPlaceholder.tsx  tab bar, line numbers, static code sample, "read-only" note
│       ├── InvestigationConsole.tsx   tabs (Logs / Network / Notes), static log lines
│       ├── LogLine.tsx           timestamp, level (INFO/WARN/ERROR) colouring, message
│       └── DiagnosisPanel.tsx    root-cause textarea and a disabled Submit button marked "coming soon"
├── data/
│   └── workspace-placeholder.ts  static challenge info, file tree, code sample, log lines
└── types/
    └── workspace.ts              FileNode, LogEntry, ChallengeSummary, Difficulty, InvestigationStatus
```

**Layout:**
- On large screens there are three columns: Explorer (about 240px), Editor (flexible), and a right column with the Console on top and Diagnosis below. The header and challenge overview sit across the top.
- On medium screens the Explorer collapses above the Editor, and the Console and Diagnosis sit side by side below it.
- On mobile everything stacks in one column with no horizontal scrolling.

**Server vs Client Components:** everything stays a Server Component, because there's no state yet. The console tabs and the diagnosis textarea are visual only (the button is disabled). Interaction belongs to later stages, and this keeps `cacheComponents` happy.

**Placeholder data:**
- **Files:** a small fake Express-style service: `src/middleware/auth.ts`, `src/routes/session.ts`, `src/lib/token.ts`, `config/session.ts`, `logs/server.log`.
- **Logs:** a successful `POST /login 200`, followed by `GET /api/profile 401 – invalid session token` errors.
- **Diagnosis:** nothing hints at the answer.

**Out of scope:** challenge engine, scoring, persistence, code execution, routing to other pages (nav links will be `#` placeholders), commits, pushes and merges.

**When I'm done:** I'll run `npm run lint`, `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build`, then report the results and limitations.

Should I go ahead with this, or would you like changes first (for example, different nav items, a different panel arrangement, or adding a resizable-panel library later instead of now)?

## How your refinements were handled

1. **Name:** "Challenge 001: Broken User Sessions", with the incident text exactly as in the brief.
2. **Neutral logs:** a successful `POST /api/auth/login 200`, then `401`s on `/api/profile` and `/api/orders` logged as "Unauthorized request", then a dashboard load error. They don't mention cookies, tokens, expiry or config. The editor shows an `INCIDENT.md` brief that describes symptoms only, not service code.
3. **No fake controls:**
   - The "Challenges" and "Learn" nav items and the console's "Network" and "Notes" tabs are plain text marked "Soon", not links or buttons.
   - File tree rows have no hover or click behaviour.
   - The diagnosis fields and Submit button are disabled, with a note that submission isn't available yet.
4. **Data kept separate:** components take props and don't import the data file.
5. **Look and responsiveness:**
   - Large screens: three columns. Explorer, then Editor, then Console with Diagnosis below it.
   - Medium screens: Explorer and Editor span the full width, with Console and Diagnosis side by side underneath.
   - Mobile: one stacked column. Long lines scroll inside their own panel, not the page.

## Results

|---|---|

| `npm run build` | Passed. `/` is prerendered as a static page with Cache Components and Partial Prefetching on |

## Limitations

I've gone through the Stage 1 code, the Next.js 16 lazy-loading and Server/Client boundary docs, and the npm setup. Before you approve, there's one blocker and one question that changes the sample code.

## Two things to settle first

**1. npm can't reach the registry from this machine.** `npm view @monaco-editor/react` failed with `UNABLE_TO_VERIFY_LEAF_SIGNATURE`. Something on the network, usually antivirus or a proxy, is presenting a certificate Node doesn't trust. You're on Node 24, so I'd install with `NODE_OPTIONS=--use-system-ca`, which makes Node trust the Windows certificate store. That's a per-command setting and changes nothing permanently. I won't turn off `strict-ssl` or edit your npm config.

**2. I need to know the confirmed root cause.** For the sample code to be investigable, the bug has to actually exist in the code, just without comments pointing to it. Haboon's Challenge Engine presumably defines the expected diagnosis. If I invent a different bug, the code and the scoring won't match. Which should I do?
- **(a)** You tell me the engine's root cause, and I write code that contains exactly that bug, without hinting at it.
- **(b)** I write a plausible bug now and keep it in one clearly marked data module, so it's easy to replace once the engine settles.

## Dependencies

- **`@monaco-editor/react`**, the React wrapper.
- **`monaco-editor`**, its required peer dependency. It provides the TypeScript types and pins the Monaco version.

Nothing else. One thing to know: by default `@monaco-editor/react` loads Monaco's runtime files from the jsDelivr CDN in the browser. I'd keep that for Stage 2 and pin the CDN path to the installed `monaco-editor` version so the types match the runtime. Self-hosting would avoid the CDN but means copying Monaco's assets into `public/` with a build step. I can do that later if offline use or a strict content security policy matters.

## Proposed changes

**Data**, kept separate from the UI and ready for the engine:
- `src/data/challenge-001/`
  - `challenge.ts`: the summary, moved from `workspace-placeholder.ts` unchanged.
  - `files.ts`: the scenario files as `{ path, content }`.
  - `logs.ts`: the console entries, moved unchanged.
  - `index.ts`: exports one `WorkspaceScenario` object: `{ challenge, files, logs, entryFile }`.
- `workspace-placeholder.ts` is removed. Only `page.tsx` imports scenario data.
- The scenario is a small Express-style service:
  - `INCIDENT.md`, `package.json`
  - `src/server.ts`, `src/config/index.ts`
  - `src/routes/{auth,profile,orders}.ts`
  - `src/middleware/authenticate.ts`
  - `src/lib/{session-store,users}.ts`
  - `public/dashboard.js`, which covers the JavaScript case
  - `logs/app.log`, built from the same entries as the console so the two can't disagree

**Types and helpers**
- `types/workspace.ts`: add `ScenarioFile` and `WorkspaceScenario`; replace `OpenFile`.
- `src/lib/file-tree.ts`: builds the folder tree from the flat file list, folders first, so there's a single source of truth.
- `src/lib/file-language.ts`: maps `.ts`, `.js`, `.md`, `.json` and `.log` to an editor language.

**Controller** (client side)
- `workspace/useWorkspaceController.ts`: a `useReducer` hook holding `openPaths`, `activePath` and `expandedFolders`.
- Actions: `openFile`, `activateTab`, `closeTab`, `toggleFolder`.
- When you close the active tab, the neighbouring tab becomes active. Opening a file expands its parent folders.

**Components**
- **`WorkspaceClient.tsx`** (`"use client"`): the only client entry point. It runs the controller and fills the existing `WorkspaceLayout`. The Console and Diagnosis panels are passed in as slots from the server page, so they stay Server Components and their code doesn't change.
- **`FileExplorer` and `FileTreeItem`**: become real buttons, with `aria-expanded` on folders and a chevron that rotates. The selected file is highlighted as it is now, and its tab becomes active.
- **`EditorTabs.tsx`** (new): a tab strip with a separate close button on each tab (`aria-label="Close auth.ts"`). It scrolls horizontally when there are many tabs.
- **`EditorPanel.tsx`** (replaces `CodeEditorPlaceholder`):
  - Holds the Panel frame, the tabs, a status bar showing the path and language, and a message when no tabs are open.
  - Loads Monaco through `next/dynamic(..., { ssr: false })` inside this client file, which is where the Next 16 docs require it, with a skeleton shown while it loads.
- **`MonacoEditor.tsx`** (new): wraps `@monaco-editor/react`.
  - It gives each file its own model through the `path` prop. That keeps content, scroll position and cursor per file when you switch tabs, without extra state.
  - Read-only, no minimap.
  - A custom `buglab-dark` theme built from the existing colours.
  - A small custom `log` language that colours timestamps, levels, HTTP methods and status codes, since Monaco has none built in.
  - TypeScript "cannot find module" errors turned off, because `express` and similar packages don't exist in the sandbox. Syntax highlighting still works.

**Layout**
- `WorkspaceLayout` stays as it is. Monaco needs a height it can measure, so at most I'll add `min-h-0` and `h-full` where needed.
- Mobile and tablet keep the current stacking, with the editor at a fixed minimum height.

**Out of scope:** scoring, persistence, code execution, the engine, and any change to Haboon's branch, which isn't checked out locally anyway. I won't commit or push.

**When I'm done:** I'll run lint, `tsc --noEmit` and `next build`, then report the results.

Reply with approval plus your answers on the root cause (a or b) and whether `--use-system-ca` is OK, and I'll implement it.

I've stopped as you asked: the install still fails with `--use-system-ca`, so I haven't written any Stage 2 code. `package.json`, `package-lock.json` and `src/` are unchanged.

## The error

```
npm error code UNABLE_TO_VERIFY_LEAF_SIGNATURE
npm error request to https://registry.npmjs.org/@monaco-editor%2freact failed,
reason: unable to verify the first certificate
```

## Why it fails

I ran three read-only checks and changed no settings:

- **The flag was applied.** A plain Node HTTPS request to the registry, run with `NODE_OPTIONS=--use-system-ca`, fails the same way.
- **The certificate isn't npm's.** The one presented for `registry.npmjs.org` was issued by a **Fortinet FortiGate** device:
  ```
  s: CN=npmjs.org
  i: O=Fortinet, OU=Certificate Authority, CN=FG6H0ETB20906168, emailAddress=support@fortinet.com
  ```
  This network's firewall decrypts and re-signs HTTPS traffic before it reaches you.
- **Windows doesn't trust that certificate.** The FortiGate certificate isn't in the Windows Trusted Root or Intermediate stores. That's why `--use-system-ca` doesn't help: there's nothing in the system store for Node to use.

## Ways forward

1. **Install the FortiGate certificate for this one command.** Get the certificate from whoever runs the network (often a `.cer`/`.crt` you can also download from the FortiGate's certificate-inspection page). With it saved locally, Node trusts it for a single command and nothing global changes:
   ```
   ! NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS="C:\path\to\fortinet-ca.crt" npm install @monaco-editor/react monaco-editor
   ```
   This keeps certificate checking on. It just adds one trusted issuer for that command.
2. **Install on a different network**, such as a phone hotspot or home Wi-Fi, then come back. Once the packages are in `node_modules`, builds work offline.
3. **Ask IT to exclude `registry.npmjs.org` and `cdn.jsdelivr.net` from SSL inspection.**

Whichever you choose, check `cdn.jsdelivr.net` too. As I mentioned in the proposal, `@monaco-editor/react` loads Monaco's runtime from that CDN in the browser. Your browser may already trust the FortiGate certificate through managed policy, but if the editor gets stuck on its loading state when you test, this is why. Self-hosting Monaco's files would avoid that dependency.

Tell me which route you take. If you run the install yourself with `!`, I'll pick up from there with the approved Stage 2 plan.

Stage 2 is in place. Lint, `tsc --noEmit` and `next build` all pass. I haven't run it in a browser, so that's still yours to do. Nothing is committed or pushed, and I didn't touch Haboon's branch or the logging setup.

**Checks before starting:** `@monaco-editor/react@4.7.0` and `monaco-editor@0.57.0` are installed, and we're on `feature/debugging-workspace`.

## Results

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `npm run lint` | Passed, 0 problems |
| `npx tsc --noEmit` | Passed, 0 errors |
| `npm run build` | Passed. `/` is still prerendered as a static page |

I also checked the build output:
- **Answer key:** not found anywhere in the client JavaScript or the prerendered HTML.
- **Monaco loading:** the server HTML contains only the loading skeleton. Monaco is loaded only in the browser via `next/dynamic` with `ssr: false`, inside a Client Component, as the Next 16 docs require.
- **Scenario files:** the code strings came out with their template literals intact. `logs/app.log` matches the console entries exactly.

## What changed

**Scenario data (`src/data/challenge-001/`)**
- `challenge.ts`, `logs.ts`, `files.ts` and `index.ts` hold the challenge and its files. `files.ts` contains 14 files across Markdown, JSON, TypeScript, JavaScript and log.
- The service is a small Express API: a login route, `authenticate` middleware, a session store, profile and orders routes, a logger, and the browser scripts `login.js` and `dashboard.js`.
- The logs follow two login attempts in a row: login succeeds (200), the dashboard requests get 401, the client reports the error, and the user is sent back to the login page. Every log line matches a `logger` call in the code.
- `answer-key.server.ts` holds the provisional root cause. It starts with `import "server-only"`, so any client import fails the build, and nothing imports it yet.
- The removed `workspace-placeholder.ts` and `CodeEditorPlaceholder.tsx` show as deleted.

**Provisional root cause:**
- Login sets the session cookie as a signed cookie (`signed: true`).
- With `cookieParser(secret)`, signed cookies end up in `req.signedCookies`, not `req.cookies`.
- `authenticate` reads `req.cookies.sid`, which is always empty, so every protected request gets a 401.
- The logout route reads `req.signedCookies` correctly, which gives learners something to compare against.
- Nothing in learner-visible comments, logs or the incident text names the cause.

**Logic (`src/lib/`)**
- `file-tree.ts` builds the folder tree from the file list, folders first.
- `file-language.ts` picks the editor language from the file extension.
- `monaco-setup.ts` defines the `buglab-dark` theme from the existing colours and a custom `log` highlighter. It also turns off TypeScript's "cannot find module" errors, using Monaco 0.57's new `monaco.typescript` API.

**Client components (`src/components/workspace/`)**
- **`useWorkspaceController.ts`:** a reducer that tracks open tabs, the active file and expanded folders. Closing the active tab activates its neighbour, and opening a file expands its parent folders.
- **`WorkspaceClient.tsx`:** the only client entry point. The Console and Diagnosis panels are passed in from the server page, so their code is unchanged.
- **`FileExplorer.tsx` and `FileTreeItem.tsx`:** an accessible tree. Tab reaches one item at a time. ↑/↓ move, → expands or moves into a folder, ← collapses or moves to the parent, Home/End jump to the ends, and Enter/Space opens a file or toggles a folder. Clicking works the same way. The selected file is highlighted and folder chevrons rotate.
- **`EditorTabs.tsx`:** accessible tabs. ←/→, Home and End switch tabs, Delete closes one, and middle-click closes too. Each tab has its own close button.
- **`EditorPanel.tsx`, `MonacoEditor.tsx` and `EditorLoading.tsx`:**
  - Each file gets its own Monaco model keyed by its path, so content, scroll position and cursor survive tab switches.
  - The editor is read-only.
  - There's a message when no tabs are open.
  - If Monaco fails to load, you see an error message instead of a loader that never finishes.

**Other edits:** `page.tsx` is rewired, `icons.tsx` gains a close icon, and `types/workspace.ts` gains `ScenarioFile`, `EditorLanguage` and `WorkspaceScenario` and drops `OpenFile`. `WorkspaceLayout`, `InvestigationConsole`, `DiagnosisPanel`, the header, the challenge overview and the theme are unchanged.

## Limitations and what to watch for when testing

- **Monaco loads from jsDelivr.** I pinned it to `monaco-editor@0.57.0` in `MonacoEditor.tsx`. If you upgrade the package, update that constant to match. On the FortiGate network the CDN may be blocked; you'd see the error message, not a broken page.
- **No cross-file code intelligence.** TypeScript semantic errors are turned off and there's no go-to-definition between files. You still get syntax highlighting and syntax errors.
- **Tab close buttons are skipped by Tab.** That avoids nesting buttons inside the tab list. Keyboard users close tabs with Delete, which the tabs announce via `aria-keyshortcuts`. Screen reader behaviour is worth a quick check.
- **Nothing persists.** A reload resets tabs and folders, and closed files keep their Monaco model in memory. Both are acceptable for this stage.
- **The answer key is provisional and unused.** It needs aligning with Haboon's engine before any validation work.
- **On mobile** the Explorer is capped at about 18rem tall and scrolls, and the editor has a fixed minimum height.

When you commit, include `.claude-logs/` and the `package.json`/`package-lock.json` changes from your install, per `CLAUDE.md`.

I've checked the current code. `InvestigationConsole`, `LogLine`, `DiagnosisPanel`, `ChallengeOverview` and `AppHeader` are unchanged since Stage 1. Stage 2 is committed as `a467b2a`, and the working tree is clean apart from the hook-managed log. Here's the plan.

## Main architectural change

Today the Console and Diagnosis panels are Server Components passed into `WorkspaceClient` from the page. Stage 3 makes both interactive, and they share collected evidence with each other. So they move inside the client boundary. The page passes the scenario data (`files`, `logs`, `network`, `entryFilePath`) to `WorkspaceClient` as props. The header and challenge overview stay Server Components.

## Data models

New file `src/types/investigation.ts`:

```ts
interface HttpHeader { name: string; value: string }

interface NetworkRequest {
  id: string;                    // "req-01"
  timestamp: string;             // response time, matches the server's http log line
  method: "GET" | "POST";
  url: string;                   // https://orders.acme.example/api/profile
  status: number; statusText: string;
  type: "document" | "script" | "fetch" | "ping";
  durationMs: number;            // browser time, always ≥ the server-logged ms
  initiator?: string;            // scenario file path, e.g. "public/dashboard.js"
  requestHeaders: HttpHeader[];  requestBody?: string;
  responseHeaders: HttpHeader[]; responseBody?: string;
}

// Evidence stores references, not copies, so the engine can check IDs later
type EvidenceRef = { kind: "log"; logId: string } | { kind: "network"; requestId: string };
interface EvidenceItem { id: string; ref: EvidenceRef; note: string; addedAt: number }

type HypothesisStatus = "open" | "supported" | "ruled-out";
interface Hypothesis  { id: string; text: string; status: HypothesisStatus; createdAt: number }
interface Observation { id: string; text: string; createdAt: number }
```

`WorkspaceScenario` gains `network: NetworkRequest[]`.

## Scenario data

The new file is `src/data/challenge-001/network.ts`. It has 14 requests, one for every `[http]` log line from the two login attempts:
- the login POST
- `dashboard.html` and `dashboard.js` (200 the first time, 304 the second)
- `/api/profile` and `/api/orders` (401)
- the `client-errors` beacon (204)
- `login.html`

Each request's time and status match its log line. Durations are a few ms longer than the server-logged time. Initiators point at real scenario files: the fetch calls come from `dashboard.js` and the login POST from `login.js`.

One realism point for you to approve: the network data shows the actual cookie headers.
- **Login response:** `Set-Cookie: sid=s%3A<uuid>.<sig>; Max-Age=28800; Path=/; HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Lax`
- **Later requests:** they send `Cookie: sid=s%3A…`

This is the key piece of evidence: it shows the browser *is* sending the cookie, which points learners at the server side. The `s:` prefix is the visible format of a signed cookie. It's real DevTools data and never names the cause, but it's the strongest clue in the scenario. If you'd rather make it harder, I can leave it out.

`logs.ts` and `files.ts` stay as they are. I'll verify cross-file consistency (log line ↔ request ↔ route) with a one-off script in the scratchpad, not in the repo. The server-only answer key also gets the evidence IDs (`req-04`, `log-06`…) so the engine can use them later. It stays server-only and imported by nothing.

## State management

- **`useInvestigationController`** (`useReducer`, owned by `WorkspaceClient`): holds `evidence`, `hypotheses` and `observations`. Actions: add/remove evidence, edit an evidence note, add/edit/remove a hypothesis, set its status, add/remove an observation.
  - It's shared by the Console and the Diagnosis panel.
  - It's the natural seam where the engine can later supply or receive this state.
- **View state** (filters, search text, selection, active console tab) lives in each view with `useState`. All three tab panels stay mounted and inactive ones are hidden, so switching tabs doesn't lose your filters or selection.
- **No persistence:** a reload clears notes, as with editor tabs. See question 2.

## Components

Everything lives under `src/components/workspace/investigation/` and replaces the current `InvestigationConsole.tsx`/`LogLine.tsx`.

| Component | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `InvestigationConsole` | Panel with real ARIA tabs: Logs · Network · Notes, with badge counts (evidence count on Notes) |
| `logs/LogsView` | Filter bar, then the list, then a details pane for the selected entry |
| `logs/LogFilterBar` | Toggle buttons per severity with counts, a search box, "n of m entries" |
| `logs/LogList`, `LogRow` | Selectable list with ↑/↓ keys; search matches highlighted; a dot marks collected entries |
| `logs/LogDetails` | Full ISO timestamp, level, source, message, parsed `key=value` fields, an **Add to evidence** button |
| `network/NetworkView` | Filter by text plus type (All / Fetch / Doc / JS), then the request list, then details |
| `network/RequestList` | Status (coloured), method, path, type, time; selectable with ↑/↓ |
| `network/RequestDetails` | Sub-tabs **Headers** (General, Response, Request), **Payload**, **Response**; an initiator link that **opens the file in Monaco**; **Add to evidence** |
| `network/HeaderTable` | Name/value list that wraps long cookie values |
| `notes/NotesView` | Three sections: Hypotheses, Observations, Evidence |
| `notes/HypothesisList` | Add, edit text, cycle status, remove |
| `notes/ObservationList` | Add and remove time-stamped notes |
| `notes/EvidenceList` | Collected items with a short summary, an optional note, remove, and "jump to source" (switches tab and selects the item) |
| `EvidenceToggle` | Shared Add/Collected ✓ button |
| `lib/text-highlight.tsx` | Splits text around search matches to wrap them in `<mark>` |

**Layout:** on large screens the console sits in the right-hand column, which is about 400px wide. There, the details pane stacks under the list (roughly 40/60) and can be closed. Medium and mobile keep the current stacking. `WorkspaceLayout`, the explorer, the editor and its tabs are unchanged, apart from adding an `openFile` callback for initiator links.

**Diagnosis panel:** the disabled "Supporting evidence" text box becomes a read-only list of collected evidence, showing the count and a short label for each item. The root-cause box and Submit button stay disabled, since submission belongs to the engine.

## Small fixes

- **Header label:** moves to a single constant (`src/config/app.ts` → `"Stage 3 · Preview"`), so it isn't hard-coded in the header again.
- **Challenge overview spacing:** most of its height on large screens comes from Difficulty, Status and Time being stacked vertically. I'll put them in one horizontal row, move the topic tags up onto the "Challenge 001" line, and tighten the padding a little. Text sizes stay the same.

**Unchanged rules:** no new dependencies; no scoring, database or code execution. When I'm done I'll run lint, `tsc` and the build, check that the answer key isn't in the bundle, and not commit.

## Questions

1. **Diagnosis panel:** OK to replace its evidence text box with the live evidence list?
2. **Persistence:** keep notes and evidence in memory only (recommended until the engine owns persistence), or save them per browser in `localStorage`?
3. **Cookie headers:** include the realistic `Set-Cookie`/`Cookie` headers described above, or leave them out for a harder challenge?

## Results

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `npm run lint` | Passed, 0 problems |
| `npx tsc --noEmit` | Passed, 0 errors |
| `npm run build` | Passed. `/` is still a static page |
| Scenario consistency check | **Passed.** All 14 requests and 23 log lines agree. Run from the scratchpad, not added to the repo |
| Answer-key leak check | **Clean.** No answer-key strings in the client JavaScript or the prerendered HTML. The only other mentions are comments in server-side data files |
| Raw HTML rendering | No `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` or `innerHTML` anywhere in `src/` |

The consistency script loads the real data modules and checks that:
- Each request matches exactly one `[http]` log line on time, method, path and status.
- Browser durations are never shorter than the server-logged ms.
- `Date` headers match the timestamps.
- Every `Content-Length` matches its body's byte count.
- The `dashboard.js` response body is the scenario file itself.
- The `Cookie` header on each request carries the session from the most recent `Set-Cookie`, including the old session being sent on the second login.
- Every 304 corresponds to an `If-None-Match` request.
- Initiators are real scenario files, and answer-key IDs exist.

## What's new

**Logs tab**
- Severity toggles show counts, and the search box highlights matches.
- Clear filters resets both. The list is selectable: ↑/↓, Home/End, Escape to clear the selection.
- The details pane shows the full timestamp and parsed `key=value` fields. HTTP lines have a "View in Network" link.

**Network tab**
- Filter by path or by type (Fetch, Doc, JS, Ping), with the same keyboard support as the logs list.
- The details pane has Headers, Payload and Response tabs. Headers covers the general info, response headers and request headers.
- Initiator links open the file in Monaco through the existing editor controller. Each request also links back to its server log line.
- Bodies are shown only as escaped text in `<pre>`, with JSON pretty-printed.

**Notes tab**
- Hypotheses with a status (Open / Supported / Ruled out), and time-stamped observations.
- Collected evidence, each with an optional note, a Show button that jumps back to the source, and Remove.
- A warning says notes and evidence are temporary.

**Evidence**
- IDs are fixed per source (`log:log-06`, `network:req-04`), and the reducer ignores repeat adds, so nothing can be collected twice.
- Collected rows get a green dot, and the Notes tab shows the count.
- The Diagnosis panel shows the live, read-only list with "Resets on refresh". Submission is still disabled.

**Other changes**
- **Header:** reads "Stage 3 · Preview", now set from `src/config/app.ts`.
- **Challenge overview:** more compact. Tags sit beside "Challenge 001", the incident text is one line, and the metadata is a single row.

**Files**
- **New:** `types/investigation.ts`, `data/challenge-001/network.ts`, `lib/evidence.ts`, `lib/text-highlight.tsx`, `config/app.ts`, `ui/TabList.tsx`, and `workspace/investigation/` (the controller, list keyboard handling, Logs, Network and Notes components).
- **Removed:** the old Stage 1 `InvestigationConsole.tsx` and `LogLine.tsx`.
- **Explorer, Monaco and editor tabs:** untouched, apart from initiator links reusing `openFile`.

## Things to know

- **I changed one log line.** `log-12` was `GET /login.html 200 2ms` and is now `304 1ms`. The user had already loaded the login page before signing in, so the browser would revalidate, not re-download.
- **The key clue is in the network data, as you approved.** The login response sets `sid=s%3A<uuid>.<sig>`, and the next requests send that same cookie and still get 401. All session values are fictional, generated with a throwaway secret.
- **The answer key now lists four stable evidence IDs** (`req-01`, `req-04`, `log-06`, `log-02`) for future engine matching. It's still server-only and imported by nothing.
- **Layout heights changed.**
  - On large screens the workspace's minimum height rose from 640px to 720px to fit the details panes. On short laptop screens the page will scroll.
  - On mobile the console's minimum height is now 480px.

## Limitations and open issues

- **Not tested in a browser.** Worth checking:
  - the console at about 400px wide on large screens, where the list sits above the details pane
  - keyboard focus when jumping between tabs
  - screen reader announcements
- **Nothing persists.** Reloading clears everything, and the UI says so.
- **Hypotheses can't be edited after adding.** You can only change their status or remove them.
- **The request-details tabs point at panels that aren't rendered.** Only the active panel is rendered, so the inactive Headers/Payload/Response tabs have `aria-controls` targets that don't exist. It's a minor ARIA imperfection; the console's main tabs are fine.
- **Monaco still loads from jsDelivr**, as in Stage 2.

When you commit, include `.claude-logs/` per `CLAUDE.md`.

Stage 3 is committed as `17d69cf`, including the tab accessibility fix, and the working tree is clean apart from the hook log. In this Next version, POST route handlers are never cached, and `params` arrives as a Promise that has to be awaited. Here's the plan.

## 1. Contract (shared, client-safe): `src/types/diagnosis.ts`

```ts
export interface RootCauseOption { id: string; label: string; description: string }

/** What the client sends. No correctness flag; extra fields are ignored. */
export interface DiagnosisSubmission {
  rootCauseId: string;
  explanation: string;        // trimmed, 40–2000 chars
  evidence: EvidenceRef[];    // Stage 3 references, 1–20 items, de-duplicated
}

export interface AttemptStatus { used: number; limit: number; remaining: number; resetsAt?: string }

export interface DiagnosisFeedback {
  headline: string;
  hints: string[];            // written on the server, never names the right option
  evidenceSummary: { total: number; logs: number; network: number };
}

export type DiagnosisResponse =
  | { status: "correct";      feedback: DiagnosisFeedback; resolution: { summary: string }; attempts: AttemptStatus }
  | { status: "incorrect";    feedback: DiagnosisFeedback; attempts: AttemptStatus }
  | { status: "invalid";      errors: { field: "rootCauseId" | "explanation" | "evidence" | "body"; message: string }[] }  // 400
  | { status: "rate-limited"; retryAfterSeconds: number; attempts: AttemptStatus }                                          // 429
  | { status: "error";        message: string };                                                                          // 404 / 413 / 415 / 500
```

`correct` means "your diagnosis matches", nothing more. There's no score and no claim that the challenge is complete. The UI will say completion tracking arrives with the Challenge Engine.

## 2. Data split

| Where | What | Who sees it |
|---|---|---|
| `data/challenge-001/diagnosis-options.ts` (new) | 5 plausible options with `id`, `label`, `description`; the correct one isn't listed first | Client |
| `answer-key.server.ts` (extended) | `correctRootCauseId`, hints for each wrong option, resolution summary, the existing `keyEvidenceRefs` | Server only |

Draft options (IDs are descriptive slugs, so the ID alone doesn't give the answer away):

1. The browser doesn't send the session cookie (Secure/SameSite)
2. Sessions expire too early (TTL or unit mismatch)
3. **The middleware reads the session ID from unsigned cookies, but login issues a signed cookie** ← correct
4. The in-memory session store loses sessions between requests
5. The dashboard's fetch calls leave out credentials

Each wrong option has a hint that disproves it without pointing at the answer. For example, option 1: *"Compare the request headers on the failing calls. Is the cookie actually missing?"*

I left out "cookie secret mismatch" on purpose. It produces the same symptoms as the real cause, and only environment details we don't show could tell them apart, so learners couldn't fairly rule it out.

## 3. Server side (all `import "server-only"`)

```
src/app/api/challenges/[challengeId]/diagnosis/route.ts   POST, uses ctx.params (awaited)
src/server/diagnosis/registry.ts                          challengeId → { answer key, valid evidence IDs }
src/server/diagnosis/evaluate.ts                          pure: (submission, key) → result
src/server/diagnosis/attempt-limiter.ts                   in-memory limiter, injectable clock
src/lib/diagnosis/validate.ts                             pure, shared with the client (no secrets)
```

**How a request is handled:**
1. **Request checks.** The method must be POST. `Content-Type` must be `application/json`, otherwise 415. That requirement also forces a CORS preflight for cross-site requests. If an `Origin` header is present it must match the host, otherwise 403. The body is read as text and capped at 16 KB, otherwise 413.
2. **Challenge ID.** It must be in the registry, otherwise 404 with a generic message.
3. **Parse and validate.** `JSON.parse` sits inside a try/catch, and everything is validated with the shared validator:
   - field types are checked;
   - `rootCauseId` must be one of the challenge's option IDs;
   - control characters are stripped from the explanation and its length checked;
   - evidence refs must have the right shape, exist in the scenario, and are de-duplicated.

   Failures return 400 with per-field messages that never mention correctness.
4. **Attempt limit.** Over the limit returns 429 with `Retry-After`.
5. **Evaluate.** Compare `rootCauseId` with the key on the server, then build the feedback.
   - **Wrong answer:** that option's hint, plus general evidence advice, for example "none of your evidence comes from the Network tab". It never says which evidence is key.
   - **Right answer:** the resolution summary, plus how many key observations your evidence covered.
6. **Respond** with `Cache-Control: no-store`. The explanation text is never echoed back or logged.

Only a correct answer reveals anything from the key, and only that answer's own resolution summary. The correct option ID never appears in any response.

## 4. Attempt limiting

- **Anonymous attempt cookie.** `buglab_attempt` is a random ID set on the first submission (`HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict; Path=/api`). It allows **3 wrong answers per 10 minutes per challenge**. When the window expires, the counter resets.
- **Per-IP throttle**, so clearing the cookie doesn't reset everything. It allows **10 submissions per 10 minutes per client address**, taken from the first `X-Forwarded-For` hop and otherwise a shared "unknown" bucket.
- **A correct answer is final.** Once a session gets it right, later submissions return the same result and don't count.

Honest limits:
- **Memory only.** Counts live in the server's memory, so they reset on restart and aren't shared between instances or serverless functions. A database or engine-side store would replace this later.
- **The IP can be faked** unless the app sits behind a proxy that sets it reliably.
- **Guessing is slowed, not prevented.** With 5 options, someone determined can still guess in about 2 windows. Making the evidence count toward correctness would make guessing harder. Question 2 covers that.

## 5. Client side

- **`lib/diagnosis/client.ts`**: `submitDiagnosis(challengeId, submission, signal)`. It has a 15-second timeout and turns network errors or bad responses into `{ status: "error" }`.
- **`useDiagnosisSubmission`**, a client state machine separate from both the investigation controller and the engine:
  - **States:** `idle → submitting → correct | incorrect | rate-limited | error`.
  - **Form state:** the chosen option and explanation draft, kept in memory.
  - **Retry:** after an error it resends the same payload. After a wrong answer the form stays filled in for revision.
  - **No double submits:** they're blocked, and an in-flight request is cancelled if the component unmounts.
- **`DiagnosisPanel`**, rewritten as a form with small parts:
  - **`RootCauseOptions`:** native radio buttons in a `fieldset` with a `legend`.
  - **`ExplanationField`:** a textarea with a character counter, `aria-invalid`, and `aria-describedby` linking it to its error.
  - **`EvidenceReview`:** the current live list with a required minimum of 1, plus a "Manage in Notes" button that switches the console to the Notes tab.
  - **`DiagnosisResult`:** an `aria-live` region with the success, wrong-answer, rate-limit and error views. The rate-limit view counts down until you can retry. Each view has a retry or "Revise" action.
  - **On submit:** client-side checks run first using the shared validator, and focus moves to the first invalid field. The server checks everything again regardless.
  - **After a correct answer:** the form becomes read-only.
- **Layout:**
  - On large screens the right column's console/diagnosis split changes from 3:2 to **1:1**, because the form needs more room. Its body already scrolls.
  - On mobile and tablet, panels just stack as they do now.
  - The Monaco editor, Explorer, Logs, Network and Notes don't change. The console gains a small way for the Diagnosis panel to switch it to the Notes tab.

## 6. Tests (no new dependencies)

I'd use Node 24's built-in `node:test` runner with its built-in TypeScript type stripping. One small loader file, `tests/setup/register.mjs`, maps `@/` to `src/`, resolves imports that omit the `.ts` extension, and stubs out `server-only`. `package.json` gains `"test": "node --test --import ./tests/setup/register.mjs tests/**/*.test.ts"`.

| File | Covers |
|---|---|
| `validate.test.ts` | missing or wrong-type fields, explanation length and control characters, unknown option, bad, unknown or duplicate evidence refs, too many refs, extra fields ignored |
| `evaluate.test.ts` | correct → `correct` with resolution; every wrong option → `incorrect` with its hint; **no wrong-answer response contains the correct ID or its label** |
| `attempt-limiter.test.ts` | counting, lockout after 3, window reset (fake clock), correct is final, the IP bucket |
| `route.test.ts` | calls `POST` directly with `new Request(...)`, covering: 415, 403 (wrong Origin), 413, bad JSON → 400, unknown challenge → 404, invalid body → 400, wrong → 200, correct → 200, fourth wrong → 429 with `Retry-After`, a forged `correct: true` field is ignored, `no-store` header set, and no response before success contains the correct ID |

After the build I'll also search the client JavaScript for answer-key strings, `correctRootCauseId` and the hint text, then run lint, `tsc` and the build. No commit.

## Boundaries

- **Engine integration point:** `src/server/diagnosis/registry.ts` plus the `DiagnosisResponse` contract. Haboon's engine can later replace the in-memory answer key and limiter, or call `evaluate()` itself. The UI only depends on `submitDiagnosis()` and the response types.
- **Haboon's branch:** I won't touch it.

## Questions

1. **Limits:** OK with 3 wrong answers per 10 minutes per attempt cookie, plus 10 submissions per 10 minutes per IP, both in memory?
2. **Evidence:** require at least one evidence item to submit (recommended)? And should a correct answer with *no* key evidence still count as correct, or come back as a "needs supporting evidence" result? The second makes guessing harder, but it hints that the chosen root cause is right.
3. **Test runner:** is the built-in `node:test` with a small loader OK, or would you rather add Vitest as a dev dependency?

## Results

| Check | Result |

| `npm test` | 45 passed, 0 failed |
| `npm run lint` | 0 problems |

| Live check (`next start` + curl) | Three wrong answers, then a 429 with `Retry-After: 600`. The correct answer is blocked the same way while locked. Without the cookie, correct returns 200. 415, 403, 404 and 405 all work |

The tests run on Node's built-in runner. The only setup is one ~40-line resolve hook (`tests/setup/register.mjs`) that maps `@/`, resolves imports without file extensions, and stubs `server-only`. No new dependencies.

## Changed files

- **Contract and data:**
  - `types/diagnosis.ts` defines the request and response types.
  - `data/challenge-001/diagnosis-options.ts` holds 5 options in a fixed order, with nothing marking the correct one.
  - `answer-key.server.ts` gains the correct option ID and the resolution summary.
  - `index.ts` and `types/workspace.ts` are wired up.
- **Shared, client-safe:**
  - `lib/diagnosis/validate.ts` checks input shape and membership only, never correctness.
  - `lib/diagnosis/client.ts` makes the request, with a timeout, and never throws.
- **Server only, each starting with `import "server-only"`:**
  - `server/diagnosis/registry.ts` maps challenges to answer keys. This is where the engine plugs in.
  - `evaluate.ts` produces generic feedback for wrong answers: the same for every wrong option, and based only on the learner's own evidence mix.
  - `attempt-limiter.ts` counts attempts in fixed windows.
  - `client-ip.ts` handles the IP limit, applied only when a trusted proxy is configured.
  - `handler.ts` processes each request.
  - `app/api/challenges/[challengeId]/diagnosis/route.ts` exposes it.
- **UI:**
  - `DiagnosisPanel.tsx` is rewritten as a working form.
  - `workspace/diagnosis/` holds the form parts: root-cause choices, explanation field, evidence review, result display, the submission hook and a countdown.
  - The console tab is now controlled by its parent, so "Manage in Notes" can switch to it.
  - The right column is split 1:1 on large screens.
- **Tests:** `tests/diagnosis/` covers validation, evaluation, the limiter and IP handling, the HTTP handler, and confidentiality. `package.json` gains `"test"`.

## Security limitations

- **The limits are best-effort, not production-grade.** Counts live in the server's memory, so they reset on restart and aren't shared between instances or serverless functions.
- **Clearing the cookie gets around the limit when no proxy is trusted.** That's the default. Set `BUGLAB_TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS=N` only when exactly N trusted proxies append to `X-Forwarded-For`. That enables the 10-per-10-minutes IP limit, and spoofed leftmost entries are ignored.
- **With 5 options, determined guessing is slowed, not prevented.**
- **The correct option's ID is public, as one of five choices.** Nothing marks it as correct. The answer only appears after a correct submission, and only as the resolution summary.
- **Basic CSRF protection only.** Cross-site requests are blocked by requiring a JSON content type (which forces a browser preflight) and checking the `Origin` header. There are no CSRF tokens.

## Browser testing steps

1. **Run it:** `npm run dev`, then open `/`.
2. **Required fields:** Submit with nothing filled in. Errors should appear inline and focus should move to the first root-cause option. "Manage in Notes" should switch the console to the Notes tab.
3. **Wrong answer:** Collect evidence (for example `req-04`), pick a wrong option, and write at least 40 characters. You should see generic feedback, "2 of 3 incorrect attempts left", and a Revise button.
4. **Rate limit:** Submit two more wrong answers, then try the correct one. All three should be blocked with a countdown, and the Continue button should stay disabled until it reaches zero.
5. **Correct answer:** In a private window, or after the 10-minute window passes, submit the correct option. You should see "Diagnosis confirmed" with the resolution, the form should become read-only, and there should be no score.
6. **Network failure:** Stop the dev server and submit. You should get the error view, and Retry should resend the same answer.
7. **Regression check:** Make sure Monaco, the Explorer, Logs, Network and Notes still work, and check the layout at mobile and tablet widths.

When you commit, include `.claude-logs/` per `CLAUDE.md`.

I have everything I need. Nothing was modified: I fetched the refs, read Haboon's branch through `git show`, and simulated the merge with `git merge-tree`, which only writes temporary objects. The working tree still shows only the hook-managed log.

# Stage 5 integration audit: workspace ↔ Challenge Engine

The integration is very doable. There's one real merge conflict, about the test runner. But Haboon's committed session log shows plans that overlap with ours in three places:
- He's designing his own server-side diagnosis path.
- His reducer can only mark a challenge solved through Fix Mode.
- He's still waiting for the Broken User Sessions spec before building any Challenge 001 content.

So most of this audit is about decisions to agree, not code.

## What's on each branch

| | `feature/debugging-workspace` (ours) | `feature/challenge-engine` (Haboon) |
|---|---|---|
| Base | `333dab2`, plus Stages 1–4 | `333dab2`, plus `b7588e5` (Stage 2a) |
| Contents | Workspace UI, Challenge 001 scenario, diagnosis API, 45 `node:test` tests | `src/engine/{types,session/reducer,session/scoring,test-fixtures}.ts`, 27 vitest tests |
| Dependencies added | `@monaco-editor/react`, `monaco-editor` | `vitest@^3.2.7` (dev) |
| Overlapping files | `package.json`, `package-lock.json` | same |

**Simulated merge result:**
- `package.json` conflicts only on the `"test"` script: ours is `node --import … --test`, his is `vitest run`.
- `package-lock.json` has 2 conflict hunks and needs regenerating.
- Everything else merges cleanly. `src/engine/**` doesn't collide with anything of ours.

**A hidden problem behind the conflict:** vitest's default file pattern would also pick up our `tests/diagnosis/*.test.ts`. Those use `node:test` and an `@/` alias, and import `server-only`, so `vitest run` would fail on them. Just picking either side of the conflict isn't enough.

**From Haboon's log, not yet code:**
- **Planned modules:** `engine/server` (a registry, `'use server'` actions `checkDiagnosis`, `revealHint`, `revealExplanation`), a `useChallengeSession()` client hook, and a Web Worker runner.
- **Planned storage:** `challenges/<id>/secret.json`, saved progress in localStorage, and JavaScript-only support.
- **Challenge 001:** his "Shared Defaults" idea is parked. Broken User Sessions is the first challenge, but he says he hasn't seen its brief.
- **Vitest:** he recommends moving to vitest `^4.1.12`, because 3.2.7 brings back 3 dev-only audit findings.

## 1. Already compatible

- **The reducer and scoring are pure and UI-agnostic.** They can be used as they are.
- **Answer-key confidentiality.** His `ChallengePublic` deliberately has no answer keys, and his plan keeps diagnosis answers on the server. That matches our server-only answer key and route.
- **Root-cause options.** His `causeOptions: {id, label}[]` is a subset of our `RootCauseOption`, which adds a `description`.
- **Trust model.** Scores are advisory under his D2. Our Stage 4 already treats the server as the authority on correctness.
- **No file overlap in source code.** We own `src/app`, `src/components`, `src/server/diagnosis`. He owns `src/engine` and the planned `challenges/`.

## 2. Types that need adapters

| Ours | Engine | Adapter or decision |
|---|---|---|
| `ChallengeSummary` (workspace.ts) | `ChallengeSummary` (engine) | **Same name, different shapes.** Ours becomes a UI view built from `ChallengePublic` |
| `difficulty: "intermediate"` | `difficulty: 1–5` | Mapping: 1–2 → beginner, 3 → intermediate, 4–5 → advanced. Challenge 001 = 3 |
| `incident` / `estimatedMinutes` | `symptom` / `estMinutes` (+ `summary`) | Field renames |
| `id: "challenge-001"`, `number: "001"` | ids like `"000-fixture"` | **Agree an ID**, e.g. `001-broken-user-sessions`. It affects our API route, answer key and tests |
| `ScenarioFile {path, content}` | `WorkspaceFile {path, contents, readOnly}` | Simple mapping. For a diagnosis-only challenge every file is read-only, so `createSession` creates no editable files, which is correct |
| TypeScript scenario files | `language: "javascript"` (fixed) | **Conflict**: Challenge 001 is TypeScript and Express. The field needs widening or making optional while there's no runner |
| `RootCauseOption.description` | no description field | Either Haboon adds an optional `description`, or we keep descriptions in workspace data keyed by option ID |
| `logs`, `network`, `incidentDate`, `entryFilePath` | not modelled | Keep them as a workspace-owned investigation pack keyed by the engine challenge ID (recommended), or add `ChallengePublic.investigation?` |
| `InvestigationStatus` | `SessionStatus` + `diagnosed` | Mapping: `in_progress` → in progress, `diagnosed` → diagnosis confirmed, `solved` → resolved, `revealed` → revealed |
| `DiagnosisSubmission {rootCauseId, explanation, evidence}` | planned `diagnose(causeId)` | **Contract change**: the engine's diagnose call has to carry the explanation and evidence |
| Our `LogEntry` (incident logs) | `ConsoleLine` (runner output) | Different concepts. Don't merge them |

## 3. How a correct diagnosis updates `SessionState`

The server decides correctness. The client only turns the server's verdict into an engine event:

| Server response | Engine event |
|---|---|
| `correct` | `DIAGNOSIS_CHECKED { correct: true }` |
| `incorrect` | `DIAGNOSIS_CHECKED { correct: false }` |
| `invalid`, `rate-limited`, `error` | none |

That mapping lives in one small pure function, `verdictToEvent()`, which gets its own tests. Nothing else dispatches diagnosis events.

The reducer already ignores diagnosis events once `diagnosed` is true, and our form locks after a correct answer, so the two agree.

**Gap:** the reducer only sets `status: "solved"` when all tests pass (`passedAll`). A correct diagnosis never solves a challenge. See section 7.

## 4. Keeping diagnosis attempts in sync

There are two counters, and that's on purpose:
- **The server limiter** (3 wrong answers per 10 minutes, per attempt cookie) protects against guessing.
- **The engine's `wrongDiagnoses`** drives the score penalty.

Both go up on the same `incorrect` response, so they match during normal use. They can drift apart:
- **The engine resets** when localStorage is cleared. That's acceptable, since scores are advisory under D2.
- **The server window expires** after 10 minutes. Only the lockout resets; the engine keeps its count.

The server stays the authority for lockout. The engine is the record for scoring.

**Known weakness:** a learner can dispatch a fake correct answer from DevTools, which follows from D2's advisory scores. If scores ever need to be trusted, the server could return a signed verdict token, or the session could live on the server. That's a later decision.

**Reload problem:** if the session is saved as `diagnosed: true`, after a reload we no longer have the resolution summary, because it only came in the response. Options:
- store the summary with the session (it's the learner's own earned content);
- let a server-side "reveal explanation" call check for proof of a correct diagnosis;
- show "Diagnosis confirmed" without the summary.

This needs agreeing.

## 5. Representing evidence

- **Keep the current format:** `EvidenceRef` (`{kind:"log", logId}` / `{kind:"network", requestId}`), with ID strings like `log:log-06`.
- **Keep evidence out of `SessionState` for now.** Scoring doesn't use it, and adding it would mean a version-2 session format.
- **For saving progress later:** either a separate workspace investigation store next to his `SessionStore`, or an optional `investigation` field in a future session version. That's Haboon's call, because his scoring and saving code would have to handle it.
- **The server keeps checking every evidence ref** against the scenario's IDs, as it does now.

## 6. Scoring without duplicating engine logic

- **The UI never calculates points.** It calls `computeScore(state, challenge.scoring)`, or uses the `score` from his hook once it exists, and shows it labelled as advisory.
- **Our server evaluator returns no score.** That's already true. The "1 of 4 key observations" line is feedback, not points, and can stay.
- **Scoring rules are data on `ChallengePublic.scoring`.** What Challenge 001's rules should be depends on section 7.

## 7. Diagnosis-only completion or Fix Mode for Challenge 001?

I recommend diagnosis-only completion for now, with Fix Mode as a later phase.

- **Why not Fix Mode yet:** his planned runner is a Web Worker running plain JavaScript with no Node APIs. Challenge 001 is Express in TypeScript, with cookie-parser and http. It *can* get Fix Mode later: the bug is in `authenticate(req)`, which a JavaScript port can unit-test with fake `req` objects that mimic cookie-parser. That needs a port, visible and hidden tests, and the runner, none of which exist yet.
- **How to support diagnosis-only completion**, which is a decision for Haboon:
  - **(a, recommended)** Add `completion: "diagnosis" | "fix"` to `ChallengePublic`, and have the reducer set `solved` on a correct diagnosis when the mode is `"diagnosis"`. That's a small change, it's his to make, and it gets a reducer test.
  - **(b, no engine change)** Set Challenge 001's scoring to 0 for both fix stages. The UI then treats "diagnosed and no fix stage" as complete. The status stays `in_progress`, which misleads saved progress and any future dashboards.
- **Scoring with option (a):** diagnosis 30 points, minus 5 per wrong answer, and 0 for the fix stages. Hints only if hint text gets written. The maximum is 30 until Fix Mode, or 100 if Fix Mode points are kept and shown as "coming soon". Product decision.

## 8. Keeping the answer key confidential

- **One answer key.** It moves to Haboon's planned format (`challenges/001-…/secret.json`) and is read only through `engine/server`. Our `src/server/diagnosis/registry.ts` becomes a thin adapter over his registry, or goes away.
- **JSON can't declare itself server-only.** A JSON file can't contain `import "server-only"`. So `secret.json` must only be read through a server-only module. Our confidentiality test gets extended to flag any import of `challenges/**/secret*` from outside `src/server/**`, `src/engine/server/**` or `app/api/**`.
- **Server Functions are public endpoints too.** If his `checkDiagnosis` becomes a `'use server'` action, it's just as reachable from the outside as our route. It needs the same input validation and the same attempt limiting, and must return only a verdict and feedback. Better still, have a single entry point: either our route calls his evaluation code, or his action reuses our handler. Not two.
- **Bundle checks:** keep checking the built client JavaScript for answer-key data, for example as `scripts/check-client-bundle.mjs` run after the build.

## 9. Files that would change

**Ours:**
- `package.json` and `package-lock.json` (agreed jointly)
- `vitest.config.ts` (new); `tests/setup/register.mjs` deleted
- `tests/diagnosis/*.test.ts` (ported to vitest)
- `src/lib/engine-adapter.ts` (new) and its tests
- `src/types/workspace.ts` (view types, renamed `ChallengeSummary`)
- `src/data/challenge-001/*` (ID, scoring rules, investigation pack; secrets move later)
- `src/app/page.tsx` (builds `ChallengePublic` on the server)
- `WorkspaceClient.tsx` (engine session), `DiagnosisPanel.tsx` (dispatch and score), `ChallengeOverview.tsx` (status from the session)
- `src/server/diagnosis/{registry,evaluate}.ts` (hand off to the engine)

**Haboon's, which he changes himself:**

chars: 5426

The zero is correct for the request you collected, and there's no submission bug. The cause is the answer key: it lists one specific request, while the scenario contains two that look the same.

## What happened

The incident has two login attempts, and each one produces a `GET /api/profile → 401`:

| ID | Request | Time | In `keyEvidenceRefs`? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `req-04` | GET /api/profile 401 (1st attempt) | 09:14:02.487 | ✅ yes |
| `req-11` | GET /api/profile 401 (2nd attempt) | 09:14:10.105 | ❌ no |

The answer key lists exactly four IDs: `network:req-01`, `network:req-04`, `log:log-06`, `log:log-02`. The evaluator counts an exact ID match only. I ran the real code path for each request: the investigation reducer, then `item.ref`, then the JSON body, then server-side validation, then `evaluateDiagnosis`:

```
req-04: sent=[{"kind":"network","requestId":"req-04"}] -> correct: "Your evidence included 1 of 4 key observations."
req-11: sent=[{"kind":"network","requestId":"req-11"}] -> correct: "Your evidence included 0 of 4 key observations."
```

The live curl test in Stage 4 also sent `req-04` and got "1 of 4". So you almost certainly collected `req-11`. That's easy to do: the Network list doesn't show timestamps, so the two rows look identical apart from 8 ms vs 7 ms.

## Is the submission path correct?

Yes:
- `EvidenceItem.ref` stores `{ kind: "network", requestId }`.
- `WorkspaceClient` sends `investigation.evidence.map(item => item.ref)`.
- The validator keeps `kind` and the ID and drops anything else.
- Both the evaluator and the registry build IDs with the same `getEvidenceId()`. The registry ignores the extra `why` field, so `"network:req-04"` matches as it should.

There's no ID mismatch and nothing goes wrong in serialization.

## So it's a modelling problem, not a code bug

Each key observation is tied to one instance, even though the same observation happens on both attempts. `req-11` proves exactly what `req-04` proves: the browser sent the session cookie and still got a 401. The same goes for the Unauthorized log line `log-17` vs `log-06`, the successful login `log-13` vs `log-02`, and the second login's `Set-Cookie` in `req-08` vs `req-01`. Telling a learner they found "0 of 4" when they found a valid one is misleading.

## Smallest safe correction (recommended)

Turn each key observation into a group of equivalent references. A group counts as covered if any one of its members was collected. Scoring rules don't change. This only affects the feedback line, which appears only after a correct diagnosis.

- **`answer-key.server.ts`:** replace the flat `keyEvidenceRefs` with four groups:

  | Observation | Equivalent refs |
  |---|---|
  | Login issues the cookie in signed `s:` format | `req-01`, `req-08` |
  | Browser sends the session cookie and still gets 401 | `req-04`, `req-11` (*optionally the `/api/orders` ones too: `req-05`, `req-12`*) |
  | The authenticate middleware rejects the request | `log-06`, `log-17` (*optionally `log-08`, `log-19`*) |
  | Login succeeded moments earlier | `log-02`, `log-13` |

- **`registry.ts`:** expose these as `keyObservations: ReadonlySet<string>[]`, not one flat set.
- **`evaluate.ts`:** count the groups covered. That's about three changed lines, and the total stays 4. Collecting `req-04` and `req-11` together still counts once.
- **Wording, optionally clearer:** "Your evidence covered 1 of the 4 key observations for this incident."

One decision for you: should the `/api/orders` 401s count as the same observation as the `/api/profile` ones? They show the identical symptom, so I'd include them. I've kept them optional because the original key only named the profile request.

If you'd rather leave the data as it is and only change the wording, something like "matched 0 of 4 highlighted evidence items" would be accurate. But it still tells a learner with good evidence that they missed everything, so I don't recommend it.

## Test coverage gaps

The existing tests don't cover this path:
- **Every handler test sends `req-04`**, the default fixture, so the passing case is the only one tested.
- **The one zero-count test** (`evaluate.test.ts`) uses `log-01`, the server-startup line, which really is irrelevant. Nothing checks that an equivalent instance like `req-11` counts.
- **Nothing tests the client side.** `investigationReducer` has no tests, and nothing goes from collecting evidence to the diagnosis feedback.

Tests I'd add with the fix:
1. **`evaluate.test.ts`:** each group member counts on its own; `req-04` plus `req-11` counts as 1; unrelated evidence such as `log-01` or `req-02` counts as 0.
2. **Answer-key integrity:** every grouped ID exists in the scenario's logs or network data, and no ID appears in two groups.
3. **Full path:** collect `req-11` through the investigation reducer, send the resulting refs through the handler with the correct root cause, and expect "1 of 4". The scratch script above is that test in rough form.
4. **Reducer:** adding the same ref twice keeps a single evidence item.

I haven't modified any files. The repro script is outside the repo, at `C:\Users\LAPTOP~1\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\C--Users-Laptop-Zone-by-JK-buglab\6a0a38fb-42bd-4091-8443-e500d4f86a7d\scratchpad\evidence-repro.ts`. Nothing is merged, committed or pushed, and Haboon's branch is untouched. Tell me whether to include the `/api/orders` equivalents and I'll make the change.

The first build failed on a type error in my new test file. The helper functions returned narrow literal types; I fixed them to return `string[]`, and everything passed after that.

To check the new tests actually catch the problem, I briefly changed the evaluator so a group needed all its items instead of any one, which is close to the old exact-ID behaviour. 5 tests failed. With the evaluator restored, all 59 pass.

## Changed files

- **`src/data/challenge-001/answer-key.server.ts`** (server-only): the flat `keyEvidenceRefs` is replaced by four `keyObservations` groups.

  | Observation | Equivalent evidence |
  |---|---|
  | `signed-cookie-issued` | `req-01`, `req-08` |
  | `cookie-sent-still-401` | `req-04`, `req-05`, `req-11`, `req-12` |
  | `auth-rejected` | `log-06`, `log-08`, `log-17`, `log-19` |
  | `login-succeeded` | `log-02`, `log-13` |

- **`src/server/diagnosis/registry.ts`:** a new `buildKeyObservations()` builds the groups. The server refuses to start if a grouped ID doesn't exist in the scenario or appears in two groups.
- **`src/server/diagnosis/evaluate.ts`:** a group counts once if any of its items was collected, so the total is always 4. The new wording is in place. Correctness, scoring, attempt limits and the API contract haven't changed.
- **`tests/diagnosis/key-observations.test.ts`** (new, 14 tests):
  - **Integrity:** exactly four groups, every ID exists, no ID is in two groups, and the registry rejects unknown or duplicated IDs.
  - **Same failure:** checks the data itself. Each `req-04/05/11/12` is a protected route that sends the signed `sid` cookie and gets a 401 Unauthorized. Each one's rejection log line is in `auth-rejected`. Both login responses set the signed cookie.
  - **Equivalent IDs:** each of the 12 grouped items counts as 1 of 4 on its own.
  - **Duplicates:** `req-04` + `req-11`, all four 401 requests, or three rejection logs each count once. One item from each group gives 4 of 4.
  - **Unrelated evidence:** the startup log, static assets, the error beacon, the login page and the access-log lines give 0 of 4, and don't raise the count when mixed with key evidence.
  - **The full `req-11` path:** collected through the investigation reducer, with a duplicate add kept as a single item, serialized the way the UI does it, then sent through the HTTP handler with the correct root cause. It returns "covered 1 of 4".
- **`tests/diagnosis/evaluate.test.ts`:** one assertion now expects the exact new wording.
- **`tests/diagnosis/confidentiality.test.ts`:** the "server-only names never appear in client code" check also looks for `keyObservation`.

When you commit, include `.claude-logs/` per `CLAUDE.md`.

I've checked the current code, worked out how each layout behaves at common laptop sizes, and measured text contrast. Stages 1–4 and the evidence fix are committed (`b1eb3af`), and the working tree is clean apart from the hook log. Here's the plan.

## What I found

**Layout at laptop sizes.** On large screens the workspace grid is `15rem | editor | minmax(20rem,26rem)`, at least 720px tall. Console and Diagnosis split the right column 1:1.

| Viewport | Editor width | Console / Diagnosis panel height | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1024×768 | about 390px | about 354px each | The editor is cramped, and the right column is only 320px wide |
| 1280×800 / 1366×768 | 560–640px | about 354px each | **Diagnosis needs about 600px of content** (5 options, explanation, evidence, result) and shows roughly half. The Network details pane leaves the request list about 96px tall |
| 1440×900+ | 700px+ | 400–430px each | Fine, though Diagnosis still scrolls |

**Contrast.** The `subtle` text colour (`#5b6576`) is used 56 times, mostly for text 10–12px in size. It measures **2.96–3.28:1**, which fails the WCAG AA requirement of 4.5:1. `muted` is fine at 5.7–6.3. Monaco's line-number colour is 2.0:1. Changing `subtle` to `#7d8799` gives **4.82–5.33:1** on every surface and keeps it visibly dimmer than `muted`.

**Focus gets lost** in a few places. When a button removes its own container, focus falls back to `<body>`:
- the **×** that closes log or request details;
- **Remove** on evidence, hypotheses and observations.

**Long names:**
- Editor tabs have no maximum width, so long names stretch the tab strip.
- A tab opened from the Explorer or an initiator link can land off-screen in the scrolling tab strip.
- Single-line truncation cuts off the end, which hides the most useful part in a few places: the request-details title, the Network path column (no tooltip), Diagnosis evidence items, and the editor's footer path, where the filename itself gets cut.

**Header:** it still says "Stage 3 · Preview", from `src/config/app.ts`.

## Prioritised plan

### P1: Demo blockers

1. **A layout for each screen size** (`WorkspaceLayout.tsx`):
   - **1280px and up:** three columns as now. The right column widens a little to `minmax(22rem,28rem)`.
   - **1024–1279px:** two rows. Explorer (13rem) and Editor on top; Console and Diagnosis side by side below, each about 480px wide. That fixes the cramped editor and the narrow Network details.
   - **Tablet and mobile:** unchanged.
2. **Expand/restore buttons on the Console and Diagnosis panels** in three-column mode. Expanding one gives it most of the right column; the other collapses to its title bar, which still shows a short summary such as "Diagnosis · 2 evidence". The default stays at 1:1. A plain button with `aria-expanded`, in `Panel.tsx`, `WorkspaceLayout.tsx` and `WorkspaceClient.tsx`. It's a new control, but it doesn't change any behaviour.
3. **More room inside the console** (`LogsView.tsx`, `NetworkView.tsx`). The request list gets a minimum of about 7rem, and the details pane can take at most about 50%.
4. **Header label**: change `APP_STAGE_LABEL`. Wording is question 1 below.

### P2: Accessibility and keyboard

5. **Contrast:** change the `--subtle` colour token to `#7d8799` in `globals.css`. Raise Monaco's line-number colour to about 3:1 in `monaco-setup.ts`. One token edit, applied everywhere.
6. **Keep focus in place:**
   - Closing details returns focus to the selected row (`LogsView`, `NetworkView`).
   - Removing a hypothesis, observation or evidence item moves focus to the next item, or to the section's add field if the list becomes empty (`HypothesisList`, `ObservationList`, `EvidenceList`).
7. **Leaving Monaco with Tab:** I'll check in the browser whether the read-only editor traps Tab. If it does, I'll turn on Monaco's `tabFocusMode` option (available in 0.57) so Tab moves on to the next control (`MonacoEditor.tsx`).
8. **Skip links** at the top of the page: "Skip to workspace" and "Skip to diagnosis". They're hidden until they receive focus (`AppHeader.tsx` or `page.tsx`).

### P3: Long names and overflow

9. **Editor tabs** (`EditorTabs.tsx`):
   - Tab names are capped at about 12rem and truncated, with the full path still in the tooltip.
   - When two open files share a name, the parent folder shows in muted text.
   - The active tab scrolls into view when it changes.
10. **Network list** (`RequestRow.tsx`): the path gets a `title` with the full path.
11. **Request and log details title** (`DetailHeader.tsx`): wraps (`break-all`) instead of truncating.
12. **Diagnosis evidence items** (`EvidenceReview.tsx`): show up to 2 lines (`line-clamp-2`) instead of 1, keeping the full text in the tooltip.
13. **Editor footer path** (`EditorPanel.tsx`): the filename always stays visible and only the folder part is truncated, for example `…/middleware/authenticate.ts`.

### P4: Optional

14. **Challenge overview status:** it says "Investigating" even after a confirmed diagnosis. Fixing that means moving the status badge into a small client component that reads the diagnosis state. That's only UI state, with no API changes, but it's new behaviour, so it's your call.

## Files that would change

`src/config/app.ts`, `src/app/globals.css`, `src/app/page.tsx` or `src/components/layout/AppHeader.tsx`, `src/components/ui/Panel.tsx`, `src/components/workspace/{WorkspaceLayout,WorkspaceClient,EditorTabs,EditorPanel,MonacoEditor,DiagnosisPanel}.tsx`, `src/components/workspace/diagnosis/EvidenceReview.tsx`, `src/components/workspace/investigation/{DetailHeader,logs/LogsView,network/NetworkView,network/RequestRow,notes/HypothesisList,notes/ObservationList,notes/EvidenceList}.tsx`, `src/lib/monaco-setup.ts`. Item 14 would also touch `ChallengeOverview.tsx`.

**Unchanged:** the diagnosis API and its validation and evaluation, scoring, challenge data, the answer key, engine contracts, dependencies, and Haboon's branch.

**Checks when I'm done:**
- `npm test` (59), lint, `tsc`, the build, and the client bundle scan again.
- A browser pass at 1024×768, 1280×800, 1366×768, 1440×900, 1920×1080, 768px and 390px wide.
- A keyboard-only walkthrough: Explorer → tabs → Monaco → console tabs → lists → details → evidence → diagnosis → submit.

## Questions

1. **Header label:** "Stage 4 · Preview", which is accurate now, or something that won't go stale, such as "Demo preview"?
2. **Expand/restore buttons (item 2):** OK to add them, or do you prefer only the layout change?
3. **The `subtle` contrast fix (item 5):** OK to change the token everywhere? It brightens all dim text slightly.

| 768–1023 (tablet) | Explorer and Editor full width; Console and Diagnosis side by side below |
| **1024–1279 (laptop), new** | **Two rows**: Explorer (13rem) and Editor on top (`clamp(420px, 100dvh − 15rem, 640px)`); Console and Diagnosis side by side below |
| ≥ 1280 (desktop) | Three columns, as before but with a wider right column (`minmax(22rem, 28rem)`) and a minimum height of 640px, down from 720 now that panels can expand |

**No scrolling inside the form at tablet and laptop sizes.** At those widths the Console fills its cell, so the bottom row takes the Diagnosis form's natural height. The form never gets its own scrollbar, and the Console's lists scroll within that same height. Every column uses `minmax(0, 1fr)`, so long content can't push the page wider than the screen.

**Expand/restore (≥1280 only):**
- **The button:** an icon button in the Console and Diagnosis title bars, from the new `PanelExpandToggle.tsx`. It has a fixed label ("Expand Investigation Console" / "Expand Diagnosis"), shows its state with `aria-pressed`, and points at the panel body with `aria-controls`. The tooltip changes to "Restore split view" when expanded.
- **Behaviour:** expanding one panel collapses the other to its title bar. Collapsed Diagnosis shows "N evidence" or "Confirmed". Pressing again goes back to the split.
- **Below 1280px:** the buttons are hidden and every panel always shows its contents, even if a panel was expanded before the window was resized.
- **"Manage in Notes"** goes back to the split view if Diagnosis was expanded, so the Notes tab is visible.

**Console sizing** (`LogsView.tsx`, `NetworkView.tsx`): the log and request lists now keep at least 7rem (up from 6rem), and the details panes take at most 50% of the height (down from 55% for logs and 60% for network).

**Header:** the label now reads "Demo Preview" (`src/config/app.ts`).

**Changed files:**
- `src/config/app.ts`
- `src/components/ui/{Panel,icons}.tsx` and `PanelExpandToggle.tsx` (new)
- `src/components/workspace/{WorkspaceLayout,WorkspaceClient,DiagnosisPanel}.tsx`
- `src/components/workspace/investigation/{InvestigationConsole,logs/LogsView,network/NetworkView}.tsx`

`Panel` gained two optional props, `bodyId` and `collapsed`. Its collapsed styling only applies at ≥1280px, so the other panels are unaffected.

**Unchanged:** the diagnosis API, validation and evaluation, scoring, challenge data, the answer key, the engine contracts, dependencies, and Haboon's branch.

## Please check in the browser

1. **1024×768 and 1280×800:**
   - At 1024 you should get the two-row layout; at 1280, three columns.
   - In the two-row layout, the Diagnosis form shouldn't have its own scrollbar, and the Console should be the same height as the form.
   - Nothing should scroll sideways.
2. **1366×768 and 1440×900:** Expand Diagnosis, then Expand Console, then Restore.
   - The collapsed panel should show only its title bar.
   - The Monaco editor should resize properly.
   - Keyboard focus should stay on the button you pressed.
3. **Manage in Notes** with Diagnosis expanded should bring back the split view and focus the Notes tab.
4. **Network details at 1280:** select a request. The list should keep about five rows visible, and the Headers tab should scroll inside the details pane.
5. **Resizing from ≥1280 to below it** while a panel is expanded: both panels should show their contents again.
6. **768px and 390px:** the stacking should look the same as before.
7. **Regression check:** Explorer, editor tabs, Monaco, log filters, Network jumps between tabs, collecting evidence, submitting a diagnosis, and the countdown after too many attempts.
8. **Screen reader:** the expand buttons should be announced as toggle buttons with a pressed state.

I'll wait for your approval before starting Batch 2. When you commit, include `.claude-logs/` per `CLAUDE.md`.
