To check the contrast test catches a failing colour, I briefly put the old `--subtle` value back. The test failed with "`--subtle` on `--background`: 3.28:1", and passed again once restored.

## What changed

**1. Text contrast**
- `--subtle` changed from `#5b6576` to `#7d8799`. That's 4.82–5.33:1 on every surface, up from 2.96–3.28.
- Monaco's comment, log timestamp, debug-level and duration colours mirrored the old `--subtle`, so they changed too.
- The `opacity-70` on the log filter counts was dropping them below 4.5:1, so I removed it.

**2. Monaco line numbers**
- Line numbers changed from `#3f4856` (2.0:1) to `#7d8799` (5.0:1 even on the highlighted current line).
- The active line number changed from `#8a94a6` to `#c3cad6`.
- To let the tests read these colours, the theme rules and colours now live in exported constants. The theme itself is unchanged apart from the colour values.

**3. Focus after closing details.** Closing log or request details with × puts focus back on that row. If filters hide the row, focus goes to the first visible row, or to the search box if nothing is shown.

**4. Focus after removing items.** Removing a hypothesis, observation or evidence item moves focus to the next item's Remove button, or the previous one's. If the list becomes empty, focus goes to that section's add field, or for Evidence, which has no field, to the section heading.

I also fixed one more case of the same kind: the "+ Add to evidence" and "Remove from evidence" buttons replace each other when pressed, so focus used to fall back to the page. It now moves to the replacement button.

**Mouse users:** focus only moves if the button that was pressed had focus. Browsers that don't focus buttons on click, such as Safari, see no focus jump. Where a click does focus the button, the moved focus shows no ring, because browsers only show focus rings after keyboard use.

**5. Monaco and Tab: no change needed.** I checked Monaco 0.57's source: its Tab and Shift+Tab key bindings only apply when the editor is writable. Our editor is read-only, so Tab already moves on to the next control. I didn't set `tabFocusMode`.

**6. Skip links.** "Skip to workspace" and "Skip to diagnosis" are the first things Tab reaches on the page. They stay hidden until focused, then appear above the sticky header. Each points at a hidden landing point inside its section, so the layout doesn't change.

**Files:**
- **New:** `src/lib/focus.ts`, `src/components/layout/SkipLinks.tsx`, `src/components/workspace/investigation/notes/useRemovalFocus.ts`, `tests/ui/accessibility.test.ts`
- **Changed:**
  - `src/app/{globals.css,page.tsx}`
  - `src/lib/monaco-setup.ts`
  - `src/components/workspace/WorkspaceLayout.tsx`
  - `src/components/workspace/investigation/{DetailHeader,EvidenceToggle,useListboxNavigation}.tsx/.ts`
  - `logs/{LogsView,LogDetails,LogFilterBar}.tsx`
  - `network/{NetworkView,RequestDetails}.tsx`
  - `notes/{NotesSection,AddEntryForm,HypothesisList,ObservationList,EvidenceList}.tsx`

**Unchanged:** the diagnosis API, scoring, challenge data, the answer key, engine contracts, dependencies, Haboon's branch, and the Batch 1 layout and expand/restore behaviour.

**New tests:**
- the colour contrast checks: `--foreground`, `--muted` and `--subtle` on all four surfaces, the line numbers on the editor and current-line backgrounds, and every syntax colour;
- `neighborAfterRemoval`: next, previous, empty list, unknown ID;
- `triggerHadFocus` returns false outside a browser.

## 2. Key Technical Concepts

- **Stack:** Next.js 16.4.3 (App Router, Turbopack, `cacheComponents: true`, `partialPrefetching: true`), React 19.3, TypeScript strict, Tailwind v4 (CSS-first `@theme inline` tokens).
- **Next 16 docs:** AGENTS.md requires reading `node_modules/next/dist/docs/` before writing code.
- **Monaco:**
  - Uses `@monaco-editor/react@4.7.0` and `monaco-editor@0.57.0`, with the CDN path pinned to `https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/monaco-editor@0.57.0/min/vs`.
  - Loaded via `next/dynamic` with `ssr:false` inside a client component.
  - Each file gets its own model via the `path` prop.
  - In 0.57, the TypeScript options live under `monaco.typescript`.
  - **Verified for Batch 2:** Monaco's Tab and Shift+Tab commands have precondition `EditorContextKeys.writable`, which is merged into the keybinding's `when` clause (`editorExtensions.js`: `kbWhen = ContextKeyExpr.and(kbWhen, this.precondition)`). A read-only editor therefore doesn't trap Tab, and `tabFocusMode` is not needed. No change was made.
- **Server/client split:** server-only modules use `import "server-only"`, which Next handles internally with no package installed. The route handler is `POST /api/challenges/[challengeId]/diagnosis`.
- **Tests:** Node's built-in `node:test` runner with native TypeScript type stripping (Node 24.20). Loaded via `tests/setup/register.mjs`, which uses `registerHooks` for the `@/` alias, extensionless imports and a `server-only` stub. Script: `"test": "node --import ./tests/setup/register.mjs --test \"tests/**/*.test.ts\""`.
- **ARIA patterns:** a flat tree with roving tabindex; listboxes where selection follows focus; tabs with automatic activation; panels kept mounted with `hidden`; toggle buttons with `aria-pressed`.
- **WCAG contrast:** the luminance formula. The old `--subtle` (#5b6576) measured about 3:1; the new #7d8799 measures 4.82–5.33:1.
- **Haboon's engine branch** (`b7588e5`): `src/engine/types.ts` (ChallengePublic, SessionState, SessionEvent, ScoringRules, Score), `session/reducer.ts`, `session/scoring.ts`, `test-fixtures.ts`, and vitest `^3.2.7`. A simulated merge conflicts only on the `package.json` `test` script and on `package-lock.json`. The merge is postponed until contracts are agreed.

## 3. Files and Code Sections

**Overall:** state from Stages 1–4 and the evidence fix is committed (latest commit `b1eb3af fix: recognize equivalent investigation evidence`). Batch 1 and Batch 2 changes are uncommitted in the working tree.

**Key existing architecture**
- `src/app/page.tsx` (server component): `<SkipLinks/>`, `<AppHeader/>`, then `<main>` containing `ChallengeOverview` and `WorkspaceClient`. It passes challengeId, rootCauseOptions, files, entryFilePath, incidentDate, logs and network from `challenge001`.
- `src/data/challenge-001/`: `challenge.ts`, `files.ts` (14 scenario files, an Express app), `logs.ts` (23 entries; `INCIDENT_DATE` is 2026-10-30), `network.ts` (14 requests, req-01 to req-14, each with a serverLogId), `diagnosis-options.ts` (5 public options in a stable order), `index.ts`, and `answer-key.server.ts` (server-only).
  - The answer key holds `correctRootCauseId: "signed-cookie-read-as-unsigned"`, a `resolutionSummary`, and `keyObservations` with four groups:
    - signed-cookie-issued: req-01, req-08
    - cookie-sent-still-401: req-04, req-05, req-11, req-12
    - auth-rejected: log-06, log-08, log-17, log-19
    - login-succeeded: log-02, log-13
- **Server diagnosis code** in `src/server/diagnosis/`:
  - `registry.ts` has `buildKeyObservations`, which validates that IDs exist and don't repeat across groups.
  - `evaluate.ts` produces generic feedback for incorrect answers. For correct answers the hint reads: "Your evidence covered X of 4 key observations for this incident."
  - `attempt-limiter.ts` is a FixedWindowLimiter.
  - `client-ip.ts` trusts X-Forwarded-For only when `BUGLAB_TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS` is set.
  - `handler.ts` checks, in order: 415 content type, 403 origin, 404 unknown challenge, 413 body over 16 KB, 400 invalid input. It then applies the 429 limits (3 wrong attempts per 10 minutes per `buglab_attempt` cookie, and 10 per 10 minutes per trusted IP) before evaluating, and sends `no-store`.
  - `src/app/api/challenges/[challengeId]/diagnosis/route.ts` wires it up.
- `src/lib/diagnosis/validate.ts` (shared input validation), `src/lib/diagnosis/client.ts`, `src/lib/evidence.ts` (`getEvidenceId` returns `log:log-06` or `network:req-04`).
- **Tests** in `tests/diagnosis/`: fixtures, validate, evaluate, attempt-limiter, handler, confidentiality and key-observations tests.

```ts
export function neighborAfterRemoval(ids: readonly string[], removedId: string): string | null {
  const index = ids.indexOf(removedId);
  if (index === -1) return null;
  return ids[index + 1] ?? ids[index - 1] ?? null;
}
export function triggerHadFocus(event: { currentTarget: EventTarget }): boolean {
  return typeof document !== "undefined" && document.activeElement === event.currentTarget;
}
```
- `useListboxNavigation.ts`: added `focusOptionOrFallback(id, fallbackId)`. It focuses the option, else the first `[role="option"]`, else `document.getElementById(fallbackId)`. It is returned from the hook.
- `DetailHeader.tsx`, `logs/LogDetails.tsx` and `network/RequestDetails.tsx`: `onClose` is now typed `(event: MouseEvent<HTMLButtonElement>) => void`, with React type imports added.
- `LogsView.tsx` and `NetworkView.tsx`: import `triggerHadFocus` (placed after the react import) and destructure `focusOptionOrFallback`. The details `onClose` became:
```tsx
onClose={(event) => {
  // Return focus to the entry so keyboard users keep their place.
  const restore = triggerHadFocus(event);
  onSelect(null);
  if (restore) focusOptionOrFallback(selected.id, LOG_SEARCH_INPUT_ID /* or SEARCH_INPUT_ID */);
}}
```
  NetworkView also has `const SEARCH_INPUT_ID = "network-search";` and `id={SEARCH_INPUT_ID}` on its search input.
- `EvidenceToggle.tsx` was rewritten. It uses `addRef`, `removeRef` and `moveFocusRef`. A `useEffect([collected])` focuses the replacement button only if the pressed button had focus. The `handle(event, action)` wrapper sets `moveFocusRef.current = triggerHadFocus(event)`.
- `notes/useRemovalFocus.ts` (new): `useRemovalFocus(ids, fallbackId)` returns `{listRef, removeWithFocus(event, id, onRemove)}`. It resolves the target before removing: the button `[data-remove-id="${nextId}"]` or the fallback element. It focuses only if the trigger had focus.
- `notes/NotesSection.tsx`: exports `notesHeadingId(title)`. The h3 gets `tabIndex={-1}` and `rounded-sm ... outline-none focus-visible:ring-1 focus-visible:ring-accent/70`.
- `notes/AddEntryForm.tsx`: optional `inputId` prop (`providedId ?? useId()`).
- `HypothesisList.tsx`, `ObservationList.tsx` and `EvidenceList.tsx`:
  - Use the hook, with `ref={listRef}` on the ul and `data-remove-id` plus `onClick={(event) => removeWithFocus(event, id, onRemove)}` on Remove buttons.
  - Fallbacks: `NEW_HYPOTHESIS_ID = "notes-new-hypothesis"`, `NEW_OBSERVATION_ID = "notes-new-observation"`, and `notesHeadingId("Evidence")`.
- `src/components/layout/SkipLinks.tsx` (new):

## Results

| Check | Result |
|---|---|

| `npx tsc --noEmit` | 0 errors |

**About the confidentiality scan:** I couldn't recover the exact list used in Batch 2, so I rebuilt it from the server files: the answer-key field names, the four observation group IDs, the hint wording, the resolution text, the proxy setting and the attempt cookie name.

A looser scan also turned up the correct option ID, `signed-cookie-read-as-unsigned`. That's expected: it's one of the five public options learners pick from, and nothing on the client marks it as the right one.

## What changed

**1. Editor tabs** (`EditorTabs.tsx`)
- Tabs are capped at 12rem. Long names end in "…", and hovering shows the full path.
- The tab strip still scrolls sideways, and the arrow keys still move between tabs.
- When a tab becomes active, for example from the Explorer or an initiator link, it scrolls into view. Only the tab strip scrolls, never the page.
- Files with the same name show their nearest folder in grey, such as `index.ts routes` and `index.ts lib`, adding more folders when needed. Screen readers hear "index.ts in routes", and the close button says "Close index.ts (routes)".
- Challenge 001 has no duplicate file names, so you'll only see the folder labels in the tests for now.

**2. Network list** (`RequestRow.tsx`): long paths are cut off with "…" and the full path shows on hover. Screen readers still read the whole path, and opening the request shows it in full in the details header.

**3. Details panels**
- The details header used to cut off long titles; it now wraps them, including long request paths.
- In log details, the source, message and field names wrap instead of pushing the layout wider.
- In request details and header tables, the value column can now shrink and wrap.
- Long header names wrap and are capped at 14rem.
- A long initiator file link wraps and stays left-aligned.
- Log rows also wrap very long messages that have no spaces.

**4. Evidence and notes**
- In Diagnosis, each evidence item shows a two-line preview. The full text is available on hover, to screen readers, and in the Notes evidence list, which still shows everything.
- Evidence, hypotheses and observations in Notes now wrap long unbroken strings such as URLs, cookie values and tokens, instead of overflowing.

**5. Editor footer** (`EditorPanel.tsx`): the folder and file name are now separate. When space runs out, the folder shortens first, so `authenticate.ts` stays readable next to the language label. Hovering shows the full path.

**Files**
- **New:** `src/lib/scroll.ts`, `tests/ui/overflow.test.ts`
- **Changed:**
  - `src/lib/file-tree.ts`
  - `EditorTabs.tsx`, `EditorPanel.tsx`
  - `investigation/DetailHeader.tsx`
  - `logs/{LogRow,LogDetails}.tsx`
  - `network/{RequestRow,RequestDetails,HeaderTable}.tsx`
  - `diagnosis/EvidenceReview.tsx`
  - `notes/{EvidenceList,HypothesisList,ObservationList}.tsx`

**Unchanged:** the diagnosis API, scoring, challenge data, the answer key, the Challenge Engine, dependencies, Haboon's branch, and the Batch 1 layout and Batch 2 focus handling.

**New tests** cover:
- the folder labels for duplicate names: unique names, a repeated parent folder, and a root-level file;
- the footer's folder and file split;
- scrolling a tab into view: already visible, cut off on the left, cut off on the right, and wider than the strip.

## Known limitations
- **Hover text isn't keyboard-reachable.** Keyboard and touch users can't open those tooltips. The full text is always available elsewhere: screen readers read it, request paths appear in full in the details header, and evidence appears in full in Notes.
- **Very narrow footer.** The folder can shrink to nothing, leaving just "…" before the file name.

1. **Tabs:** open about eight files at around 1024px wide. The strip should scroll sideways. Opening a file from the Explorer that's out of view should scroll its tab into view without moving the page. Arrow keys should keep the focused tab visible.

6. **Earlier batches:** spot-check that expand/restore still works and that focus still returns correctly after × and Remove.

timestamp: 2026-11-01T08:23:30.402Z

The proposal isn't ready to approve, mainly because it isn't on the branch. The pushed `feature/engine-workspace-integration` has no `DIAGNOSIS_ACCEPTED` event, no `onResponse` callback and no session-derived status. I searched the code, the commit messages and Haboon's session log. The branch contains only the engine core from before, moved from vitest to our `node:test` runner. If the proposal was shared another way, such as a doc, a message or unpushed commits, please send it and I'll review it against the points below.

## What's on the branch

| | |
|---|---|
| Base | Our commit `b1eb3af`, so it doesn't include our Stage 5A commit `211da9b` |
| Commits | 3 by Haboon: the engine core, the test port to `node:test`, and removing vitest |
| Files | `src/engine/{types,session/reducer,session/scoring}.ts`, `tests/engine/*`, and one session log. Nothing of ours is touched, and `package.json` and the lockfile match ours. |
| Merge with our current branch | Simulated read-only: no conflicts |
| Tests (scratch copy) | 86 of 86 pass (our 59 plus his 27) |
| `tsc` (scratch copy) | Only the `LayoutProps` error from the fresh copy, nothing from the engine |

The single runner and dropping vitest settle one of the earlier open decisions (#2) the way we wanted.

## Comparison with our contracts

**1. Shared challenge types are incompatible.** The engine defines its own versions instead of using `src/types/*`:

| | Engine | Ours |
|---|---|---|
| `ChallengeSummary` | `summary`, `tags`, `estMinutes`, no status | Has `status` (hard-coded `"in-progress"`) |
| `Difficulty` | `1–5` | `"beginner" \| "intermediate" \| "advanced"` |
| Files | `WorkspaceFile { contents, readOnly }` | `ScenarioFile { path, content }` |
| Root-cause options | `causeOptions: {id, label}` | `RootCauseOption` with `description` |
| Language | `language: "javascript"` | Challenge 001 is TypeScript, plus logs and JSON |

Same-named types in two places invite wrong imports. Also, `createSession()` only accepts the engine's `ChallengePublic`, so it can't take our data without an adapter.

**Required:** the engine should import our `src/types/*` and drop its duplicate `ChallengeSummary`, `Difficulty` and `WorkspaceFile`. `createSession` should take a small config `{ id, scoring, completion, editableFiles }`, as Haboon's own audit suggested.

**2. The diagnosis API is unaffected.** The branch changes nothing in the request, response or route, so it's fully compatible today. The additive `evidence: { covered, total }` field on correct answers from Haboon's audit is still only an idea, not on the branch.

**3. The `DIAGNOSIS_ACCEPTED` event doesn't exist.** The engine only has `DIAGNOSIS_CHECKED { correct }`, and it has two gaps:
- A correct diagnosis sets `diagnosed` but never sets `solved`. Only a fix run that passes every test does, and Challenge 001 has no fix mode, so this challenge can never be completed.
- The maximum score includes fix points that can't be earned, so a perfect diagnosis would show as 30–40% of the maximum.

**Required:** either a completion mode (`"diagnosis" | "fix"`) where a correct diagnosis sets `solved`, or a distinct accepted event with the same effect. The maximum score for a diagnosis-only challenge must leave out fix points. Whichever event is used must carry nothing from the server beyond the correct/incorrect result.

**4. Learner status vs `ChallengeSummary` is still open.** Today our status is static data in `challenge.ts`. The engine uses `"in_progress"` with an underscore; ours uses `"in-progress"` with a hyphen, and has values the engine lacks.

**Required:** the status shown should come from the session, not from challenge data. A mapping is needed from `in_progress`, `solved` and `revealed` to our labels. `status` should then leave the static `ChallengeSummary`, which should describe the challenge, not the learner.

**5. The `onResponse` callback doesn't exist.** Today `useDiagnosisSubmission` keeps the response internally. An optional `onResponse(response)` on the hook or `DiagnosisPanel` is a reasonable integration point that wouldn't change any API. It should:
- fire once per completed submission;
- not fire when a submission is cancelled (our client re-throws on abort);
- pass the same `DiagnosisResponse` the UI shows, without any extra fields.

**6. Response handling.** The only rules on record are from Haboon's audit, not the code: only `correct` and `incorrect` become events. I agree, and anything implemented later must also cover:
- `invalid`, `rate-limited` and `error` record nothing and don't count as an attempt.
- `rate-limited` must stay indistinguishable for right and wrong answers. Our server enforces this by checking limits before evaluating, and the engine must not undo it, for example by treating a 429 as an implied wrong answer.
- A second `correct` after solving must not add points. The reducer already ignores it.
- An `incorrect` after a `correct` is also ignored, which is right. Note that the server doesn't track sessions, so it will still count that submission against the attempt limit.
- `error` covers network failures and timeouts as well as server errors, so it must never be read as a judgement on the answer.

**7. Public and protected data are still separate, with one risk.** The branch adds no challenge data, and `ChallengePublic` carries no answers. The engine's scoring rules are visible to the client, which is fine because scoring is advisory.

The risk is Haboon's planned `ChallengeDefinition`, which would hold the public scenario and the answer key together. If one module both holds the key and is imported by `page.tsx`, the key ends up in the client bundle.

**Required:**
- The answer key stays in an `import "server-only"` module that only `src/server/**` imports.
- The definition the UI uses only refers to public modules.
- The confidentiality test and the bundle scan both run on the integrated branch.

## Verdict: not ready to approve

1. **Blocker:** the proposal itself isn't on the branch. Ask Haboon to push it, as code or as a contract document.
2. **Before integration:**
   - Retire the duplicate types (item 1).
   - Add diagnosis-only completion and fix the maximum score (item 3).
   - Map the status and remove it from `ChallengeSummary` (item 4).
   - Specify how each response type is handled (item 6).
   - Keep the answer key out of `ChallengeDefinition` (item 7).
   - Rebase onto `211da9b`; the simulated merge was clean.
3. **Fine to approve now:** the single `node:test` runner, dropping vitest, and the pure reducer and scoring design.

**Repo state:**
- I didn't change your working tree, branches, commits or remote. `git fetch` added the remote-tracking ref `origin/feature/engine-workspace-integration`.
- The simulated merge (`git merge-tree`) wrote temporary objects to the Git database only; no branch or file changed.
- The test run used a copy of Haboon's branch in a scratch folder outside the repo. I've removed its link to our `node_modules`, and our `node_modules` is intact.
- During my first, failed attempt, `npx tsc` downloaded an unrelated `tsc@2.0.4` package into npm's cache and stopped with a "not the tsc command you are looking for" message. Nothing was installed in the project.

The only change in the working tree is the session log, which updates itself every turn.

I haven't written or changed any code. Here's the plan for review.

## 1. Current structure and design system

**Routing**
- There are two routes: `/` (`src/app/page.tsx`, the workspace for Challenge 001) and `POST /api/challenges/[challengeId]/diagnosis`. No route groups exist yet.
- The root layout (`src/app/layout.tsx`) loads Geist and Geist Mono, sets the page title to "BugLab · Debugging Workspace", and gives `body` the background and text colours.
- With `cacheComponents` on, a page with no request data is prerendered as static, as `/` already is.

**Design tokens** (`globals.css`)
- Dark only. Backgrounds run from `background` `#0b0e14` through `surface`, `surface-raised` and `surface-sunken`, with `border` and `border-strong` for edges.
- Text uses `foreground`, `muted` and `subtle`. `subtle` meets WCAG AA after Batch 2.
- The single accent is blue `#6ea8fe`, with success, warning, danger and info colours. Tailwind v4 makes all of these available as classes like `bg-surface` and `text-muted`.

**Look and feel**
- Small, tight type, monospace for technical data, thin borders, `rounded-md` and `rounded-lg` corners, and 10% tints for badges.
- Motion is limited to the status dot's ping, which respects reduced-motion settings.

**Reusable pieces**
- `Badge` and `StatusDot` work as-is.
- `BugIcon` is the logo mark; there are about ten icons in total.
- `challenge001.challenge` provides public title, incident, tags, difficulty and estimated time.
- `AppHeader` isn't reusable here: it marks Workspace as the current page and shows "Soon" items.
- There are no button or link components yet.

## 2. Recommended sections
Everything described below already exists in the product.

1. **Header:** the BugLab mark, links to sections on the page ("How it works", "Workspace", "Challenge"), the "Demo Preview" label, and a button labelled "Open workspace" that links to `/`.
2. **Hero:**
   - A headline along the lines of *"Learn to debug like a working engineer."*
   - A short line below it: investigate a realistic incident through code, logs and network traffic, then defend your diagnosis with evidence.
   - Buttons: **Start Challenge 001** and **How it works**.
   - A static preview of the workspace underneath (see section 3).
3. **How it works:** four steps: read the incident, investigate code, logs and requests, collect evidence and notes, submit a diagnosis and get feedback.
4. **Inside the workspace:** six cards:
   - a read-only code explorer and editor;
   - log search and level filters;
   - a network inspector with headers and bodies;
   - hypotheses, observations and evidence notes;
   - evidence-backed diagnosis;
   - keyboard accessibility.

   One honest trust point: answers are checked on the server and never sent to the browser.
5. **Featured challenge:** a card for "Broken User Sessions" with difficulty, tags and about 30 minutes, built from the public data, with a CTA. It doesn't show a learner status, which is hard-coded today and being reworked by the engine integration.
6. **Planned (optional, your call):** more challenges, Fix Mode, saved progress. Each is plainly marked *Planned, not yet available*, with no dates and no buttons. We could also leave this out entirely, which is the safest way to avoid overpromising.
7. **Closing CTA and footer:** "Demo Preview", and a note that investigation notes reset on refresh.

**Left out on purpose:** scores, accounts, leaderboards, progress, challenge counts, testimonials and pricing. None of those exist.

## 3. Visual direction and interactions

**Look:** inspired by Linear and Vercel, using our own tokens.
- A large, tightly spaced headline in `foreground`, with supporting text in `muted`.
- A faint blue glow behind the hero, plus a subtle dotted grid that fades out toward the edges.
- Thin-bordered `surface` cards, and generous space between sections, roughly `py-24` on desktop.
- All built with Tailwind arbitrary values inside the landing components, so no new global tokens are needed.

**Hero preview:** a mock workspace built in plain HTML and CSS rather than a screenshot. It shows a file tree, a few lines of code, and log rows like `POST /api/login 200` followed by `GET /api/profile 401`.
- It only shows the symptom already stated in the incident text, never anything pointing at the cause such as signed cookies.
- Screen readers get one short description of it instead of the fake contents.
- It doesn't load Monaco.

**Interactions:**
- The page is all server components, with no client JavaScript.
- Links and cards have hover and focus states using the existing `focus-visible` ring style.
- "How it works" scrolls to the right section, with offsets so the sticky header doesn't cover the heading.
- There's no scroll-triggered animation. One optional extra, a gentle fade-in on the hero, would apply only when the visitor hasn't asked for reduced motion.

**Responsive:**
- On mobile, the sections stack, the preview shrinks to a single log panel, and the header keeps only the logo and CTA.
- From the `md` breakpoint, the feature cards form a 2-column grid; from `lg`, a 3-column grid and the full 3-pane preview.
- The page keeps the 16px side gutter and never scrolls sideways.

## 4. Files to create
All new; nothing existing is modified.

| File | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `src/app/welcome/page.tsx` | The `/welcome` route: page-level title and description, assembles the sections, prerendered static |
| `src/data/landing.ts` | All landing copy and lists: steps, features, planned items, CTA links. Keeps content separate from UI, as the challenge data does. |
| `src/components/landing/LandingHeader.tsx` | Header for the landing page |
| `src/components/landing/Hero.tsx` | Headline, CTAs and the background glow and grid |
| `src/components/landing/ProductPreview.tsx` | Static workspace mock-up |
| `src/components/landing/HowItWorks.tsx` | The four steps |
| `src/components/landing/FeatureGrid.tsx` | Workspace capability cards |
| `src/components/landing/FeaturedChallenge.tsx` | Challenge 001 card built from the public challenge data |
| `src/components/landing/Planned.tsx` | Only if you want section 6 |
| `src/components/landing/LandingFooter.tsx` | Closing CTA and footer |
| `src/components/landing/icons.tsx` | Landing-only icons (arrow, search, network, notes, shield, keyboard), kept separate so the shared `icons.tsx` stays untouched |
| `tests/ui/landing.test.ts` | Checks below |

**What the tests check:**
- Every CTA points to a route that exists.
- Planned items are all marked planned, and nothing in the features list says "fix", "score" or "progress".
- The landing data doesn't import any server-only or answer-key module.

As usual, I'd also run tests, lint, `tsc`, the build, a scan of the client bundle for answer-key strings, and a check that `/welcome` builds as static.

## 5. Possible conflicts with the engine integration
- **No overlapping files.** The engine works in `src/engine/**` and `tests/engine/**`. The integration is expected to change `src/types/workspace.ts`, `challenge.ts`, `page.tsx` and `ChallengeOverview`. The landing page touches none of these.
- **Shared challenge type.** `FeaturedChallenge` reads `ChallengeSummary`. If the integration changes `difficulty` to `1–5` or renames fields, the landing card breaks. It skips `status`, so that removal won't affect it. Any break shows up as a `tsc` error rather than failing silently. The difficulty labels would be a small copy of the mapping in `ChallengeOverview` rather than a new export, to avoid editing a workspace file.
- **Workspace URL may move.** If integration later moves the workspace to something like `/challenges/challenge-001`, or makes the landing page `/`, the CTA links change. I'd keep that link in one constant in `src/data/landing.ts`.
- **Nothing links to `/welcome` yet.** Adding a link would mean editing `AppHeader`, which is shared with the workspace. That waits until you decide whether the landing page eventually becomes `/`.
- **No dependencies, config or global CSS changes.** That avoids conflicts in `package.json`, the lockfile and `globals.css`.
- **Branch:** I'd build this on a new `feature/landing-page` branch from `feature/debugging-workspace`, so it stays separate from the integration.

**Decisions I need from you:**
1. Include the "Planned" section, or leave it out?
2. Is a new `feature/landing-page` branch okay?
3. Do you want the optional hero fade-in?

I'll wait for your approval before writing anything.

# BugLab landing page, v2: "Signal in the noise"

## Concept
Debugging is finding one signal in a lot of noise. The page acts that out. Each section moves from noise to signal: dense, dim, monospace texture first, then one line that lights up in BugLab blue and leads the visitor on.

The identity rests on three motifs:

1. **The trace.** A thin, glowing blue line runs down the page like a request trace. It starts in the hero, passes through each section and ends at the final CTA, as if the visitor's investigation leads to the button.
2. **Timestamps as texture.** Monospace timestamps like `09:14:02.487`, incident IDs and `+00:03.2` offsets act as typographic ornament. Linear and Vercel use plain sans type; this gives BugLab its own character.
3. **The flare.** The one recurring highlight is a single log line or row lighting up red, then blue: the moment something is noticed. It's used sparingly, so it stays meaningful.

**Lighting:** three layers in the hero.
- A dim blue aurora at the top.
- Very faint film grain from a small inline SVG, which takes the flat digital edge off.
- A glowing "horizon" line that the product showcase rises out of.

Elsewhere the page stays near-black, with light appearing only where attention should go.

**Type:**
- We keep Geist; there are no new fonts.
- The headline is much larger, about 3–5.75rem depending on screen width, tightly spaced, and fades from `foreground` to a dimmer grey across the line.
- Small labels are monospace, like terminal output.
- Body text stays `muted`, at a comfortable line length.

## Messaging
The landing page will no longer use "Demo Preview", `APP_STAGE_LABEL`, or the words demo, prototype, coming soon or assessment. A test will enforce this.

**Headline options:**
- **"Every bug leaves a trail."** (recommended), with the subline *"BugLab drops you into a production incident: the code, the logs, the network traffic. Follow the evidence and prove what broke."*
- Alternative: *"Find the bug. Prove it."*

The note about notes resetting is true and stays, reworded as "Notes and evidence live in your browser tab."

**One mismatch I can't fix within your constraints:** the workspace's own header (`AppHeader`, a shared file) still shows "Demo Preview" and "Soon". Visitors will see those right after clicking the CTA. Changing them is a separate decision.

## Sections

**0. Header**
- A frosted bar that gets slightly more solid once you scroll past the hero.
- The section links mark which section you're in, using a small blue underline that slides between them.
- The CTA has a slowly rotating glowing border on hover.

**1. Hero**
- A mono label at the top: `● INC-001 · SEV-2 · opened 09:16 UTC`, with the dot pulsing.
- The headline, subline and two CTAs: "Start investigating" and "Watch an investigation", which scrolls down to the replay.
- **Background, a "log field":** columns of real, public log lines drifting slowly upward at about 6% opacity and fading out toward the edges. Every few seconds a single `401` line flares.
  - It's pure CSS: one block of text moving with `transform`, so it's cheap to run.
  - It's hidden from screen readers.
  - It pauses when off-screen, and doesn't move at all for visitors who prefer reduced motion.

**2. Incident replay** (the showcase, described in detail below). The workspace frame starts tilted back about 10° and straightens as you scroll into it, landing flat and level just as the replay starts.

**3. The method.** The four cards become a trace waterfall, the kind of chart engineers read in observability tools.
- Each step is a horizontal bar with an offset: `+00:00 Read the incident` → `+02:40 Search the logs` → `+06:15 Inspect requests` → `+11:30 Collect evidence` → `+18:05 Submit a diagnosis`.
- The bars grow from left to right as the section scrolls into view, with a sentence of copy beside each.
- On mobile it becomes a vertical timeline.

**4. Inside the workspace** (replaces the identical 6-card grid). A grid of tiles in mixed sizes, each showing one real feature, and some you can try:

| Tile | Size | What it shows |
|---|---|---|
| Network inspector | Large | A mini request list; hovering or focusing a row slides in its status, timing and harmless headers |
| Log search | Wide | A real, labelled search box that filters eight sample lines as you type, with matches highlighted |
| Notes | Tall | Hypothesis, observation and evidence entries stacking up with a gentle spring |
| Checked on the server | Medium | A small diagram: the browser sends a diagnosis, the server replies with feedback, and a lock icon sits on the server side. The trace line travels across it. |
| Keyboard first | Small | Keycaps (`↑ ↓ Tab Enter`) that light up in sequence; real key presses light them while the tile has focus |

All tiles share a soft blue glow that follows the pointer (see "Hover and feedback").

**5. Case file** (replaces the featured challenge card). Challenge 001 presented as an incident dossier:
- a case number in the corner and a SEV-2 badge;
- the incident summary;
- the public timeline from `INCIDENT.md`: 08:55 deploy, 09:14 first reports, 09:20 acknowledged;
- difficulty, tags and about 30 minutes;
- the CTA "Open the case".

When it scrolls into view, the timeline draws in one entry at a time.

**6. Closing**
- A large statement: *"The logs are waiting."*
- The trace line that ran down the page ends here and lights up the CTA as it arrives.
- A minimal footer underneath.

## The workspace showcase: "Incident replay"
A 5-chapter scripted investigation playing inside a mock workspace built in plain HTML and CSS, with no Monaco. **It stops just before the diagnosis**, which is the hook.

| # | Chapter | What happens |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Read | `INCIDENT.md` opens, and its summary types in line by line |
| 2 | Search | The log panel fills in. A cursor types `401` into the filter, non-matching rows fade out, and the four 401 lines flare. |
| 3 | Inspect | `GET /api/profile 401` is selected and its details slide in: status, timing, initiator `public/dashboard.js`, and only harmless headers such as `Content-Type`, `Cache-Control` and `Date` |
| 4 | Collect | "+ Add to evidence" is pressed, the evidence counter ticks 0 → 1 → 2, and a note types in: *"Login succeeds, but the next authenticated requests return 401."* |
| 5 | Diagnose | The Diagnosis panel opens with its options blurred and unreadable, and a message: **"You have the evidence. What broke?"** → **Finish the investigation** |

**Never shown:** `Cookie` or `Set-Cookie` headers, any code, any option text, or anything about where the problem is. Everything shown restates the symptom from the incident summary. Tests will check the replay script against hint words and against every option's ID and label.

**How it plays:**
- A row of chapter buttons (Read · Search · Inspect · Collect · Diagnose) acts as tabs: click one to jump there.
- A progress bar fills under the active chapter.
- There's a play/pause button, which WCAG 2.2.2 requires for anything that moves automatically for more than 5 seconds.
- A visible caption explains each chapter, so the story works without the animation.

**When it plays:**
- It starts automatically only when at least 50% of it is on screen, the browser tab is visible, and the visitor hasn't asked for reduced motion.
- It pauses when scrolled away and after it finishes; visitors can replay it.
- With reduced motion, it never plays automatically. Visitors move through it with the chapter buttons, and each chapter appears instantly.

**How it's built:**
- One client component.
- The step logic is a pure reducer, so it can be unit-tested.
- Timers come from a small hook.
- Animations use only `transform` and `opacity` changes.
- The server renders chapter 1 in full, so search engines and visitors without JavaScript see a complete, meaningful frame.

## Motion system

**Feel:** one easing curve (`cubic-bezier(.2,.8,.2,1)`), durations of 180ms for interactions, 400ms for small transitions and 700ms for scroll reveals, and a 60ms stagger between items. Movement is subtle: at most 12px or 2% scale. Nothing bounces except the note entries.

**Scroll reveals:**
- A small `<Reveal>` client component uses one shared IntersectionObserver.
- Content is visible by default in the server-rendered page. Only elements still below the fold when the page loads are prepared for animation, before the first paint, so there's no flash of hidden content and nothing breaks without JavaScript.
- Each element reveals once.
- Patterns vary by section rather than repeating one fade-up: text rises slightly, bars grow, timelines draw in, tiles reveal from their own edge.

**Scroll-linked effects** (progressive enhancement):
- The showcase tilt and the trace line drawing itself use CSS scroll-driven animations (`animation-timeline: view()`) where the browser supports them: Chrome, Edge and Safari 26.
- In Firefox and with reduced motion, they show their finished, static state. No JavaScript scroll listeners.

**Off-screen and hidden tabs:** the log field, the pulsing dot and the replay all pause.

## Hover and feedback

**CTA buttons**
- Hovering rotates a glowing conic-gradient border around the button; this needs `@property`.
- The arrow nudges forward 2px.
- Pressing scales the button to 0.98.
- Keyboard focus gets the same glow plus a clear outline.

**Tiles and the case file**
- A blue glow follows the pointer. One shared pointer handler sets CSS variables, so nothing re-renders.
- The border brightens on hover.
- Keyboard focus shows a centred version of the same glow.

**Links and the active section underline** use a growing underline.

**Feedback on interactive bits:**
- Log search matches pulse once.
- Replay chapter buttons fill as they progress.
- Copying the case ID gives a small "Copied" confirmation. This is optional and is the only extra interaction beyond what's described above.

## Files

**Changed:**
- `src/data/landing.ts`: new copy, the replay script, bento tile data, the case timeline, no stage label.
- `Hero.tsx`, `LandingHeader.tsx`, `LandingFooter.tsx` and `CtaLink.tsx`.
- `tests/ui/landing.test.ts`: banned-words check; hint and option checks on the replay script and tile data.

**Replaced (deleted):** `ProductPreview.tsx`, `HowItWorks.tsx`, `FeatureGrid.tsx`, `FeaturedChallenge.tsx`.

**New**, all in `src/components/landing/`:
- `LogField.tsx`: the hero's drifting log field.
- `TraceLine.tsx`: the line that runs down the page.
- `Reveal.tsx` (client): scroll reveals.
- `Spotlight.tsx` (client): the pointer-following glow.
- `replay/IncidentReplay.tsx` (client), `replay/ReplayStage.tsx`, `replay/ChapterRail.tsx`, `replay/useReplay.ts` and `replay/replay-reducer.ts`: the showcase.
- `MethodWaterfall.tsx`: the method section.
- `instruments/{NetworkTile,LogSearchTile,NotesTile,ServerCheckTile,KeyboardTile}.tsx`: the grid tiles; `LogSearchTile` is a client component.
- `CaseFile.tsx` and `ClosingCta.tsx`.
- `landing.module.css`: the animation keyframes, `@property` and scroll-driven rules.

**New test:** `tests/ui/replay.test.ts` covers the chapter order and timing, that it never moves past chapter 5, that pause and resume work, and that reduced motion disables autoplay.

**Unchanged:** `page.tsx`, the workspace, the engine, diagnosis, `globals.css`, shared UI, `config/app.ts`, `package.json` and the lockfile.

## Tradeoffs and concerns

1. **CSS needs:** keyframes, `@property` and scroll timelines can't be written as Tailwind classes alone. I'd put them in `landing.module.css`, a CSS Module that is scoped to the landing components and only loaded on `/welcome`. It doesn't touch `globals.css`. Please confirm that's acceptable.
2. **JavaScript cost:** this adds roughly 8–12 KB of JavaScript, gzipped, for the replay, reveals, glow and search tile. There are no libraries, and everything else stays server-rendered and static. I'd keep the hero text out of any JS-driven animation so the page's main content appears as fast as possible.
3. **Autoplay can distract.** It's limited to when the replay is on screen, has a pause button, stops after one run, and is off with reduced motion. If you'd rather have no autoplay at all, a "▶ Replay the investigation" button works too, at some cost to the "living product" feel.
4. **Firefox** misses the scroll-linked tilt and line drawing, and sees them in their finished state. The reveals and everything else work the same.
5. **Readability:**
   - Gradient headline text must stay at least 3:1 contrast even at its dimmest point. I'll add that to the contrast tests.
   - The log field sits behind the text at very low opacity, under a darkening fade.
   - The grain is limited to 3% opacity.
6. **Accessibility:**
   - The replay is a proper tab pattern with captions.
   - It doesn't announce every automatic step to screen readers; it only announces chapters the visitor picks.
   - The search tile has a real label.
   - Decorative layers are hidden from screen readers.
7. **Spoiler risk:** the replay is the highest-risk part, so its content lives in `landing.ts` where the tests can check it, and it ends before any diagnosis.
8. **Maintenance:** the replay copies the workspace's look rather than reusing its components, so it can drift if the workspace UI changes. That's deliberate: importing real workspace components would tie the landing page to code the engine integration is about to change.

## Decisions for you
1. Is `landing.module.css`, a CSS Module scoped to the landing page, okay?
2. Should the replay play automatically when it scrolls into view (recommended), or only when clicked?
3. Which headline: "Every bug leaves a trail." or "Find the bug. Prove it."?
4. Should the workspace's "Demo Preview" and "Soon" labels stay for now, or would you approve a separate small change to `AppHeader`?

I'll wait for your approval before writing any code.

## Results

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `npm test` | 96 passed, 0 failed |
| Lint / `tsc` / build | All clean; `/welcome` is still prerendered as static |
| Confidentiality scan | 0 hits for 17 answer-key and server-only strings across 54 client and prerendered files |

**Browser checks** (scripted in headless Chrome against the production build):
- **Overflow:** no sideways scrolling at 360, 768 or 1440px; no console errors.
- **Scroll reveals:** all 23 fire while scrolling; nothing stays hidden.
- **Autoplay:**
  - idle until the replay is half visible, then advances a chapter about every 4.5 seconds;
  - holds when scrolled away and resumes from where it stopped;
  - stops on Diagnose with a "Replay" button and a "Finish the investigation" link to `/`.
- **Reduced motion:** no autoplay after 6 seconds in view; no hidden content; the log field and tilt are off; chapter tabs still work.
- **Keyboard:**
  - the first Tab reaches "Skip to content";
  - ← → Home End move between chapters, wrapping at the ends;
  - Enter and Space toggle play and pause;
  - focus outlines show;
  - the live region announces only chapters you pick.
- **Live tiles:** searching "401" shows "2 of 8 lines" with highlights, and clicking a request updates its details.

## JavaScript impact (measured gzipped)

| | v1 | v2 |
|---|---|---|
| All JS loaded by `/welcome` | 174.3 KB | **182.8 KB (+8.5 KB)** |
| The page's own chunk | 3.3 KB | 12.0 KB |
| Landing CSS (only `/welcome` loads it) | none | 2.6 KB |
| HTML | about 20 KB | 18.6 KB |

The rest is the React and Next runtime that every page loads; the workspace page loads 188.5 KB.

## Problems found and fixed during verification
1. **The CSS Module didn't work in this project.** The Turbopack rule in `next.config.ts` loads every `*.css` file, modules included, as global CSS. In the first build every `styles.x` was `undefined`, so the whole animation system was silently off.
   - I couldn't edit `next.config.ts`, so `landing.css` is now a plain stylesheet loaded only by `/welcome`, with every class and keyframe prefixed `lp-`.
   - `styles.ts` maps the names for components.
   - A test checks that every mapped class is defined and that nothing else loads the stylesheet. I confirmed it fails if a class goes missing.
2. **The full scenario log was in the client JavaScript.** The hero's log lines now live in a server-only module, `landing-hero.ts`.
3. **Sample headers weren't accurate.** I first wrote `Cache-Control: no-store`, which the real JSON responses don't send. The samples now match the scenario data.
4. **Screenshot fixes:**
   - The hero's log lines ran behind the subtitle; there are now two columns flanking the headline, at lower opacity.
   - Trace nodes overlapped the section eyebrows.
   - Highlighted log-search lines were cut off.
   - A content type broke mid-word.

## Deviations from the approved design
- **Stylesheet:** plain, `lp-`-prefixed CSS instead of a CSS Module (problem 1 above).
- **Trace line:** centred segments between sections, ending at the closing CTA, rather than one continuous line. This works the same way on mobile.
- **Log field:**
  - two columns rather than three;
  - not paused when off screen, since it only moves with `transform`, which is cheap.
- **Header:** no active-section underline, so the header stays server-rendered.
- **Keyboard tile:** the keycaps light up on hover only, not from real key presses. You asked for minimal client code outside the replay and the two live tiles.
- **Code explorer tile:** dropped, since the replay already shows the explorer and editor. Also skipped: the optional copy-case-ID button.
- **Network tile:** uses toggle buttons rather than a listbox.
- **Paused mid-chapter:** if you scroll past mid-chapter, the frame stays partly drawn until you come back. On the Read chapter that can look half-typed.

## Files
- **New:**
  - `src/components/landing/`: `landing.css`, `styles.ts`, `LogField`, `Reveal`, `Spotlight`, `TraceConnector`, `ReplaySection`, `MethodWaterfall`, `CaseFile`, `ClosingCta`.
  - `replay/`: `IncidentReplay`, `ReplayStage`, `ChapterRail`, `useReplay`, `replay-reducer`.
  - `showcase/`: `WorkspaceShowcase`, `ShowcaseTile`, `NetworkTile`, `LogSearchTile`, `StaticTiles`.
  - `src/data/landing-hero.ts`, `tests/ui/replay.test.ts`.
- **Rewritten:** `Hero`, `LandingHeader`, `LandingFooter`, `CtaLink`, `SectionHeading`, `icons`, `src/data/landing.ts`, `src/app/welcome/page.tsx`, `tests/ui/landing.test.ts`.
- **Removed (v1):** `ProductPreview`, `HowItWorks`, `FeatureGrid`, `FeaturedChallenge`.

## Still needs your eyes
- **Firefox and Safari:**
  - Firefox shows the trace, tilt and header in their finished state, with no scroll-linked motion;
  - Safari needs version 26 for the scroll-linked effects.
- **A real screen reader and touch devices.**
- **Taste:** how strong the glow and log field feel, and the replay's pacing of about 4.5 seconds per chapter.

I reviewed BugLab's v2 landing page in the browser. I'm satisfied with the overall page, animations, Incident Replay, and other sections.

However, I want to redesign ONLY the hero section's layout and visual composition.

The current hero has everything centered vertically: incident badge, headline, description, and CTA buttons. It looks too conventional.

New direction: a premium, asymmetric, split-screen hero.

Design requirements:

1. On desktop, place the main headline, incident label, description, and CTA buttons on the LEFT, with strong left-aligned typography and generous spacing.

2. On the RIGHT, create a visually striking, animated debugging visualization using layered terminal, network, or incident-trace panels. It should communicate the process of investigating a failure without revealing Challenge 001's root cause.

3. Preserve the existing moving-log background, dark atmospheric lighting, blue identity, and overall "Signal in the Noise" concept.

4. Add purposeful horizontal and layered transitions:
   - Headline enters subtly from the left.
   - Supporting text and CTAs follow with staggered motion.
   - Right-side debugging panels enter from the right.
   - Log and request entries animate sequentially.
   - One failed request briefly highlights to represent finding a signal.
   - Add a subtle transition connecting the hero to the Incident Replay as the visitor scrolls.

5. The hero should feel cinematic and distinctive, not like a generic two-column SaaS template. Use depth, intentional asymmetry, layered panels, and strong visual hierarchy.

6. On mobile, stack the layout naturally, reduce decorative motion, and keep the primary CTA visible without excessive scrolling.

7. Respect prefers-reduced-motion, maintain accessibility, and avoid unnecessary JavaScript or dependencies.

8. Keep the existing headline "Every bug leaves a trail." unless a layout adjustment requires different line breaks.

Important constraints:
- Work only on feature/landing-page.
- Modify only hero-related landing components and landing-specific styles/data if necessary.
- Do not change the Incident Replay, other landing sections, workspace, engine, diagnosis API, or shared application files.
- Keep all displayed incident information public and spoiler-free.
- Do not commit or push automatically.

First inspect the existing hero implementation, then implement this focused redesign.

Afterward, run tests, lint, TypeScript checks, and production build. Verify desktop and mobile layouts, reduced-motion behavior, and confidentiality.

Wait for my browser review before committing.
</pasted_content id="4081">

**Right: the debugging scene.** Three overlapping panels, tilted slightly in 3D and bleeding past the content edge from 1280px up. They play once on load, in about 4.5 seconds:

| Layer | Content | Sequence |
|---|---|---|
| Back | Request waterfall | Bars grow one request at a time |
| Middle | `tail -f logs/app.log` | Lines arrive one by one; a scan beam sweeps down; `GET /api/profile 401` flares red, then settles into a blue marker, and "● Signal" appears |
| Front | The failed request | Slides in last, with status, type, duration and "+369 ms after login", then "✓ Pinned as evidence" |

All scene content is the public symptom only. The "+369 ms" is worked out from the public log timestamps: login at `.118`, profile at `.487`.

**Scroll bridge to the replay**
- As the hero scrolls away, the three layers drift up at different speeds for depth.
- The horizon widens and brightens, and a trace segment draws down from the hero toward the Incident Replay heading.
- This needs a browser with scroll-driven animations (Chrome, Edge, Safari 26). Firefox shows the finished, static state.

**Background:** the drifting log field stays, now in three columns with a mask that keeps the left-hand copy clear. The aurora and grain are unchanged.

**Mobile:** text first, with both CTAs above the fold, then a single terminal panel; the waterfall, request card, tilt and slide-ins are off. The log lines still arrive in sequence there; that's the only decorative motion left.

## 2. Key Technical Concepts

**Stack:** Next.js 16.4.3 (App Router, Turbopack, `cacheComponents: true`, `partialPrefetching: true`), React 19.3, TypeScript strict, and Tailwind v4.3.3 with CSS-first `@theme inline` tokens. Tailwind features used: the `starting:` variant (`@starting-style`), `wrap-anywhere`, `bg-size-[…]`, and arbitrary values.

**Critical project quirk:** `next.config.ts` has a Turbopack rule, `"*.css": { loaders: ["@tailwindcss/turbopack"], as: "*.css" }`. It treats every `.css` file, including `*.module.css`, as global CSS, so CSS Module class names resolve to `undefined`.
- Solution: a plain `src/components/landing/landing.css` with every class and keyframe prefixed `lp-`.
- `src/components/landing/styles.ts` maps camelCase names to the `lp-` class names.
- The stylesheet is imported only from `src/app/welcome/page.tsx`. It becomes its own CSS chunk, loaded only on `/welcome`.

**Tests:** Node's built-in `node:test` runner via `tests/setup/register.mjs`. Run with `npm test`, which uses `"node --import ./tests/setup/register.mjs --test \"tests/**/*.test.ts\""`.

**Design tokens** (`globals.css`, dark only):
- `--background #0b0e14`, `--foreground #d6dce6`
- surfaces: `--surface`, `--surface-raised`, `--surface-sunken`
- borders: `--border`, `--border-strong`
- text: `--muted #8a94a6`, `--subtle #7d8799`
- `--accent #6ea8fe`, plus `--success`, `--warning`, `--danger`, `--info`
- Fonts: Geist and Geist Mono.

**Motion system:**
- Hero entrances use CSS `@starting-style`, so they need no JavaScript.
- Scroll reveals use a client `Reveal` component with a single IntersectionObserver. It hides an element only if it is below the fold at hydration, never with reduced motion, and the content is visible without JS.
- Scroll-driven effects (`animation-timeline: view()/scroll(root)`) apply under `@supports` as progressive enhancement.
- Reduced motion uses `animation-duration: 0s !important` inside `.lp-stage`/`.lp-scene`, so the final frame shows.

**Incident Replay:**
- A pure reducer (`replay-reducer.ts`) with statuses `idle|playing|paused|done`, a `suspended` flag, `midChapter` and `run`.
- `stageMode()` returns `static|running|frozen`.
- The `useReplay` hook autoplays once at ≥50% visible, suspends when off screen or when the tab is hidden, never autoplays with reduced motion, and keeps the remaining time across pauses.
- Chapters are ARIA tabs with automatic activation.

**Browser verification without dependencies:** headless Chrome at `C:/Program Files/Google/Chrome/Application/chrome.exe` driven over CDP using Node 24's built-in WebSocket, with scripts in the scratchpad. The production server runs as `npx next start -p 3123` in the background. It is stopped via PowerShell with `Get-NetTCPConnection -LocalPort 3123` → `Stop-Process`. "Failed exit code 127" notifications are just stopped servers.

**Paths:**
- Scratchpad: `C:\Users\LAPTOP~1\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\C--Users-Laptop-Zone-by-JK-buglab\6a0a38fb-42bd-4091-8443-e500d4f86a7d\scratchpad`.
- Bundle-size script: `jssize.js` (usage `node jssize.js .next/server/app/welcome.html [-v]`).
- Browser scripts: `cdp-check.mjs` (full page), `enter-check.mjs`, `hero-check.mjs`.
- Screenshots: `scratchpad\browser\shots\` and `scratchpad\browser\hero\`.

## 3. Files and Code Sections

All landing files below are new or uncommitted on `feature/landing-page`.

### `src/app/welcome/page.tsx`
The route. It imports `"@/components/landing/landing.css"` and `{ styles }`.
- Metadata: `title: "BugLab · Every bug leaves a trail"`, `description: hero.subtitle`.
- It wraps everything in `<div className={`${styles.root} flex flex-1 flex-col`}>`.
- Order inside: LandingHeader; `<main id={SECTION_IDS.content} tabIndex={-1}>` containing Hero, ReplaySection, TraceConnector, MethodWaterfall, TraceConnector, WorkspaceShowcase, TraceConnector, CaseFile, TraceConnector, ClosingCta; then LandingFooter.

### `src/data/landing.ts`
Imported by client components, so it must not import the full scenario logs.
- **Routes and sections:** `ROUTES = { welcome: "/welcome", workspace: "/" }`. `SECTION_IDS` = content, replay, method, workspace, caseFile.
- **CTAs:** `primaryCta` ("Start investigating"), `openWorkspaceCta`, `caseCta` ("Open the case") and `replayCta` ("Finish the investigation"), all pointing at `ROUTES.workspace`. `navLinks` covers Replay, Method, Workspace and Case file.
- **`hero`:**
  - `ticker`: `INC-001 · SEV-2 · opened 09:16 UTC`
  - `title`: "Every bug leaves a trail."
  - `subtitle`, and `secondaryCta` (`#replay`, "Watch an investigation")
  - `proofPoints: ["Production-style logs", "Full network traffic", "Server-checked diagnosis"]` — added in the hero task
- **Replay:** `replayChapters` (read, search, inspect, collect, diagnose, each with `durationMs` 4200–5000) and the `replay` script. The script includes `note` as a 2-line array and `evidence`.
- **Sections:** `methodSteps`; `sampleRequests`, whose harmless headers are taken from the real scenario: `Content-Type: application/json; charset=utf-8`, `X-Powered-By: Express`, `Connection: keep-alive`, and for the HTML page `text/html; charset=UTF-8` and `Cache-Control: public, max-age=0`.
- **Showcase:** `defaultSampleRequestId`, `sampleLogLines`, `sampleNotes`, `showcase` tiles (network, logs, notes, server, keyboard).
- **Case file and closing:** `featuredChallenge` (narrowed public fields; no `status`), `difficultyLabels`, `caseFile` (public `INCIDENT.md` timeline), `closing`.

### `src/data/landing-hero.ts` (server-only use)
```ts
import { incidentLogs } from "@/data/challenge-001/logs";
export const heroLogLines = incidentLogs.map((entry) => `${entry.timestamp} ${entry.level.padEnd(5)} [${entry.source}] ${entry.message}`);
export const heroScene = {
  trace: { title: "Requests · 09:14:02", rows: [
    { method: "POST", path: "/api/auth/login", status: 200, start: 0, width: 40 },
    { method: "GET", path: "/dashboard.html", status: 200, start: 44, width: 6 },
    { method: "GET", path: "/dashboard.js", status: 200, start: 56, width: 5 },
    { method: "GET", path: "/api/profile", status: 401, start: 74, width: 4, signal: true },
    { method: "GET", path: "/api/orders", status: 401, start: 75, width: 4 } ] },
  terminal: { title: "tail -f logs/app.log", lines: [ /* 7 lines 09:14:02.117–.512, profile 401 line has signal: true */ ] },
  inspector: { method: "GET", path: "/api/profile", status: "401 Unauthorized",
    rows: [["Type","fetch"],["Duration","3 ms"],["After login","+369 ms"]], pinned: "Pinned as evidence" },
};
```

### `src/components/landing/HeroScene.tsx` (new, hero task)
A server component, `aria-hidden`.
- **Timeline `T`:** tracePanel 250, terminalPanel 420, bars 700, lines 820, scan 1950, signal 3000, inspectorPanel 3150, inspectorRows 3600, pinned 4300.
- **`enterRight`:** ``"motion-safe:sm:transition-[opacity,translate] motion-safe:sm:duration-1000 ... motion-safe:sm:starting:translate-x-12 motion-safe:sm:starting:opacity-0"``.
- **`Layer({depth, enterAt, className})`:** the outer element gets `styles.depth` with `--depth` (scroll parallax); the inner element gets the panel styling, `enterRight` and a `transitionDelay`.
- **Container:** `${styles.scene} font-mono text-[11px] leading-5 sm:h-[31rem] lg:h-[33rem] lg:[transform:perspective(1800px)_rotateY(-9deg)_rotateX(2deg)] xl:-mr-20`.
- **Back layer** (trace waterfall; `hidden sm:block absolute right-0 top-0 w-[80%]`): bars use `styles.grow` with staggered delays; the signal row gets a `styles.signal` overlay.
- **Middle layer** (terminal; `relative w-full sm:absolute sm:left-0 sm:top-[39%] sm:w-[86%]`):
  - log lines use `styles.appear`, delayed at 820 + i·170;
  - a `styles.scan` beam;
  - a signal overlay on the profile 401 line;
  - a "● Signal" label;
  - a caret.
- **Front layer** (inspector; `hidden sm:block absolute bottom-0 right-[3%] w-[54%]`): rows appear, then the chip "✓ Pinned as evidence".

### `src/components/landing/landing.css`
`lp-` namespaced. Sections:
- **Base:** `@property --lp-cta-angle`; `.lp-root` variables (`--ease`, `--fast`, `--medium`, `--slow`).
- **Hero background:**
  - `.lp-headline` (gradient text with a forced-colors fallback), `.lp-aurora` (breathe), `.lp-grain`, `.lp-horizon`;

  - `.lp-log-track` (drift), `.lp-flare`.
- **Reveals and chrome:**
  - `.lp-reveal[data-reveal=pending]`, `.lp-span`;
  - `.lp-header` (static background, plus a scroll-linked `lp-firm`);
  - the trace: `.lp-trace`, `.lp-trace-pulse`, `.lp-trace-node`;
  - `@supports (animation-timeline: view())` blocks for trace draw, tilt settle and the header;
  - `.lp-cta` / `-primary` (sheen) / `-secondary` (orbiting conic border), `.lp-arrow`, `.lp-link`, `.lp-spot` (pointer glow), `.lp-key`, `.lp-packet`.
- **Replay:** `.lp-stage[data-paused]` and `[data-static]` rules; `.lp-typed`, `.lp-appear`, `.lp-vanish`, `.lp-dim`, `.lp-slide-in`, `.lp-press`, `.lp-rise`, `.lp-fill`, `.lp-caret`.

  - `.lp-grow` (scaleX)
  - `.lp-scan` (beam)
  - `.lp-signal` (blue marker) with an `::after` red flare (`lp-signal`, `lp-signal-flare`)
  - `@supports (animation-timeline: scroll())`:
    - `.lp-depth`: `animation-range: 0 80vh`; keyframe `transform: translate3d(0, calc(var(--depth,1) * -28px), 0); opacity: calc(1 - var(--depth,1) * 0.12)`
    - `.lp-horizon`: `lp-horizon`, scaleX .55→1 and opacity .35→.8 over 0–60vh
- **Last block, `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)`:** disables the infinite and scroll animations (now including `.lp-depth` and `.lp-horizon`); `.lp-scan { display: none }`; `.lp-scene *`, `.lp-stage *` and their `::after` get `animation-duration: 0s !important; animation-delay: 0s !important`.

## 4. Errors and fixes

- **CSS Module classes resolved to `undefined`** because of the Turbopack global-CSS rule. Fixed by converting to `landing.css` with `lp-` prefixes plus `styles.ts`, and adding a coverage test. That test was mutation-checked: it was first too weak, because the reduced-motion block still mentioned the class, so it now inspects only the part of the file before that block.
- **A conversion script mangled the CSS** (regex backslashes were lost, so `.` matched any character) and had already deleted the module file. I rewrote `landing.css` by hand.
- **The client chunk included all incident logs** through `landing.ts` → `incidentLogs`. Fixed by moving them to `landing-hero.ts`.
- **v1 `featuredChallenge` re-exported the hard-coded `status`**, which a test caught. It now exports narrowed fields only.
- **Sample headers were inaccurate** (`Cache-Control: no-store`). They are now aligned with the `network.ts` data.
- **The `color-mix` glow fallback** rendered solid blue in v1; replaced with the accent colour plus `opacity-20`.
- **The word "progress"** in a comment tripped the claims test; I reworded the comment.
- **The hero log field overlapped the subtitle** (v2); fixed with flanking columns and lower opacity. Trace nodes overlapped the eyebrows; fixed by adding `mb-7`. Log-search highlights were clipped, and a content type broke mid-word; fixed with `wrap-anywhere` and `wrap-break-word`.
- **CDP Enter test false negative:** the key event needed `text: "\r"`. Re-verified: Enter and Space toggle.
- **Hero redesign:**
  - the terminal covered the waterfall's 401 rows, so I moved it to `top-[39%]` and increased the heights to `sm:h-[31rem] lg:h-[33rem]`;
  - the headline was enlarged on lg;
  - `lg:-mr-8` clipped the scene at 1024px, so it was removed and only `xl:-mr-20` remains;
  - the claim "Real logs" overstated a fictional scenario and became "Production-style logs".
- **Earlier workspace-era fixes** (summarised): a typo, a shadowed prop, the TLS install being stopped as instructed, and `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV` for `git show`.

Now I want the remaining landing-page sections to reach the same level of visual creativity and polish.

The current sections work well, but the overall page still feels too much like a conventional vertical SaaS layout. I want a more immersive, editorial, cinematic experience where scrolling feels like progressing through an investigation.

Do not modify code yet.

Propose a v3 design plan for ONLY these sections:

1. Incident Replay
2. The Method
3. Workspace Features / Instruments
4. Case File
5. Closing CTA and transitions between sections

Creative direction:
- Break away from repetitive vertically stacked sections and conventional card grids.
- Explore one purposeful horizontal or pinned-scroll storytelling experience, preferably around Incident Replay.
- Use asymmetric compositions, overlapping panels, visual depth, staggered content, and connected transitions.
- Make each section visually distinct while preserving the "Signal in the Noise" identity.
- Create a sense of progression through an investigation rather than separate marketing blocks.
- Preserve the existing interactive replay, log search, network inspector, and functional CTAs.
- Use the new split hero as the quality benchmark.

Important:
- Do not use horizontal scroll hijacking or create confusing navigation.
- Ensure keyboard, touch, mobile, and reduced-motion experiences remain accessible.
- Prefer CSS-based effects and progressive enhancement over heavy JavaScript.
- Keep the existing dark theme, typography, and blue identity.
- Do not reveal Challenge 001's root cause.
- Do not touch workspace, engine, diagnosis, or shared application files.
- Do not introduce dependencies.
- Keep all changes isolated to feature/landing-page.

1. A section-by-section visual concept.

5. Interaction and accessibility considerations.

# BugLab landing v3 proposal: "The Investigation"

I haven't changed any code. This plan is based on the current section components (`ReplaySection`, `IncidentReplay`, `MethodWaterfall`, `WorkspaceShowcase`, `CaseFile` and `ClosingCta`).

**The idea in one line:** the page is one incident, from report to resolution. One trace line runs down the page from the hero. Each section is a phase of the investigation with its own layout, and the joins between sections read like log lines that move the incident clock forward.

| Phase | Section | Layout | Main motion |
|---|---|---|---|
| Report | Hero (unchanged) | Split screen | Entrances |
| Reproduce | Incident Replay | Pinned stage, scrolling chapter text | Scroll moves through the chapters |
| Reason | The Method | One full-width trace across the page | A time cursor moves across as you scroll down |
| Instrument | Workspace | Workspace frame that opens into labelled panels | Panels separate, then settle flat |
| Commit | Case file | Overlapping dossier | The dossier slides over the previous section and lands |
| Resolve | Closing | Calm log field around one signal | The noise settles |

---

## 1. Section-by-section concepts

### Incident Replay: "The Investigation Room" (the pinned centrepiece)
- **Desktop (lg and up):** two columns.
  - **Left:** five tall chapter blocks in normal document flow, each about 70vh. Each block has a large `01/05` index, the chapter title and its caption, styled like a book chapter.
  - **Right:** the replay window sticks to the viewport (`position: sticky`). The chapter whose block sits in the middle of the screen plays in it.
  - The trace line from the hero's bottom connector becomes the progress line beside the chapter blocks. It fills as you read.
  - **Scrolling is never taken over.** You scroll normally, and the sticky window simply stays put for about 3.5 screens.
- The chapter tabs become a small chapter index of in-page links. Clicking one scrolls to that chapter's block; it never jumps the stage by itself.
- The "Finish the investigation" CTA stays in the diagnose chapter's block.
- A "Skip replay" link at the start goes straight to the Method.

### The Method: "One trace, read left to right"
- The waterfall stops being a boxed table. It becomes one full-width trace across the viewport.
- The five steps are a staircase of glowing bars on a shared time axis. Their annotations hang alternately above and below the axis, laid out like an editorial spread with large `+0:00` offsets.
- **Horizontal story without horizontal scrolling:** as you scroll down through the section, a vertical time cursor moves left to right along the axis. Each bar lights up as the cursor reaches it. This is pure CSS, using `animation-timeline: view()` with a different start point for each bar.
- The heading sits at the start of the axis instead of centred above it.

### Workspace: "The Instrument Bench"
- This replaces the grid of five cards.
- It opens as one assembled workspace frame, tilted slightly, recognisably the real BugLab layout. As it scrolls into view, the frame comes apart into layers:
  - the **network inspector** and **log search** become two large, overlapping, fully interactive panels;
  - **notes**, **server check** and **keyboard** stop being cards. They become small callouts with thin connecting lines pointing at the parts of the frame they describe.
- **Key rule:** motion happens only on the way in. In the final position the panels are flat (`transform: none`). Hovering or focusing inside a panel stops all motion, so you never type into something that is moving.

### Case file: "The dossier lands"
- The dossier slides up and slightly over the end of the workspace section (negative margin plus stacking order). It lands with a small tilt that settles, like a folder dropped on a desk.
- **Layers:**
  - the dossier card;
  - a clipped "evidence strip" showing the public 401 symptom, the same one the hero shows;
  - the timeline as a vertical strip of tape on the right, with times stamped in one after another as you scroll.
- The heading "Your first incident is open." becomes the dossier's stamp, not a separate centred heading.
- The last timeline point hands the trace on to the closing section.
- **Content:** only the public fields and timeline used today.

### Closing CTA: "Signal, resolved"
- Mirrors the hero. The drifting log field comes back, but calm: fewer lines, dimmed, no flares.
- One line stays bright where the trace ends. The trace ends in a point that becomes the CTA's existing orbiting border.
- As the section enters, the noise settles (drift slows and fades) using a scroll-linked animation.
- **The message:** you came in with noise and leave with a signal.

### Transitions between sections
The four `TraceConnector`s become **timestamped log lines on the trace**, for example `09:16 INFO replay → method`. Each one advances an incident clock. The wording is generic: it never hints at a cause and never names a diagnosis option.

Section headings are no longer all the same centred block. Each phase places its heading in its own layout:
- replay: in the left column;
- method: at the start of the axis;
- workspace: a callout on the frame;
- case file: the stamp.

## 2. How visitors move through the page
1. **Hero:** they get the report.
2. **Replay:** the trace carries them in, and the window pins. They read five short chapters at their own scroll speed while each chapter plays.
3. **Method:** the window releases. The trace widens into the full-width axis, and scrolling down sweeps the cursor along it.
4. **Workspace:** the axis line meets the workspace frame and the frame opens. They can stop here and try the inspector and log search.
5. **Case file:** the dossier slides over the workspace.
6. **Closing:** the last timeline point drops the trace into the calm field and the CTA.

Every section can be reached from the header nav or by normal scrolling, and the page never moves sideways.

## 3. Specific effects
**Pinned**
- The replay window uses `position: sticky` (CSS).
- A small hook tracks which chapter block is active using one IntersectionObserver, the same pattern as `Reveal`.

**Horizontal**
- The Method's time cursor and bar lighting are driven by vertical scroll.
- Only the visuals move sideways; the page doesn't.

**Scroll-linked (CSS `view()` and `scroll()`, inside `@supports`)**
- the replay progress line filling;
- the method cursor;
- the workspace panels separating and settling;
- the dossier landing;
- timeline stamps;
- the closing field settling;
- the connector lines drawing in.

All of these animate only `transform` and `opacity`.

**Layered**
- the workspace panels and callouts at three depths;
- the dossier over the end of the workspace;
- the evidence strip clipped over the dossier;
- the replay window above the faded chapter text.

## 4. Desktop and mobile
| | Desktop (lg and up) | Mobile and tablet (below lg) |
|---|---|---|
| Replay | Pinned window, scroll-driven chapters | Today's tabbed player (autoplay once, pause, chapter tabs), restyled |
| Method | Horizontal axis with the moving cursor | Time runs downward; the cursor becomes a line that fills |
| Workspace | Opening frame, overlapping panels, callouts | Stacked flat panels with labels; no opening effect |
| Case file | Overlap, tilt, tape timeline beside the dossier | Single column, timeline below, no overlap |
| Closing | Calm field, trace into the CTA | Same, one column of logs |

## 5. Interaction and accessibility
**Replay on desktop**
- The chapter blocks are real headings and paragraphs in reading order, which is better for screen readers than tabs. The window stays `aria-hidden`.
- The chapter index is a `nav` with in-page links. Keyboard users can tab to "Skip replay".
- Each chapter's animation lasts under 5 seconds and then stops. I'd still keep a Pause control.
- There's no autoplay: scrolling advances the chapters.

**Without JavaScript**
- The sticky layout needs JS to switch chapters. A tiny `<noscript><style>` turns pinning off.
- Each chapter block then shows a static final frame of its own chapter. No layout shift for anyone who has JS.

**Reduced motion**
- The pinned layout stays, because pinning isn't motion.
- Chapters switch instantly to their final frames.
- All scroll-linked effects show their settled state: method bars lit, panels flat, dossier landed, field still.

**Other**
- **Interactive panels:** flat and still while hovered or focused. Touch targets stay at least 24px. The existing keyboard behaviour of the network and log tools is kept.
- **Browsers without scroll-driven animations:** they get the settled state (Firefox today).

## 6. Complexity and risks
| Item | Complexity | Risk and mitigation |
|---|---|---|
| Pinned replay | **High** | A sticky element fails if any ancestor has `overflow: hidden`. I'll check the `/welcome` wrappers; the hero's `overflow-hidden` doesn't contain the replay. There are now two replay modes (scroll on desktop, tabs on mobile); the reducer stays shared, with new tests. The section gets about 3.5 screens longer; offset by "Skip replay" and tighter blocks. Anchor jumps to `#replay` land on chapter 1. |
| Method cursor | Medium | Fine-tuning the start point for each bar. Pure CSS. |
| Workspace bench | **High** | Interactive panels inside transforms, and the callout lines at different widths. The panels settle with no transform, and the lines are hidden below lg. |
| Case file overlap | Medium | Stacking-order and spacing interplay with the workspace above. |
| Closing field | Low | Reuses the log-field pattern. |
| JS cost | Low | One observer hook and the noscript styles. I'll measure the bundle rather than estimate. |
| Performance | Medium | About 15–25 scroll-linked animations, all compositor-only. I'll profile in headless Chrome. |

## 7. Files that would change
**Change**
- **`ReplaySection.tsx`:** rewritten as two columns plus the sticky stage.
- **`replay/IncidentReplay.tsx`:** adds the desktop scroll mode.
- **`replay/ChapterRail.tsx`:** becomes the chapter-index variant on desktop.
- **`replay/useReplay.ts` and `replay-reducer.ts`:** chapter selection from scroll, no auto-advance in scroll mode.
- **`MethodWaterfall.tsx`:** rewritten.
- **Workspace, `showcase/WorkspaceShowcase.tsx`:** rewritten as the bench.
- **Workspace, `showcase/ShowcaseTile.tsx`:** panel variant.
- **Workspace, `showcase/StaticTiles.tsx`:** becomes the callouts.
- **Workspace, `NetworkTile.tsx` and `LogSearchTile.tsx`:** wrapper classes only; behaviour unchanged.
- **`CaseFile.tsx`, `ClosingCta.tsx`**
- **`TraceConnector.tsx`:** adds the log-line labels.
- **`SectionHeading.tsx`:** adds placement variants.
- **`LogField.tsx`:** an optional `calm` prop; the default output stays the same, so the hero is unchanged.
- **`welcome/page.tsx`:** connector props only.
- **`landing.css`, `styles.ts`**
- **`src/data/landing.ts`:** connector log-line text and chapter indexes.
- **`tests/ui/landing.test.ts`, `tests/ui/replay.test.ts`:** new reducer tests and the connector copy in the spoiler scan.

**New**
- `replay/ReplayChapters.tsx`: the scrolling chapter blocks.
- `replay/useActiveChapter.ts`: the observer.
- `showcase/InstrumentCallout.tsx`

**Not touched:** `Hero.tsx`, `HeroScene.tsx`, the header, the footer, and everything outside the landing files and `/welcome`. No new dependencies.

## Decisions I need from you
1. **Desktop replay:** should scroll drive the chapters with no autoplay (recommended), or should the window pin with today's autoplay running inside it?
2. **Order of work:** build the pinned replay first as its own batch for your browser review, then the other four (recommended, since it carries most of the risk), or all at once?
3. **No-JavaScript fallback:** is the small `<noscript><style>` acceptable? It's the cleanest way to have no layout shift with JS and a correct static page without it.

I'll wait for your approval before writing any code.
