I've reviewed the BugLab v3 "The Investigation" proposal and approve the overall visual direction.

Let's implement it incrementally, starting ONLY with the Incident Replay redesign.

Decisions:

1. On desktop (lg and above), use the proposed two-column layout:
   - Left: five editorial chapter blocks in normal document flow.
   - Right: a sticky investigation workspace that advances as the visitor scrolls through the chapters.
   - Preserve manual chapter navigation so visitors can revisit chapters without losing keyboard accessibility.
   - Keep native scrolling; no scroll hijacking, forced snapping, or horizontal page movement.

2. On mobile and tablet, preserve the existing tabbed replay with its current autoplay, pause, replay, and keyboard behavior.

3. Keep the desktop experience compact. Avoid unnecessarily extending the page by 3.5+ viewports if shorter chapter blocks provide a better experience.

4. Use a small, page-scoped no-JavaScript fallback if necessary, but verify that all five chapter descriptions and a meaningful static workspace frame remain available without JavaScript. Avoid layout shifts.

5. Respect prefers-reduced-motion. Desktop chapter changes should be instantaneous in reduced-motion mode.

6. Preserve the existing spoiler-free replay content, captions, functional CTA, and public data boundaries.

7. Do not change the new split-screen hero or any other landing-page section yet.

8. Work only on feature/landing-page. Do not touch workspace, engine, diagnosis API, shared files, or package dependencies.

After implementation:
- Run tests, lint, TypeScript, and production build.
- Verify scroll-driven chapter selection, manual navigation, keyboard behavior, mobile autoplay, reduced motion, and no-JavaScript fallback.
- Check for sideways overflow at mobile, tablet, and desktop widths.
- Run confidentiality checks.
- Report any performance or bundle-size changes.

Do not commit or push. Wait for my browser review before proceeding to the Method, Workspace, Case File, or Closing sections.
</pasted_content id="4081">

The Incident Replay is now two columns on desktop: chapter text on the left, a pinned replay window on the right that follows your scrolling. Tests, lint, TypeScript and the production build pass, and `/welcome` is still prerendered as static. Nothing is committed.

## How it behaves
- **Desktop (1024px and wider):** the five chapter blocks scroll normally on the left. The window stays pinned near the vertical middle of the screen and plays whichever chapter is in the middle. There is no autoplay, no snapping and no sideways movement; your scrolling is the only clock.
  - The line beside the chapters fills up to the current chapter.
  - Chapters you're not on are shown in dimmer colours, still at readable contrast.
  - On screens shorter than 640px the window doesn't pin.
- **Chapter navigation:** the row of chapter links above the window are plain in-page links. Pressing one jumps to that chapter and moves keyboard focus to it, and the next Tab goes on from there. Clicking the chapter already showing replays it.
- **Mobile and tablet:** the existing tabbed replay, with its autoplay, pause, replay and keyboard behaviour unchanged. The desktop and mobile versions are switched in CSS, so nothing jumps when the page loads.
- **Length:** the whole section is about 2.3 screen heights at 1440×900, heading included. The window stays pinned for about 1.4 of those.
- **Without JavaScript:** every width shows the desktop markup, unpinned and undimmed. All five chapters and the "Finish the investigation" button are visible, next to a still window showing the incident report and logs (on mobile, the incident file only). This uses a three-rule `<noscript>` style scoped to this section.
- **Reduced motion:** chapters switch straight to their final frame, with no colour fades and no animation of the fill line.

## Browser checks (headless Chrome, production build)
| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Scroll selects each chapter in turn, both downwards and back up | ✓ |
| Window pinned at the same spot through chapters 1–4 | ✓ at 1440, 1280 and 1024 |
| Keyboard: Enter on "Collect" jumps there, focuses it, and the next Tab reaches the next button | ✓ |
| Autoplay on mobile (390) and tablet (768), with Pause showing | ✓ |
| Reduced motion: final frames, no transitions | ✓ |
| Without JavaScript: all 5 descriptions, the window and the button visible | ✓ at 1440 and 390 |
| Sideways overflow at 390, 768, 1024, 1280 and 1440 | 0 px |
| Layout shift (CLS) | 0 |
| Console errors | 0 |

## Confidentiality
The page shows no diagnosis option IDs or labels and no root-cause words. The replay content and data limits are unchanged, and the diagnose frame still shows only blurred outlines in place of the options.

## Size changes
| | Before | Now |
|---|---|---|
| JavaScript (gzipped) | 182.8 KB | 184.0 KB (+1.2 KB) |
| Landing CSS (gzipped) | 3.0 KB | 3.2 KB |
| HTML (gzipped) | 23.2 KB | 24.8 KB, because both replay layouts are in the page |

## Where I departed from the plan
- **Heading:** still centred above the replay, so the trace from the hero still lands on it.
- **Heading description:** now ends "Follow along, or jump to any chapter." instead of "Pick any chapter, or let it play.", so it fits both versions.
- **Desktop button:** "Finish the investigation" now sits in the Diagnose chapter.
- **No "Skip replay" link:** the desktop version only has six things you can Tab to.
- **Last chapter:** the window starts scrolling away about 10px before chapter 5 reaches the middle. It's barely noticeable.

## Files
- **New:** `replay/ScrollReplay.tsx`, `replay/useActiveChapter.ts`, `replay/ReplayWindow.tsx` (the window frame both versions share).
- **Changed:** `ReplaySection.tsx`, `replay/IncidentReplay.tsx` (now uses the shared window, behaviour identical), `replay/ReplayStage.tsx` (a desktop layout option; the default is unchanged), `landing.css`, `styles.ts`, `tests/ui/replay.test.ts` (4 new tests for chapter selection).

The hero, the other sections and everything outside the landing files are untouched.

**Worth checking by hand:** how the pinning feels with a trackpad and a mouse wheel; scrolling quickly through all five chapters, which restarts each chapter's animation; and the size of the gaps between chapters on your screen.

I'll wait for your browser review before starting on the Method section. When you commit, include `.claude-logs/`.

Let's continue BugLab v3 "The Investigation" with Stage 2.

The new split-screen hero and pinned Incident Replay are implemented. Do not modify them.

Now redesign ONLY:

1. The Method section
2. The Workspace Features / Instrument Bench section
3. Their landing-page-specific visual transition

THE METHOD — Horizontal Investigation Timeline

- Replace the conventional waterfall/card layout with a full-width horizontal investigation trace.
- Arrange the five steps along a timeline with staggered explanations above and below the axis.
- Introduce a subtle scroll-driven cursor that moves horizontally as visitors scroll vertically.
- Illuminate each stage as the cursor reaches it.
- Use large timestamps, thin connecting lines, elegant typography, and restrained blue highlights.
- Keep the layout asymmetric and editorial rather than a conventional row of cards.
- On mobile, transform the horizontal timeline into a clean vertical investigation path.
- Preserve the existing content and meaning.

WORKSPACE — Interactive Instrument Bench

- Replace the conventional feature-card grid with a visually connected debugging workspace.
- Make the log search and network inspector the two main interactive instruments.
- Integrate notes, server-checked diagnosis, and keyboard features as smaller connected callouts.
- Use overlapping panels, subtle depth, and an intentional asymmetric composition.
- As the section enters the viewport, the workspace should appear to unfold or separate into instruments.
- Panels must settle into stable, flat positions before interaction.
- Preserve the existing log-search and network-inspector functionality.
- Maintain keyboard navigation, focus visibility, and accessible labels.
- Avoid hover-only functionality.

TRANSITIONS

- Visually connect the Method timeline to the Workspace section.
- Use horizontal movement, staggered reveals, and scroll-linked motion where appropriate.
- Keep animations purposeful and performant.
- Provide static fallbacks for browsers without scroll-driven animation support.
- Respect prefers-reduced-motion.

IMPORTANT CONSTRAINTS

- Work only on feature/landing-page.
- Do not modify the hero, Incident Replay, Case File, Closing CTA, workspace application, challenge engine, diagnosis API, or shared application files.
- Do not introduce new dependencies.
- Do not reveal Challenge 001's root cause or answer-key details.
- Preserve all functional CTAs.
- Keep responsive behavior polished at 390, 768, 1024, 1280, and 1440px.
- Avoid horizontal page overflow and scroll hijacking.
- Do not commit or push.

After implementation, run tests, lint, TypeScript checks, production build, confidentiality checks, and browser verification.

Report changed files, any behavior changes, performance impact, and anything requiring manual review.

Wait for my browser approval before proceeding to Stage 3.

Stage 2 is in place: the Method is now a horizontal timeline, the workspace section is an instrument bench, and the two are joined by a trace that turns down into the workspace. Tests (100), lint, TypeScript and the production build pass, `/welcome` is still prerendered as static, and nothing is committed.

## The Method
- **Desktop (1024px and wider):** the five steps sit on one full-width time axis. Their explanations alternate above and below it on stems of different heights, with large `+02:40`-style timestamps. The heading is off-centre: title on the left, description on the right.
- **The cursor:** as you scroll down, a cursor moves left to right along the axis. Each span draws itself as the cursor passes, and each step brightens when the cursor reaches it. The sweep runs while the timeline passes through the middle of the screen. It's pure CSS, with no JavaScript.
- **Below 1024px:** the same list becomes a vertical path with a dot per step and a small time bar under each one. The path fills, and each step lights up as it scrolls in.
- **Fallbacks:** browsers without scroll-driven animation (Firefox today), reduced motion and no JavaScript all get every step fully lit and no cursor.

## The transition into the workspace
At the right-hand end of the axis the trace turns down, with an "→ into the workspace" label, and lands on the workspace heading, which is right-aligned to meet it. Below 1024px it continues the vertical path down the left instead.

## The instrument bench
- **Desktop composition:**
  - The network inspector lifts out of a faint workspace window on the left.
  - Log search sits lower on the right and overhangs the window's edge.
  - Notes, "Checked on the server" and "Keyboard first" are smaller callouts hanging from the instruments on thin connector lines.
- **Unfolding:** when the bench scrolls into view, the pieces start folded together and move to their places over about 1.5 seconds. They then stay flat: verified at every width, with no transform left on any piece and no panels overlapping each other.
- **Interaction:** the network inspector and log search work as before. Checked by mouse and keyboard: selecting a request updates its details, typing in the search updates the line count, and Tab then Enter on a suggestion button works with a visible focus ring. Nothing depends on hover.
- **Below 1024px:** a stacked layout in the order network, log search, then the three callouts, with a gentle fade-up. Reduced motion and no JavaScript show it already settled.

## Checks
| | 390 | 768 | 1024 | 1280 | 1440 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Sideways overflow | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| Console errors | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| Bench settled flat | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| Step explanations overlapping | — | — | 0 | 0 | 0 |

- **Confidentiality:** none of the 10 diagnosis option IDs or labels appear in the page HTML or the client JavaScript, and none of the root-cause words appear in the HTML.
- **Replay unchanged:** I re-ran the replay checks. Scroll selection, the keyboard jump, the no-JS fallback and mobile autoplay all pass, with zero layout shift.

## Size changes
| | Before Stage 2 | Now |
|---|---|---|
| JavaScript (gzipped) | 184.0 KB | 184.2 KB |
| Landing CSS (gzipped) | 3.2 KB | 3.5 KB |
| HTML (gzipped) | 24.8 KB | 27.4 KB, from the extra markup for the axis and composition |

The only new client code is the bench's reveal, which reuses the existing reveal component's observer.

## Files
- **New:**
  - `MethodTimeline.tsx` (replaces `MethodWaterfall.tsx`)
  - `showcase/InstrumentBench.tsx` (replaces `WorkspaceShowcase.tsx`)
  - `MethodBridge.tsx` (the transition)
  - `EditorialHeading.tsx` (the off-centre headings)
- **Changed:**

<pasted_content id="4081">
Let's continue BugLab v3 "The Investigation" with Stage 3 — the final landing-page visual redesign.

The split-screen hero, pinned Incident Replay, horizontal Method timeline, and Workspace Instrument Bench are implemented and approved.

Now redesign ONLY:

1. Case File section
2. Closing CTA section
3. The visual transition connecting them

CASE FILE — "The Dossier Lands"

- Replace the conventional challenge card with a cinematic, layered investigation dossier.
- Create an asymmetric editorial composition rather than a centered heading and card.
- Make the dossier feel like a real incident case file, with a prominent incident identifier, status, title, evidence summary, and public timeline.
- Add overlapping layers such as a clipped evidence strip, timestamp markers, and subtle document-depth effects.
- On desktop, let the dossier visually overlap the end of the Workspace Instrument Bench without obscuring interactive elements.
- As visitors scroll, the dossier should slide into place with a subtle tilt that settles.
- Preserve the existing Case File CTA and its navigation.
- Do not reveal the challenge's actual root cause, correct diagnosis, or answer-key details.

CLOSING CTA — "The Final Handoff"

- Replace the current centered "The logs are waiting" composition with a distinctive asymmetric closing experience.
- Use the headline: "The next move is yours."
- On desktop, create a split composition:
  - Left: strong editorial headline, concise supporting text, and prominent Open Workspace CTA.
  - Right: a layered, visually polished incident handoff panel showing only public challenge information.
- Make the closing feel connected to the opening hero without duplicating it.
- Reintroduce the atmospheric log background in a calmer form.
- The investigation trace should visually flow from the Case File into the closing CTA.
- Use subtle horizontal entrances, staggered reveals, and a restrained final CTA highlight.
- Do not label the incident "Resolved" or imply the visitor has completed it.

TRANSITIONS AND RESPONSIVENESS

- Connect the Case File and Closing CTA through a continuous visual trace.
- Use scroll-linked effects where supported, with sensible static fallbacks.
- Respect prefers-reduced-motion.
- On mobile, use clean stacked layouts without overlapping or clipped content.
- Avoid horizontal page overflow and scroll hijacking.
- Preserve keyboard accessibility, focus visibility, and functional navigation.

IMPORTANT CONSTRAINTS

- Work only on feature/landing-page.
- Do not modify the hero, Incident Replay, Method, Workspace Instrument Bench, application workspace, challenge engine, diagnosis API, or shared files.
- Do not introduce new dependencies.
- Preserve the existing dark theme and blue visual identity.
- Keep the page spoiler-free.
- Do not commit or push automatically.

After implementation:
- Run tests, lint, TypeScript, and production build.
- Verify at 390, 768, 1024, 1280, and 1440px.
- Check reduced motion, keyboard accessibility, layout shift, and horizontal overflow.
- Run the confidentiality scan.
- Report changed files, bundle-size impact, and any browser-review concerns.

Wait for my approval before making further changes.
</pasted_content id="4081">

Stage 3 is in place: the Case file is now a layered dossier, the closing is the split "The next move is yours." handoff, and one trace runs from the dossier into the handoff panel. Tests (100), lint, TypeScript and the production build pass, and `/welcome` is still prerendered as static. Nothing is committed.

## Case file: "The dossier lands"
- **Desktop (1024px and wider):** the heading is on the left, with **INC-001** set large in blue, then "Your first incident is open." and one line of copy. The dossier sits on the right, built in layers:
  - a case folder behind it that reaches up behind the end of the workspace section;
  - two tilted sheets underneath;
  - a clipped public log line (`09:14:02.487 GET /api/profile 401 3ms`) over its top edge;
  - the timeline taped over its right edge.
- **Dossier contents:**
  - an **Open** status, SEV-2 and the service name;
  - the title and incident report;
  - a new "Evidence on file" grid with four public symptoms: the dashboard fails to load, login returns 200, `/api/profile` returns 401 right after, and the v2.3.0 deploy at 08:55;
  - difficulty, estimated time, topics, and **Open the case** (still links to `/`).
- **Overlap with the bench:** the dossier itself would have covered the "Keyboard first" callout, because the bench has no empty space at its bottom edge. So the case folder (about 95px of it) rises *underneath* the bench instead. I checked 245 points across the bench panels at every width, and none is covered.
- **Motion:** the dossier slides up with a slight tilt and settles completely flat by the time a third of it is on screen. This is CSS scroll-driven animation; browsers without it, and reduced motion, get it static.

## Closing: "The final handoff"
- **Left:** "05 / Handoff", the headline **"The next move is yours."**, the existing subtitle, and **Open workspace**. The button glows once as the copy arrives.
- **Right:** an incident handoff panel with public details only:
  - the status reads **"Open · awaiting investigator"**;
  - the service, severity, opening time (09:16 UTC) and **Owner: You**;
  - an incident log showing 09:16 opened, 09:20 acknowledged, then "now: Handed over to you".

  Nothing says resolved or implies the visitor has finished; the check for "resolved"/"solved" found nothing.
- **Background:** the hero's drifting logs return, calmer: two columns, about twice as slow, much fainter, with no flashing 401 lines, and kept clear behind the copy.
- **Entrances:** the copy comes in from the left, the panel from the right, and the log lines appear in sequence.

## The trace between them
A line leaves the bottom of the dossier with a "→ handoff" label and lands on the handoff panel's top edge, within 1px horizontally at 1024, 1280 and 1440. On mobile both sections stack, with nothing overlapping or cut off.

## Checks

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Sideways overflow | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| Bench panels covered | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| Console errors | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| Layout shift (CLS) | 0.014 | 0.003 | 0.003 | 0.002 | 0.001 |

- **Layout shift:** all values are well under the 0.1 threshold. I traced the 390px shifts to the existing mobile replay's Play/Pause button changing width, not the new sections.
- **Keyboard:** Tab goes from Open the case to Open workspace (closing) to Open workspace (footer), each with a visible focus outline.
- **Reduced motion:** no slide-in, no reveal, no glow and no drifting logs.
- **Without JavaScript:** the handoff panel and its three log lines are fully visible.
- **Earlier stages:** the Stage 1 replay and Stage 2 bench checks still pass.
- **Confidentiality:** none of the 10 diagnosis option IDs or labels appear in the HTML or JavaScript, and none of the root-cause words appear in the HTML. The spoiler test caught one false alarm: "Assigned" contains the protected word "signed", so that row is now labelled **Owner** rather than loosening the test.

PR #1 is **ready to merge**, at the commit you reported: `8955585` ("fix: handle diagnosis abort compatibility and unexpected errors", by Habin Abdul Rahman). Nothing blocks the merge. There are four follow-ups worth scheduling, two of them before real users arrive.

## How I reviewed it
- I fetched the PR's head ref (`refs/pull/1/head`); it matches `origin/feature/engine-workspace-integration`.
- The PR is based on `main` (`333dab2`) and contains 15 commits, including the clean workspace commit `48fad4a`.
- I reviewed it in a detached worktree at `…\scratchpad\wt-pr1`. No branch was created, nothing was committed, pushed or merged, and `feature/landing-page` and your uncommitted session log are untouched.

## Quality checks
| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Tests | **396/396 pass** (53 suites), matching the reported 396 |
| TypeScript | Clean |
| ESLint | Clean |
| Production build | Passes; `/` is static and the diagnosis API is dynamic |

I first ran the checks with your `node_modules` linked in. The build failed only because Turbopack won't follow that link to a folder outside the worktree. After a `npm ci` from the PR's own lockfile, the build and all 396 tests passed again.

## 1. D1/D2 fixes: verified
- **D1, older browsers:**
  - The check for `AbortSignal.timeout` and `AbortSignal.any` now happens inside the error handling, so a missing API returns a clear "please update your browser" message instead of throwing.
  - No request is ever sent without a timeout.
  - The 15-second timeout, a genuine abort (rethrown) and network errors each still produce their own result.
- **D2, form stuck on "Submitting":**
  - The hook now ignores an error only when its own request was aborted by the page unmounting.
  - Any other error is logged and shown as a recoverable error, so the form unlocks and Retry works.
  - A result that arrives after unmount is dropped.
- **Tests:** 20 new client tests and 16 hook tests cover the missing APIs, the timeout with and without a caller signal, genuine aborts, a duplicate submission while one is in flight, retry and reset, an error that isn't an unmount, and late results after unmount. A stray `AbortError` is correctly treated as a failure.

## 2. Diagnosis integration: verified
- **Server validation and attempt limits are unchanged:** there are no changes to `src/app/api`, `src/server` or the answer key since `b1eb3af`, the version reviewed earlier. The attempt-limiter and handler tests pass.
- **Malformed responses can't mark the challenge resolved:**
  - The new validator checks the full shape of every response type. A body that only says `{ status: "correct" }` is rejected; there's a test for exactly that.
  - Only a fully valid `correct` response marks the challenge solved. Unknown statuses never complete it.
  - Only `incorrect` counts as a submitted diagnosis; `invalid`, `rate-limited` and `error` don't.
- **Learner status is in memory only.** It resets on refresh and gives no score.

## 3. Security
- **Answer key:** none of its structure reaches the browser.
  - The key object, the field holding the correct answer, and the accepted-evidence and key-observation lists are all absent from the shipped files.
  - The correct option ID does appear, but only as one of the five choices, next to the other IDs, with nothing marking it as correct. It's third of five, so its position gives nothing away.
  - The resolution summary is sent only with a `correct` response.
- **Monaco and DOMPurify:**
  - `npm audit` reports 1 critical issue: DOMPurify 3.4.15, bundled inside `monaco-editor` 0.57.0. That's the latest Monaco; DOMPurify 3.4.17 has the fixes.
  - All five advisories need DOMPurify settings Monaco doesn't use (`IN_PLACE`, `SAFE_FOR_TEMPLATES`, or the XHTML parser mode). Monaco calls it only with a fixed tag allowlist, and the content it sanitizes is code you wrote.
  - **Not exploitable here; not a merge blocker.**
- **Monaco loaded from a CDN:** the editor's code is downloaded at runtime from `cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/monaco-editor@0.57.0`. That third-party code runs with your site's permissions, with no integrity check and no Content-Security-Policy. This dates from the original workspace commit `a467b2a`, not this PR.
- **Other audit findings:** 16 moderate and 1 low, all in ESLint's `minimatch` dependency (a denial-of-service via glob patterns). They only run on developer machines and never ship to browsers.

## Findings by severity
**Blocking:** none.

**Medium: fix before real users**
1. **Older browsers can't submit at all.** Safari before 17.4, Chrome before 116 and Firefox before 124 now get a clear error instead of a frozen form, but still can't submit. Fix: when `AbortSignal.any` or `.timeout` is missing, fall back to one `AbortController` aborted by `setTimeout` plus a listener on the caller's signal. Add a test for that path.
2. **Third-party code from a CDN.** Serve Monaco from your own site: copy `monaco-editor/min/vs` into `public/` or bundle it, and point `loader.config` there. Then add a Content-Security-Policy. This removes the CDN dependency and makes Monaco upgrades a single `package.json` change.

**Low: track**
3. **DOMPurify.** Upgrade `monaco-editor` once a release bundles DOMPurify 3.4.17 or later. An npm override won't help: the CDN copy and Monaco's own bundled copy ignore it.
4. **Hook tests use a minimal React stand-in, not real React.** They wouldn't catch real-React behaviour such as Strict Mode's double effects. Add one test with real React when a DOM test setup exists.
5. **ESLint dependencies:** bump `eslint` / `eslint-config-next` when non-breaking versions are out.

**Info and nits**
- **"Not started" status can never show.** `investigationStarted` is hard-coded to `true` (marked TEMPORARY) and needs your decision. The comment says changing it requires your approval to modify the investigation controller.
- **Client-side "resolved" can be faked with browser devtools.** That's harmless while nothing is saved or scored on the server, but needs revisiting if progress is ever persisted.
- **Formatting nit:** `export type Difficulty ="beginner"` is missing a space.

## Landing branch
A read-only merge test of `origin/feature/landing-page` and `8955585` **merges cleanly, with no conflicts.** The challenge fields the landing page uses (`number`, `tags`, `estimatedMinutes`, `Difficulty`) all still exist after the PR. The PR removes `status`, but the landing page never uses it.

## Recommendation
**Ready to merge.** Items 1 and 2 should be tackled in a follow-up before real users, and items 3–5 can wait.

The review worktree is still in the scratchpad at `C:\Users\LAPTOP~1\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\C--Users-Laptop-Zone-by-JK-buglab\6a0a38fb-42bd-4091-8443-e500d4f86a7d\scratchpad\wt-pr1`, detached and with no changes. It has its own `node_modules` from the `npm ci`. Remove it with `git worktree remove` whenever you like. I'll wait for your approval before changing anything.

## 2. Key Technical Concepts

**Stack:** Next.js 16.4.3 (Turbopack, `cacheComponents`), React 19.3, TypeScript strict, Tailwind v4.3.3, and tests via `node:test` (`npm test`).

**Landing CSS approach:**
- Turbopack treats all `.css` as global, so the landing styles live in `src/components/landing/landing.css` with an `lp-` prefix, mapped through `styles.ts`.
- `tests/ui/landing.test.ts` checks that every class is defined outside the reduced-motion block.
- It also enforces the spoiler and messaging rules: banned words include `signed` (which is why "Assigned" was renamed to "Owner"), `cookie`, `middleware`, `session store`, `sid`, `progress`, `demo`, `preview`, and others.

**Motion patterns:**
- CSS scroll-driven animations (`view()`, named `view-timeline`, `animation-range` with CSS variables) inside `@supports`.
- `Reveal` uses an IntersectionObserver with a pending/shown state.
- Reduced-motion overrides; no layout shift.

**Pinned replay:**
- `useActiveChapter` uses an IntersectionObserver over a thin middle-of-viewport band (`rootMargin -48%`).
- `useSyncExternalStore` provides the hydrated and reduced-motion flags.
- A `<noscript><style>` fallback.

**Browser verification:**
- Headless Chrome is driven over CDP by scratchpad scripts: `replay-check.mjs`, `stage2-check.mjs`, `stage3-check.mjs`, `footer-check.mjs`, `cls-probe.mjs`.
- The server is started with `npx next start -p 3123` and stopped via PowerShell `Get-NetTCPConnection`. "Exit code 127" notifications just mean the server was stopped.

**Worktrees:** created with `git worktree add --detach` in the scratchpad.
- `node_modules` junctions must be removed with `(Get-Item).Delete()` before removing a worktree.
- Turbopack can't build through a junction to outside the root, so use `npm ci --prefer-offline` in the worktree.
- `npx next typegen` is needed for `LayoutProps` in a fresh worktree.

I recommend copying Monaco's prebuilt files into `public/` at build time and pointing the loader at our own origin. It's a small change with no new packages, and the only one I'm confident works with our Turbopack setup. One step needs your sign-off: adding a `.gitignore` line, which an earlier constraint ruled out.

Nothing was modified. I read everything from the existing `wt-pr1` review worktree, which is still clean at `8955585`. I ran one `git fetch`, which only updates remote tracking refs:
- **PR #1:** still at `8955585`.
- **Landing page:** still at `22e3903`, with only your session log modified.
- **New branch:** `origin/feature/server-attested-session-foundation` appeared (Habin's, one commit, `02e1c2b`). It only adds files under `src/server/session` and `tests/session`, so it doesn't overlap with this work.

## How Monaco loads today
- **Where it's set:** `src/components/workspace/MonacoEditor.tsx:11-14` points `@monaco-editor/react`'s loader at `https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/monaco-editor@0.57.0/min/vs`. The version string is typed by hand, and `package.json` allows `^0.57.0`, so the two can drift apart.
- **Load sequence:** `@monaco-editor/loader` 1.7.0 adds `<vs>/loader.js` as a `<script>`, then loads `vs/editor/editor.main`. The rest of the editor and its stylesheet follow as more script tags and one `<link>`, all from jsDelivr.
- **Server rendering:** `EditorPanel.tsx` already loads the editor in the browser only.
- **Workers:** `editor.main.js` sets `self.MonacoEnvironment` itself.
  - Each worker starts from a small `blob:` URL that loads the real worker file from the full URL of wherever Monaco was served.
  - The full URLs are built against the page address, so a site-relative path like `/monaco/0.57.0/vs` works.
  - Workers are started only when needed. For our file types that means the editor worker, the JSON worker and the TypeScript/JavaScript worker.
- **No `eval` needed:** the loader's `eval` code is only used by an older worker style that 0.57's self-contained workers don't use. The only `eval` in the TypeScript worker is inside a type-declaration string. A browser check should still confirm this.
- **Fonts:** the icon font is embedded in `editor.main.css` as a `data:` font. There are no other font or image requests.
- **Size:** `min/vs` is 151 files, about 25 MB on disk. Browsers download only the pieces they use, the same as from the CDN today.

## Copying into `public/` vs bundling
| | **Copy `min/vs` into `public/`** (recommended) | **Bundle Monaco's own modules and workers** |
|---|---|---|
| Code change | Change the `vs` path; `@monaco-editor/react` stays as is | Import Monaco directly, hand it to the loader, and set up the workers ourselves |
| Build risk | Low: Turbopack never processes these files | High: about 1,241 modules and 122 CSS imports from `node_modules`, all passing through our Tailwind CSS loader. Monaco support in Next.js has historically needed webpack-only plugins |
| Workers | Work unchanged | Turbopack has to bundle 5 worker entry points |
| Build time and app bundle | Unchanged | Much slower builds and a larger app bundle |
| Upgrades | Bump the version, and the copy script follows it | Watch for breaking changes in Monaco's module layout |
| Downside | A build step, plus a generated folder we must keep out of Git | More moving parts for little extra benefit |

**Don't commit the 25 MB of copied files.** Generate them at build time instead.

## Changes the recommended approach needs
1. **`scripts/copy-monaco.mjs`** (no new dependencies): reads the installed `monaco-editor` version, then copies `node_modules/monaco-editor/min/vs` to `public/monaco/<version>/vs` with `fs.cpSync`. It fails loudly if key files are missing (`loader.js`, `editor/editor.main.js`, `editor/editor.main.css` and the worker files).
2. **`package.json` scripts:** add `predev` and `prebuild` hooks that run the copy script. Also pin `"monaco-editor": "0.57.0"` exactly; the lockfile already says 0.57.0, so nothing new is installed.
3. **`MonacoEditor.tsx`:** set `paths: { vs: "/monaco/0.57.0/vs" }` and change the error message, which currently blames the CDN.
4. **`.gitignore`:** add `/public/monaco/`. This needs your approval.
5. **`next.config.ts` (optional):** a `headers()` rule giving `/monaco/:path*` a one-year immutable cache, which is safe because the path includes the version.

## Effect on dev, build, deployment and browsers
- **Development:** `npm run dev` copies first, a second or two once. Turbopack doesn't touch `public/`.
- **Production build:** the build output is unchanged, and the files are served statically.
- **Deployment:** this works anywhere that runs `npm run build`, such as Vercel's default. A pipeline that calls `next build` directly would skip the copy, and the editor would then show its "couldn't load" message; the build itself wouldn't fail. There's no deploy config in the repo to check this against.
- **Browsers:** no change, since it's the same files from our own address. It removes the dependence on jsDelivr being up or reachable, on corporate networks that block it, and on code we don't serve ourselves.

## Content-Security-Policy directives Monaco needs (for coordinating with Habin)
| Directive | Value | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `script-src` | `'self'` | `loader.js` and the editor files come from our own site; no `unsafe-eval` should be needed |
| `worker-src` | `'self' blob:` | Monaco always starts workers from `blob:` URLs |
| `style-src` | `'self' 'unsafe-inline'` | Monaco adds `<style>` elements and inline styles |
| `font-src` | `'self' data:` | The embedded icon font |
| `img-src` | `'self' data:` | Monaco's stylesheet uses inline images |
| `connect-src` | `'self'` | Monaco makes no network requests of its own |
| (drop) | `cdn.jsdelivr.net` | No longer needed anywhere |

Three points for that discussion:
- Next.js's own inline scripts need either a nonce or `'unsafe-inline'`. A nonce makes `/` render per request instead of as a static page, which is Habin's area.
- If we use nonces with `'strict-dynamic'`, the loader's added `loader.js` script inherits trust. Whether the workers' internal script loading passes needs a browser test.
- Don't enable Trusted Types enforcement yet. Monaco creates several Trusted Types policies of its own.

## Tests
**Existing:** none need updating. No test references the CDN, and the Monaco tests in `accessibility.test.ts` only check theme colours.

**New `node:test` regression tests:**
1. **No external URLs:** `MonacoEditor.tsx` has no `https://` loader path, and nothing in `src/` mentions `cdn.jsdelivr.net` or `unpkg`.
2. **Versions agree:** the version in `MonacoEditor.tsx` equals the installed `monaco-editor` version, and `package.json` pins it exactly.
3. **Copy script:** run it into a temporary folder and check that the key files and the 5 worker files exist. Running it twice gives the same result, and a missing source produces an error.

**Browser check (Chrome DevTools, like the earlier ones):**
- Load the workspace, open a `.ts` file and a `.json` file, then check:
  - every request stays on our own address;
  - the workers start;
  - there are no console errors.
- Then block `cdn.jsdelivr.net` and confirm the editor still loads.

## Plan
1. **Branch:** create `feature/monaco-self-host` from `origin/feature/engine-workspace-integration` (`8955585`), in a new worktree, so neither the landing-page branch nor PR #1 is touched.
2. **Changes:** the 5 file changes above plus the new tests; nothing in the engine, the landing page or `src/server`.
3. **Checks:** tests, TypeScript, ESLint, build, then the browser check.
4. **Review:** report back to you before any commit.
5. **Pull request:** open it once PR #1 is merged; otherwise it would carry all of PR #1's changes.

## Decisions for you
1. **Approve the overall approach:** copy into `public/`, plus a new branch from PR #1's head.
2. **Approve adding `/public/monaco/` to `.gitignore` on the new branch.** The alternative is committing 25 MB of copied files, which I don't recommend.
3. **Include the optional cache rule in `next.config.ts`, or leave it for the CSP work with Habin?**

## Tests and build

  - no CDN address in `src/`, the config or the script, and the editor taking its path only from our config.

- **`npm run build`:** passes. The copy runs first and `/` is still a static page.
- **Missing-files check:** with `public/monaco` removed, a direct `next build` stops before compiling, names the missing folder and says to run `npm run monaco:copy`.

## Browser checks
I ran these in headless Chrome against both the production server and `npm run dev`. `npm run dev` started with no copied files and regenerated all 151.

| Check | Normal | jsDelivr blocked |
|---|---|---|
| TypeScript file (`src/server.ts`) opens in the editor | ✅ | ✅ |
| JSON file (`package.json`) opens in the editor | ✅ | ✅ |
| Editor, TypeScript and JSON workers start (each worker file loaded from `/monaco/0.57.0/vs/assets/`) | ✅ 3 | ✅ 3 |
| Workers actually respond (a deliberate syntax error is flagged in both a TypeScript and a JSON test file) | ✅ | ✅ |
| Requests to any other site | 0 | 0 |
| Failed requests / console errors | 0 / 0 | 0 / 0 |
| `/monaco/0.57.0/vs/loader.js` cache header | `public, max-age=31536000, immutable` | — |

The screenshot taken with jsDelivr blocked shows the editor rendering normally in BugLab's theme.

## Remaining concerns
1. **The loader's default jsDelivr address is still in the browser code** (`…monaco-editor@0.55.1/min/vs`), but it's never used. The editor only starts after our path is set, and the blocked-CDN run made no requests to jsDelivr. A future CSP that leaves jsDelivr out would guarantee it can never load.
2. **Deployment:** a pipeline that runs `next build` directly skips the copy step. It now fails with a clear message instead of shipping a broken editor, so make sure deployments use `npm run build`.
3. **Merging with your landing-page branch:** `package.json`, `package-lock.json` and `.gitignore` all change here. The landing branch doesn't touch them, so I don't expect conflicts, but I haven't run a test merge.
4. **CSP is documented, not implemented.** The directives are in `docs/architecture/monaco-self-hosting.md`:
   - `script-src 'self'`
   - `worker-src 'self' blob:`
   - `style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'`
   - `font-src 'self' data:`
   - `img-src 'self' data:`
   - `connect-src 'self'`
   - No `'unsafe-eval'` was needed in testing.

   Two questions are for Habin: how to handle Next.js's own inline scripts (a nonce makes `/` render per request instead of statically), and whether the workers still load under `'strict-dynamic'`.
5. **Commit and session logs:** your `CLAUDE.md` says to commit `.claude-logs/` with changes. This worktree has no new session log, so tell me whether this commit should include one.

## Housekeeping
- **Line endings:** Git warns that `next.config.ts` and `package-lock.json` will be converted from LF to CRLF. That's your Windows line-ending setting and doesn't affect the code.
- **PR #1 review worktree:** `wt-pr1` is still there and unchanged.

I've fixed the test import problem and made Habin's documentation changes in the Monaco worktree. Nothing is committed, and the branch is still at `52d7a3f`, in sync with GitHub.

Caveat on the test fix: **I couldn't reproduce the failure** on this machine's Node 24.20.0. I fixed the cause I found, but please ask Habin for his exact error text and Node version so we can confirm it's the same one.

## The test import issue
**Cause:**
- The test file doesn't import `next/constants.js` directly. It imports `next.config.ts`, and that file did: `import { PHASE_DEVELOPMENT_SERVER, PHASE_PRODUCTION_BUILD } from "next/constants.js"`.
- The `next` package has no `exports` map in its `package.json`. In plain Node ESM (which our test runner uses), Next's documented form `next/constants` therefore fails with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`. That's why I switched to `next/constants.js` earlier.
- Even with `.js`, the file is CommonJS. Its named exports only exist in ESM if Node's static analysis spots them, by following a re-export and a `0 && (module.exports = {…})` hint. Node 24.20 finds them here, but that analysis differs between Node versions, so the import is fragile.
- `require("next/constants")` works reliably everywhere.

**Fix (app behaviour unchanged):**
- `next.config.ts` no longer imports `next/constants`. It checks the phase against `MONACO_VERIFY_PHASES` from `scripts/monaco-assets.mjs`, which holds the same two values: `phase-production-build` and `phase-development-server`.
- A new test reads Next's real constants with `require()` and asserts they still equal that list. If a future Next release renames a phase, the test fails rather than the check silently turning off.
- The build still stops when the Monaco files are missing; I re-ran that case, and it fails as before with the same message.

## Exact changes (4 files, +115 / −13 lines)
| File | Change |
|---|---|
| `next.config.ts` | Removed the `next/constants.js` import and switched to `MONACO_VERIFY_PHASES.includes(phase)`. The comment now names the two phases. |
| `scripts/monaco-assets.mjs` | Added `MONACO_VERIFY_PHASES`, with a comment explaining why it isn't imported. Corrected the comment on the worker list: our files start 3 of the 5 worker types, and CSS and HTML are only referenced. |
| `tests/workspace/monaco-self-hosting.test.ts` | Added the `createRequire` import, the `MONACO_VERIFY_PHASES` import and the one new test. Nothing removed or changed. |
| `docs/architecture/monaco-self-hosting.md` | The four documentation updates below. |

**Documentation updates:**
1. **Next.js inline scripts:** I counted them in the built `/` page: 16 inline scripts alongside 8 external ones.
   - One is the Turbopack runtime bootstrap and one sets up `self.__next_f`.
   - Fourteen are `self.__next_f.push(…)` calls carrying the server-rendered page data.
   - The doc explains that `script-src 'self'` alone would break the whole app, and that hashes aren't practical because these scripts change with every build.
2. **Nonce vs static rendering:** a comparison table.
   - **Nonce:** strong protection, but every page renders per request, `/` stops being static, and it interacts with Cache Components and partial prefetching.
   - **`'unsafe-inline'`:** weaker, but `/` stays static.
   - Suggested path: ship the static policy first, test it with a report-only header, and move to nonces if needed.
   - I also corrected one earlier claim. I had said `'unsafe-eval'` "wasn't needed in testing", but no CSP was active in those tests. The doc now says the code inspection found no `eval`, and that this is untested under a real policy.
3. **Workers, types vs instances:**
   - Monaco ships 5 worker types (editor, TypeScript, JSON, CSS, HTML), and all 5 are copied and checked.
   - BugLab's files use 3 of them. In the browser checks, exactly 3 workers ran, one of each type, on both the production and dev servers, with jsDelivr blocked and unblocked.
   - Workers start the first time they're needed, and one instance serves every file of its language (`.ts` and `.js` share one). Markdown, log and plain-text files start none.
4. **DOMPurify:**
   - Monaco 0.57.0 bundles DOMPurify 3.4.15; I confirmed the version string in the files we serve.
   - The doc says explicitly that this is not a claim the copy is free of vulnerabilities.
   - It records Habin's finding that no exploitable path was identified, and why: fixed allowlist, none of the options the advisories depend on, read-only author-written content. It also explains why an npm `overrides` entry wouldn't help.
   - Follow-ups: upgrade once a Monaco release ships DOMPurify 3.4.17 or later, and re-review if the editor ever shows learner-supplied or untrusted content.

## Validation
| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Monaco tests, run with `npm test`'s exact command on that file | **20/20 pass** (19 existing + 1 new) |
| Full suite | **416/416 pass** (415 + 1 new), none skipped |
| TypeScript and ESLint | Clean |
| `npm run build` | Passes; `/` is still static |
| `next build` with `public/monaco` removed | Still fails with the clear "assets are missing … run `npm run monaco:copy`" message |

**No test was weakened or removed:**
- The test file's diff only adds lines, all 19 earlier test names are unchanged, and the assertion count went from 40 to 41.
- No other test file changed.
- The Challenge Engine, the AbortSignal code, the session code and the landing page are untouched.

I didn't repeat the headless-Chrome run, because nothing the browser loads changed. The only app change is how `next.config.ts` checks the build phase, and the build output is the same.

- **Monaco worktree:** branch `feature/monaco-self-host` at `52d7a3f`, with 4 uncommitted files.
- **`feature/landing-page`:** still at `22e3903`, with only your existing session log modified.
- **Everything else:** no other branch was touched, and nothing was committed or pushed.

I corrected the worker section of the doc and pushed it. The new commit is `f7023283dbff0b96ff2128b0ddaf182a37a33c97` (`docs(workspace): clarify Monaco worker instances`). It contains only `docs/architecture/monaco-self-hosting.md`. GitHub's `feature/monaco-self-host` matches it, and the working tree is clean.

Habin was right, and my earlier statement that `.ts` and `.js` files share one worker was wrong. I confirmed this in headless Chrome against the production server, opening files one at a time:

| After opening | Worker instances | By bundle type |
|---|---|---|
| `INCIDENT.md` (the initial file) | 1 | editor |
| `src/server.ts` | 2 | editor, TypeScript |
| `src/routes/auth.ts` | 2 | no change (same TypeScript instance) |
| `public/login.js` | 3 | editor, TypeScript ×2 |
| `public/dashboard.js` | 3 | no change (same JavaScript instance) |
| `package.json` | 4 | editor, TypeScript ×2, JSON |

The TypeScript bundle runs as two instances because Monaco keeps separate TypeScript and JavaScript language services, each with its own worker. There were no page errors.

**What the doc now says:**
- **Terms:** a *bundle type* is a script file Monaco ships, and an *instance* is a running worker. One bundle type can run more than once.
- **Five bundle types still covered:** the table lists all five Monaco ships (editor, TypeScript, JSON, CSS, HTML), with a column for how many instances each runs in BugLab.
- **The total:** at most 4 instances from 3 bundle types, with the file-by-file table above.
- **Earlier browser runs:** they opened only Markdown, one `.ts` file and `package.json`, which is why they saw 3 instances.

I made no code or test changes. The comment in `scripts/monaco-assets.mjs` talks about worker types, not instances, so it's still accurate.

The branch is now `8955585` → `52d7a3f` → `28b33a0` → `f702328`. `feature/landing-page` is still at `22e3903` with only your session log modified, and no other branch was touched. No PR was opened.

The review also turned up one small problem in my Monaco branch, which I'll cover below.

Everything stayed read-only:
- I fetched remote refs, which added `origin/fix/abortsignal-legacy-compatibility`.
- I computed merges inside Git only.
- I ran the merged code in a throwaway scratchpad copy, now deleted.
- No branch, worktree or repo file changed. `feature/landing-page` is still at `22e3903` with only your session log modified, and the Monaco worktree is clean at `f702328`.

## 1. Commits verified
| Ref | Commit |
|---|---|
| `origin/fix/abortsignal-legacy-compatibility` | `92aeaa9` (Habin Abdul Rahman), a single commit directly on top of `8955585` |
| `origin/feature/monaco-self-host` | `f702328` |
| `origin/feature/engine-workspace-integration` | `8955585` |

Habin's commit changes 3 files:
- **`src/lib/diagnosis/request-abort.ts`** (new): builds one cancel signal from `AbortController`, `setTimeout` and a listener on your signal.
- **`src/lib/diagnosis/client.ts`**: now uses that helper and cleans up in a `finally` block.
- **`tests/diagnosis/client.test.ts`**: rewritten with fake timers; client tests went from 20 to 49.

I couldn't see PR #2: the GitHub API returns "Not Found" without a login, which suggests the repo is private. Nothing I recommend below touches any existing PR.

## 2. The fallback implementation
Every browser now takes the same path. It never calls `AbortSignal.any()` or `AbortSignal.timeout()`, even where they exist.

| Requirement | Verdict | Why |
|---|---|---|
| 15-second timeout | ✅ | `REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS = 15_000` is unchanged. The timer covers both the request and reading the response, because it's only released in `finally`. |
| Cancelling from the page | ✅ | Aborting your signal aborts the request with the same reason, and `submitDiagnosis` rethrows it as before. |
| Timer and listener cleanup | ✅ | Released on success, failure, timeout and cancellation. Safe to run twice. Tests count timers and listeners after every outcome, including repeated and overlapping submissions. |
| Signal already aborted | ✅ | Rejects straight away with that signal's own reason, or an `AbortError` on browsers without `reason`. No request is sent and no timer starts. |
| Error handling | ✅ | The "update your browser" message now only appears without `AbortController`. A timeout still says "The server took too long to respond." |

**Who benefits:** Next.js 16 supports Chrome/Edge 111+, Firefox 111+ and Safari 16.4+. Of those, Safari 16.4–17.3, Chrome 111–115 and Firefox 111–123 have `AbortSignal.timeout` but not `AbortSignal.any`. Since D1 they've been refused; with this fix they can submit. Every browser Next.js supports has `AbortController`, so the "update your browser" message is now effectively unreachable.

## 3. Test gaps (none block a merge)
1. **Timeout or cancel while reading the response (low).** The response is read with `response.json().catch(() => null)`. An abort during that read is swallowed:
   - A timeout at that moment shows "Unexpected response from the server (200)" instead of the timeout message.
   - Your own cancel at that moment returns an error result instead of being rethrown.

   This existed before Habin's change, and the form already discards results after unmount. Fix: in that `.catch`, rethrow if the request's signal is aborted, and add a test for each case.
2. **Timeout and cancel at almost the same moment.** If the timer fires and your signal aborts before the error is handled, the cancel wins. That's fine, but untested.
3. **No real-browser check of the fallback.** All tests run in Node with the browser APIs deleted. I can run a check with our existing headless Chrome setup:
   - remove `AbortSignal.any` and `AbortSignal.timeout` before the page loads;
   - submit a diagnosis;
   - confirm the response arrives, and that a stalled request shows the timeout message.
4. **The form's own tests (4) still use a stand-in for the client,** so nothing tests the real client and the form together. Adding that is optional.

## 4. Conflicts with your Monaco branch
**There are no conflicts:**
- **No shared files:** Habin's branch only touches `src/lib/diagnosis/*` and `tests/diagnosis/client.test.ts`. Your Monaco branch touches config, the Monaco script and editor, its test and its doc.
- **Test merges:** these computed merges are all conflict-free:
  - Monaco + AbortSignal (tree `1c3b284`);
  - landing + AbortSignal;
  - landing + Monaco.
- **Merged checks:**

  | Check | Result |
  |---|---|
  | Client tests | 49/49 |
  | Full suite | **445/445**: 396 − 20 replaced client tests + 49 new + 20 Monaco tests |
  | ESLint | Clean |
  | TypeScript | Clean, after the step below |

I didn't run a production build on the merged code; that needs a fresh `npm ci`, which the restrictions ruled out. Habin's change is plain client code with no build configuration involved, and both branches build on their own.

**Problem in my Monaco branch:** `next typegen` generates Next.js's route types, which TypeScript needs in a fresh checkout. It loads `next.config.ts` in the production-build phase, so the Monaco asset check makes it fail until `npm run monaco:copy` has run. A CI job running `npm ci → next typegen → tsc` would fail, even though Habin's run passed (probably because the assets already existed locally). Simplest fix: add a `typecheck` script that copies the assets, then generates types and runs `tsc`, and mention it in the doc. Another option is to only warn about missing assets during type generation, but Next.js uses the same phase name for both, so they can't be told apart reliably.

## 5. CSP decisions to agree with Habin before implementation
1. **Nonce or static policy.** A nonce gives strong protection, but every page renders per request and `/` stops being static. A static policy allows inline scripts and keeps `/` static. This also affects Cache Components and partial prefetching.
2. **Where the header is set:** the Next.js 16 proxy (needed for nonces), `headers()` in `next.config.ts`, or the hosting platform. `next.config.ts` already holds the Monaco cache header, so agree on who edits it to avoid conflicts.
3. **Development vs production.** Next.js's dev server needs `'unsafe-eval'`. Decide between a production-only policy and a separate, looser dev policy.
4. **Inline styles.** Monaco, the landing page (`style={{…}}` CSS variables and a `<noscript><style>`) and the workspace all need `style-src 'unsafe-inline'`. The built `/` alone has 28 inline style attributes.
5. **Workers.** `worker-src 'self' blob:` is required. If you choose `'strict-dynamic'`, test that the workers still load their scripts.
6. **Baseline directives:** `object-src 'none'`, `base-uri 'self'`, `frame-ancestors 'none'`, `form-action 'self'`, `connect-src 'self'`.
7. **Rollout and reporting.** Start with a report-only header, decide where violation reports go, and set the verification plan: headless Chrome capturing violations on the landing page, workspace, editor and diagnosis form.
8. **Defer Trusted Types.** Monaco creates its own policies.
9. **Timing.** Implement CSP last, on its own branch, after the landing page, Monaco and AbortSignal changes are merged. It has to be tested against all of them.

## 6. Recommended merge order
1. **Habin's fix into PR #1 (his decision).** `92aeaa9` sits directly on top of PR #1's head. He can either fast-forward `feature/engine-workspace-integration` to it, so PR #1 includes it, or open it as a separate PR to `main` after PR #1 merges. Optionally add the response-read fix (gap 1) first.
2. **Merge PR #1 into `main`.**
3. **Monaco PR.** Open `feature/monaco-self-host` → `main` after PR #1 merges. Its base `8955585` will already be in `main`, so the PR shows only the Monaco commits and needs no rebase. If you approve, I'll first add the `typecheck` fix in its worktree.
4. **Landing page last,** with the `211da9b` vs `48fad4a` history question handled as a separate step. The test merges with both other branches came back clean.
5. **CSP afterwards,** on a fresh branch from `main`, once the decisions above are agreed.

## Proposed next steps (waiting for your approval)
- **A.** Add the `typecheck` script and a doc note to `feature/monaco-self-host` for the `next typegen` problem.
- **B.** Run the real-browser check of Habin's fallback (gap 3) and report back without changing anything.
- **C.** Send Habin the test gaps (gap 1 especially) and the CSP decision list. I can draft a message for you to send.

I fixed the fresh-checkout problem by adding npm scripts that copy the Monaco assets before generating types. The missing-assets check is unchanged, and the full workflow now works from a clean `npm ci`. The changes are uncommitted in the Monaco worktree, which is still at `f702328`, matching GitHub.

## What was wrong
- **The failing step:** TypeScript needs types that Next.js generates into `.next/types/`, such as `LayoutProps`. In a fresh checkout those don't exist, so a bare `npx tsc --noEmit` fails on `LayoutProps`, with or without Monaco.
- **Why it fails:** generating them runs `next typegen`, which loads `next.config.ts` as if it were a production build (`next/dist/cli/next-typegen.js:83`). That triggers the Monaco asset check, so `next typegen` stops in a fresh checkout until the assets have been copied.
- **Nothing to update elsewhere:** there's no CI configuration in the repo and no existing `typecheck` script, and the README only mentions `npm run dev`.

## Changes (4 files, +47 / −4)
| File | Change |
|---|---|
| `package.json` | Three new scripts, no other changes: `"pretypegen": "npm run monaco:copy"`, `"typegen": "next typegen"` and `"typecheck": "npm run typegen && tsc --noEmit"`. |
| `scripts/monaco-assets.mjs` | The missing-assets message now also mentions `npm run typecheck`. Only the message text changed. |
| `tests/workspace/monaco-self-hosting.test.ts` | One new test checks the three scripts, and the missing-assets test now also expects the `typecheck` hint. |
| `docs/architecture/monaco-self-hosting.md` | New "Fresh checkouts, type generation and CI" section, covering the sequence below. |

The new doc section covers:
- the recommended order: `npm ci` → `npm run typecheck` → `npm run lint` → `npm test` → `npm run build`;
- why bare `npx next typegen`, `next build` or `next dev` stop until the assets are copied, and that this is deliberate;
- that a bare `tsc` fails on `LayoutProps` regardless of Monaco;
- that tests and lint don't need the copy.

I also updated the table at the top of the doc.

Only the error message changed in the script. The asset check in `next.config.ts` is as strict as before.

## Clean-checkout validation
I exported the committed branch plus these changes into an isolated scratchpad folder, ran a real `npm ci` (no linked `node_modules`), and started without a `.next` folder or `public/monaco`:

| Step | Result |
|---|---|
| Bare `npx tsc --noEmit` | Fails on `LayoutProps`, as expected without generated types |
| Bare `npx next typegen` | Fails with the clear message, now naming `npm run typecheck`. This is the problem we found, reproduced |
| `npm test` and `npm run lint`, before any copy | 417/417 pass; lint clean (neither needs the assets) |
| **`npm run typecheck`, from nothing** | ✅ Copies 151 files, generates types, TypeScript clean |
| `npm run typecheck` again | ✅ Skips the copy as up to date |
| **`npm run build`, from nothing** (assets and `.next` deleted) | ✅ Copies, then builds; `/` still static |
| Tests and lint after building | ✅ 417/417 pass; lint clean, still skipping the copied files |

The clean copy is deleted.

## Worktree validation
- **Tests:** 21/21 Monaco tests and **417/417** overall. That's the previous 416 plus the new script test; none skipped.
- **Other checks:** `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint` and `npm run build` all pass.
- **Nothing weakened:** every earlier test name is unchanged. The one rewritten assertion still checks everything it did before, plus the `typecheck` hint.

One slip along the way: my first `package.json` edit accidentally dropped the space in `"monaco:copy": …`. I restored it and re-ran the checks, so the final diff for that file is only the 3 added lines.

## State
- **Monaco worktree:** `feature/monaco-self-host` at `f702328`, with 4 uncommitted files.
- **`feature/landing-page`:** still at `22e3903`, with only your session log modified.
- **Untouched:** the Challenge Engine, diagnosis client, landing page and session code. No CSP, and no other branch changed. Nothing committed or pushed.

**Remaining point:** anyone, or any future CI job, that runs bare `npx next typegen` or `npx tsc` will still hit these failures. The doc explains this, and when CI is added it should call `npm run typecheck`.

Waiting for your approval before committing.

## Commits
| Ref | Commit |
|---|---|

Changes from `92aeaa9` to `c7d83c2`:
- **`src/lib/diagnosis/client.ts`** (+8 / −1): replaces `response.json().catch(() => null)` with a `try`/`catch` that rethrows when the request signal is aborted. Other read failures still become "Unexpected response".
- **`tests/diagnosis/client.test.ts`** (+182): 16 new tests, taking the client file from 49 to 65.
- **`request-abort.ts`**: unchanged.

## Review of the diagnosis client
| Scenario | Verdict | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| You cancel while the response is being read | ✅ Rethrown | Tested in all 4 browser setups. Also confirmed with Node's real `fetch` |
| Timeout while the response is being read | ✅ "The server took too long to respond." | Tested in all 4 browser setups. Confirmed with a real 15-second wait |
| Your abort reason is passed through | ✅ The exact same object is rethrown; without a reason, it's an `AbortError` | Tested. Confirmed with real `fetch` for both |
| Timer and listener cleanup | ✅ The timeout keeps running while the body is read. Both are released after an abort, a timeout or any other failure | Tested, including 20 timeouts in a row with no leftovers |
| Cancel just after the response is parsed | ✅ Returns the result instead of throwing. The form already ignores results after you leave | Confirmed with real `fetch`; no dedicated test (gap 1) |
| Cancel just before the response is read | ✅ By design, since the check looks at the request signal itself | Not tested (gap 2) |
| Normal success and failure responses | ✅ Unchanged | All earlier response tests pass, including a real truncated body still giving "Unexpected response (200)" |

**Why the classification is robust:** it checks whether the request's signal was aborted, not the error's name. A stray error that merely carries the name `AbortError` stays "Unexpected response", and there's a test for that. It also means classification doesn't depend on how each browser reports aborted body reads; only which error object gets rethrown does.

**Security:** none found. Rethrown errors are only ever your own cancellation reason, and on-screen messages stay generic. No server, validation or answer-key code changed.

## Test coverage
The 16 new tests genuinely exercise these cases. A fake `fetch` sends headers, then stalls the body until the test aborts, fires the timeout or lets it finish, so each test can act at exactly the body-reading stage.

Minor gaps (none blocking):
1. **Cancel just after parsing:** no test pins that this returns the result rather than throwing. It's similar in spirit to the existing "aborting after the request has finished does nothing" test.
2. **Cancel before the body read starts:** the fake `fetch` only listens for aborts once reading begins, so this window can't be tested. With real `fetch` it's handled by the same signal check. Fixing it needs the fake to check `signal.aborted` up front.
3. **No real browser yet:** all checks are in Node. I offered a headless Chrome check of the older-browser path last time, and that offer still stands.

## Combined with Monaco `a40269c`
- **No shared files:** Habin's branch changes 3 diagnosis files, the Monaco branch changes 9 config, editor and docs files.
- **Merges:** `git merge-tree` of `a40269c` and `c7d83c2` is conflict-free (tree `4978f9b`). Landing page + `c7d83c2` is also clean.

## 2. Key Technical Concepts

**Stack**
- Next.js 16.4.3 (Turbopack, `cacheComponents`, `partialPrefetching`), React 19.3, TypeScript strict, Tailwind v4.
- Node v24.20.0 locally (Habin verified on 24.3.0).
- Tests run on `node:test` via `node --import ./tests/setup/register.mjs --test "tests/**/*.test.ts"`. Register hooks map `@/` to `src/`, resolve extensionless relative imports, strip `.ts` types natively, and compile `.tsx` with TypeScript.

**Monaco 0.57.0 `min/vs`**
- Loading: `@monaco-editor/loader` 1.7.0 injects `<vs>/loader.js` (AMD), then `require(['vs/editor/editor.main'])`.
- `editor.main.js` sets `self.MonacoEnvironment.getWorker`. It creates workers from `blob:` URLs that `importScripts()` absolute same-origin URLs built via `new URL(require.toUrl(...), document.baseURI)`.
- 151 files, about 25 MB. The codicon font is a `data:` URL inside `editor.main.css`.
- No `eval` on the browser paths actually used.
- The loader's built-in default is `https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/monaco-editor@0.55.1/min/vs`. The string stays in the bundle unused, guarded because the editor never starts without the self-hosted path.

**Workers: bundle types vs instances**
- 5 bundle types: editor, ts, json, css, html.
- BugLab uses 3 types and runs at most 4 instances, because the TypeScript and JavaScript language services each run their own ts.worker instance.

**Version and environment plumbing**
- Single version source: the exact pin `"monaco-editor": "0.57.0"` in `package.json` and the lockfile root entry.
- `next.config.ts` is a phase function. It injects `env.MONACO_VS_PATH` and sets a `headers()` immutable cache on `/monaco/:path*`.
- `next typegen` loads the config in `PHASE_PRODUCTION_BUILD` (`next/dist/cli/next-typegen.js:83`).
- `next` has no package `exports` map. So `next/constants` (bare) fails under ESM, and named imports from `next/constants.js` depend on CommonJS lexer detection, which is fragile. `createRequire` is reliable.

**Next.js browser targets and inline scripts**
- Next.js 16 targets: chrome 111, edge 111, firefox 111, safari 16.4.
- `AbortSignal.any` arrived in Safari 17.4, Chrome 116 and Firefox 124.
- The prerendered `/` has 16 inline scripts: 1 Turbopack bootstrap, 1 `__next_f` init and 14 `self.__next_f.push`. It also has 8 external scripts and 28 inline style attributes.

**Worktrees and safety**
- Windows worktrees live in the scratchpad.
- Always check that `node_modules` is not a junction, or delete the junction first via `(Get-Item).Delete()`, before recursive removal.
- Turbopack can't build through a junction pointing outside the root, so isolated checks use a real `npm ci`.

**Browser verification**
- Headless Chrome is driven over the DevTools protocol (CDP) from scratchpad scripts.
- `Target.setAutoAttach` with flatten is used for workers; `Network.setBlockedURLs` blocks jsDelivr.
- Servers are stopped with PowerShell `Get-NetTCPConnection … Stop-Process`. "exit code 127" notifications just mean the server was stopped.

- Exports:

  - `REQUIRED_FILES`: loader.js, nls.messages-loader.js, editor/editor.main.js, editor/editor.main.css
  - `REQUIRED_WORKERS`: prefixes editor.worker-, json.worker-, ts.worker-, css.worker-, html.worker-, with a comment that 3 are started and CSS/HTML are only referenced
  - `MONACO_VERIFY_PHASES = ["phase-production-build", "phase-development-server"]`, with a comment explaining why it isn't imported from next/constants
- Functions:
  - `readPinnedVersion(root)`: requires `/^\d+\.\d+\.\d+$/`.
  - `readInstalledVersion`.
  - `monacoVsPath(v)`: returns `/monaco/${v}/vs`.
  - `monacoOutputDir`.
  - `findMissingAssets`: returns `["(the whole directory: nothing has been copied yet)"]` when the directory is missing.
  - `verifyMonacoAssets`: error message ends "Run `npm run monaco:copy` (it also runs before `npm run dev`, `npm run build` and `npm run typecheck`)."

    - checks version mismatch and source layout;
    - refuses if `public` or `public/monaco` is a symlink/junction (`lstatSync`);
    - removes stale version dirs;
    - skips when up to date (same file list and sizes);
    - otherwise `cpSync` plus LICENSE, then verifies.
- CLI is gated by `if (import.meta.main)`, with `copy|verify` commands.

**`next.config.ts`**
```ts
import type { NextConfig } from "next";
import { MONACO_PUBLIC_DIR, MONACO_VERIFY_PHASES, monacoVsPath, readPinnedVersion, verifyMonacoAssets } from "./scripts/monaco-assets.mjs";
export default function nextConfig(phase: string): NextConfig {
  if (MONACO_VERIFY_PHASES.includes(phase)) { verifyMonacoAssets(); }
  return { cacheComponents: true, partialPrefetching: true,
    env: { MONACO_VS_PATH: monacoVsPath(readPinnedVersion()) },
    async headers() { return [{ source: `/${MONACO_PUBLIC_DIR}/:path*`, headers: [{ key: "Cache-Control", value: "public, max-age=31536000, immutable" }] }]; },
    turbopack: { rules: { "*.css": { loaders: ["@tailwindcss/turbopack"], as: "*.css" } } } };
}
```

- `useState(!MONACO_VS_PATH)`, and `if (!MONACO_VS_PATH) return;` in the effect.

**Habin's branch (reviewed read-only)**
- `src/lib/diagnosis/request-abort.ts` (from `92aeaa9`):
  - `createRequestAbort(timeoutMs, callerSignal)` throws `UnsupportedBrowserError` when there's no `AbortController`, and throws the caller's reason (or an `AbortError` DOMException) when the signal is pre-aborted.
  - It creates a controller, a `setTimeout` that sets `timedOut` and aborts, and a listener that forwards `callerSignal.reason`.
  - `dispose()` clears both and is idempotent.
- `src/lib/diagnosis/client.ts` at `c7d83c2`:
  - `request = createRequestAbort(15_000, signal)` and the fetch uses `request.signal`.
  - New body read:
    ```ts
    let body: unknown = null;
    try { body = await response.json(); } catch (error) { if (request.signal.aborted) throw error; }
    ```
  - Outer catch: `UnsupportedBrowserError` gives the unsupported message; `if (signal?.aborted) throw error;`; otherwise `timedOut()` gives "The server took too long to respond.", else "Couldn't reach the server…".
  - `finally { request?.dispose(); }`.
- `tests/diagnosis/client.test.ts`:
  - 65 tests at `c7d83c2` (20 → 49 at `92aeaa9`, +16 at `c7d83c2`).
  - Helpers `fetchWithStalledBody`, `fetchWithFailingBody` and fake timers.
  - New tests:
    - caller abort and timeout during the body read, in all 4 AbortSignal environments;
    - the caller's own reason preserved;
    - SyntaxError, TypeError "terminated" and a fake-named AbortError DOMException are not treated as cancellation;
    - a real truncated `Response` gives "Unexpected response (200)";
    - timer/listener holding and release, plus 20 repeated timeouts.

## 4. Errors and fixes

- **`next/constants` bare import under Node ESM** → `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`. Switched to `next/constants.js`, then later to a literal `MONACO_VERIFY_PHASES` plus a `createRequire` guard test (Habin reported Node 24 incompatibility).
- **tsc TS5097** importing `../../next.config.ts` → used the extensionless `../../next.config`.
- **Missing-dir error listed an absolute path** → replaced with "(the whole directory: nothing has been copied yet)".
- **Symlink/junction deletion risk in the copy script** (found during the pre-commit review) → added the `lstatSync` guard and a test.
- **Doc claimed "no 'unsafe-eval' needed in testing"**, but no CSP was active → corrected to "code inspection found none; untested under enforcing policy".
- **Doc wrongly said `.ts` and `.js` share one TS worker** (Habin caught it) → verified 4 instances by browser and corrected.
- **Fresh-checkout `next typegen` fails** because the config verifies assets in the build phase → added `pretypegen`/`typegen`/`typecheck` scripts, doc and tests.
- **Accidentally removed the space in `"monaco:copy": …`** → restored; the final `package.json` diff is just 3 added lines.
- **Combined-check tsc `LayoutProps` missing** → caused by typegen failing without assets; resolved via copy plus typegen.
- **Earlier miscount (38 vs 39 files) on the landing commit** was disclosed.

## 5. Problem Solving

**Monaco self-hosting**
- Browser-verified on `next start` and `next dev`, with and without jsDelivr blocked.
- 0 third-party requests and 0 errors. TS and JSON markers came back from the workers.
- `loader.js` is served with `public, max-age=31536000, immutable`.

**AbortSignal `92aeaa9` review**
- Correct.
- Noted gaps: body-read abort swallowed (Habin then fixed it in `c7d83c2`), race untested, no real-browser check, hook tests use a double.

**`c7d83c2` review**
- Verified: `92aeaa9` is an ancestor (fast-forward), and `c7d83c2` changes only `client.ts` and the client tests. `request-abort.ts` is unchanged.
- Habin's branch changes only `src/lib/diagnosis/client.ts`, `src/lib/diagnosis/request-abort.ts` and `tests/diagnosis/client.test.ts`. None of those overlap with the Monaco branch's 9 files.
- `git merge-tree --write-tree a40269c c7d83c2` gave tree `4978f9b19c630552f25d74cc6028903a2811e4c0`, exit 0 (clean). Landing + `c7d83c2` is also clean.
- The combined tree was exported to `scratchpad/combined-c7`. A real `npm ci` added 364 packages, starting with no `.next` and no `public/monaco`.
  - `npm run typecheck`: copied 151 files, types generated, exit 0.
  - `npm run lint`: exit 0.
  - `npm test`: **462/462 pass**, 65 suites, 0 fail/cancelled/skipped/todo. That equals 396 − 20 + 65 + 21.
  - Per file: client 65, monaco 21, hook 4.
  - `npm run build` from no `.next`: success, `/` static, API dynamic.
- Real-fetch script (Node undici + local stalling http server) against the merged client:
  - A: a caller abort with a custom reason during the body read rejects with that exact reason.
  - B: an abort without a reason rejects with `AbortError`.
  - C: a truncated body gives "Unexpected response from the server (200)."
  - D: an abort after parsing returns a result, not a rethrow. (My fake body was not a fully valid response, so the status was "error".)
  - E: a real timeout during the body read gives "The server took too long to respond." after 15 s.
- Cleanup: `combined-c7` was deleted after checking that `node_modules` was not a link (`rd /s /q`), and `abort-real-fetch.ts` was removed. Both are confirmed gone.
- Final state:
  - The main repo is still on `feature/landing-page` at `22e3903` with only the `.claude-logs` file modified.
  - `wt-monaco` is clean at `a40269c`; `wt-pr1` is at `8955585` (detached).
  - Branches: `main` 333dab2, `feature/debugging-workspace` 211da9b, `feature/debugging-workspace-clean` 48fad4a.

**Remaining review observations to report (not yet reported)**
- No blockers, no regressions and no security concerns: rethrown reasons are the caller's own, and messages are generic.
- Minor gaps:
  1. A caller abort after `json()` resolves but before return yields a result, not a rethrow. This is benign, since the hook drops late results, but it isn't pinned by a test.
  2. An abort between the fetch resolving and the `json()` call isn't tested. The `fetchWithStalledBody` mock adds its abort listener inside `json()`, so a pre-aborted case would hang. Real fetch rejects immediately and the classification would work.
  3. The tests mock the body. My real-fetch check confirmed the behaviour in Node undici, but there's still no real-browser check of the legacy environment.
  4. The hook tests still use a client double.
- Note: abort-reason identity during the body read depends on the browser propagating `signal.reason` to the body stream. Older browsers give `AbortError`. The classification uses `request.signal.aborted`, so it's robust either way.
- I can't see PR #2 because the repo is private and `gh` isn't installed.
