This base has self-hosted, multi-language Monaco. It does not include the AbortSignal fixes or PR #2; those exist only on the integration branch.

## Components that work for multiple challenges

**Usable as they are**, because they take a challenge ID or scenario data as props:
- **The workspace:** `WorkspaceClient` and everything under it, including the explorer, tabs, editor, Monaco, investigation console, logs, network and notes.
- **The diagnosis UI:** `DiagnosisPanel` and its parts, `useDiagnosisSubmission` and the diagnosis client.
- **The engine:** `ChallengeSessionProvider` (configured per challenge) and `SessionChallengeOverview`. Editor models are already named per challenge.
- **For cards:**
  - `Badge`, `StatusDot`, `Panel`, `TabList` and `useListboxNavigation`, which suits keyboard navigation of a card grid;
  - `LogFilterBar`'s pattern of chips, search, counts and reset, as the template for library filters.

**Need a small refactor:**
- **`ChallengeOverview`:** the difficulty and status label/colour maps are private to this component. Cards would need them moved into a shared module. `status` is required, so a card needs it optional or a separate card component.
- **`AppHeader`:** "Challenges" is plain text marked "Soon", and "Workspace" is hard-coded as the current page. It needs real links and a way to mark the current page.
- **`src/app/page.tsx`:** the page assembly (provider, overview, workspace) is written inline for Challenge 001. It should become a shared `ChallengeWorkspacePage({ scenario })` used by both `/` and the new route.

## Challenge metadata available for cards

`ChallengeSummary` already has these fields:
- `id`, `number` and `title`;
- `incident`, a one-line symptom that's safe to show;
- `difficulty` (`beginner`, `intermediate` or `advanced`);
- `completion` (`diagnosis`, or `fix` reserved for later);
- `tags`, e.g. Challenge 001 has Authentication, HTTP and Backend;
- `estimatedMinutes`.

**Missing:**
- **Language:** no primary language field, so a language filter can't be built yet. Inferring it from file extensions would mean loading every scenario's files.
- **Learner status:** deliberately not stored on the summary, since it's per-learner state. Today it only exists in memory on the workspace page, so cards can't show it.
- **Optional extras:** a sort order or a "new" flag.

## Public-only fixture data required

- **Library page:** only summary records. No files, logs, root-cause options or answer keys.
- **Navigating into a fixture scenario:** a full public scenario (files, logs, network requests and root-cause options), test-only or behind a dev flag.
- **A safety problem with fixtures:**
  - The diagnosis server only knows `challenge-001`, so a fixture would get "Unknown challenge" (404) when submitted.
  - Fixtures must therefore never ship as real challenges. They should live under `tests/fixtures/`, or be clearly marked as non-submittable demos.
- **Content rules every card must follow:**
  - show the incident text only, never anything about the root cause;
  - add a spoiler check to the library's content tests. The landing page already has one for Challenge 001.
- **Suggested fixture spread:** Python, SQL (PostgreSQL), Java and JavaScript at mixed difficulties, to exercise the filters and the editor languages.

## Recommended routes

- **`/challenges`:** the library. A static server page renders all cards, and filtering happens in the browser using the URL query string (e.g. `?difficulty=beginner&tag=HTTP`).
  - Reading the query string on the server would make the page dynamic, because `cacheComponents` is enabled.
  - The browser-side filter is wrapped in Suspense, with the full unfiltered list rendered as the fallback, so the page works without JavaScript.
- **`/challenges/[challengeId]`:** the workspace for one challenge.
  - It prebuilds each known challenge with `generateStaticParams` and returns a 404 page for unknown IDs.
  - It awaits `params`, as Next 16 requires.
  - Each challenge's subtree gets its own key, so learner state from one challenge never carries over to the next. Editor models are already isolated.

## Navigating without breaking `/`

- **`/` must stay the Challenge 001 workspace.** On the landing branch, every call-to-action link (`primaryCta`, `openWorkspaceCta`, `caseCta`, `replayCta`) points to `ROUTES.workspace = "/"`.
- **Both routes render the same component** for Challenge 001: `/` and `/challenges/challenge-001` would each use the shared `ChallengeWorkspacePage` with the same data. I would not add a redirect.
- **Moving the landing links** to `/challenges` is a separate decision for later, made on the landing branch.
- **Header links:** "Challenges" links to `/challenges`. "Workspace" is marked current only on a workspace route.
- **Existing behaviour is unchanged:** the diagnosis endpoint (`/api/challenges/[id]/diagnosis`) and `challenge-001`'s editor model names stay the same.

## Decisions that depend on Habin's public registry

1. **Where the list comes from:** the module path and signatures, e.g. `listChallenges()` and `getPublicScenario(id)`, and whether they're synchronous or asynchronous. The module must not live under `src/server/`: the confidentiality test only allows server-only files to import from there, so pages couldn't use it.
2. **Fields:** whether `ChallengeSummary` gains `language` (a single value or a list) and an optional `contentVersion`, needed for the session record.
3. **Which IDs are real:** how the registry marks live challenges versus drafts or fixtures. The library should only link to challenges the diagnosis server can evaluate.
4. **Learner status on cards:** this needs Habin's status endpoint, a batch version or one call per challenge. Until then cards show no status, rather than a made-up "Not started".
5. **ID and URL format:** whether `challenge-001` is the permanent URL form, or a slug is preserved.
6. **Ordering and visibility:** sort order, and whether `completion: "fix"` challenges are hidden until Fix Mode exists.

## Proposed sequence (after your approval)

1. Pull out the shared difficulty/status display, a `ChallengeCard`, and `ChallengeWorkspacePage`. `/` should render exactly as before.
2. Add a temporary `src/lib/challenge-catalog.ts` adapter that holds only Challenge 001. It's the one file to swap for Habin's registry.
3. Add `/challenges` with cards and the filter logic, written as pure functions with tests.
4. Add `/challenges/[challengeId]` with static params and a 404 page.
5. Turn the header links on.
6. Add test-only fixtures and spoiler and leak tests.

Everything stays on `feature/challenge-library-ui`. I'll wait for your approval.

I created the worktree and analysed the Origin check. It has real weaknesses, but they aren't exploitable from a browser in the current setup, because two other defences already stop cross-site submissions. No code or tests were changed.

- **Worktree:** `C:\Users\Laptop Zone by JK\buglab-diagnosis-security`
- **Branch:** `fix/diagnosis-origin-validation`, based on `2461037`. Only git-ignored output was added (`node_modules/`, `.next/`, `public/monaco/`, `next-env.d.ts`).
- **Integration branch:** clean at `2461037` before I started.

## How the check works today

In `src/server/diagnosis/handler.ts`, the checks run in this order:
1. **Content type:** must match `^application/json\s*(;|$)`, otherwise **415**.
2. **`isSameOrigin`:**
   - no `Origin` header → **allowed**;
   - an `Origin` that can't be parsed (including `"null"`) → **403**;
   - otherwise it compares `new URL(origin).host` (hostname and port only, **no scheme**) with the request's **`Host` header**, which the client controls. `request.url` is used only when there's no `Host` header.
3. Then the challenge lookup, the 16 KB body limit, validation, the `buglab_attempt` cookie, the rate limits and evaluation.

**Cookie:** `buglab_attempt=<uuid>; Path=/api; Max-Age=86400; HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict`, with `Secure` only when `request.url` is `https:`.

**What `request.url` is under `next start`:**
- From Next's own router code: its host is the server's **bind address** (e.g. `localhost:3000`), not the client's `Host` header.
- Its protocol comes from the TLS socket, or from the client-supplied **`X-Forwarded-Proto`**.
- The repo has no `trustHostHeader`, middleware, proxy or deployment config.

## Can the check be bypassed?

I checked each case two ways: by calling the handler directly with 17 header combinations, and over real HTTP against a production build from the new worktree.

| Case | Result | Assessment |
|---|---|---|
| Missing Origin | Allowed | Browsers always send `Origin` on cross-origin fetches and on POSTs, so only non-browser clients omit it, and those can forge any header anyway. **Low.** |
| `Origin: null` or malformed | 403 | Correctly rejected. |
| Origin with a path or user info, e.g. `https://x@host/p` | Allowed | Browsers never send this. Negligible. |
| Cross-origin `Origin: https://evil.example` | 403 | The browser never gets this far anyway (see the defences below). |
| Spoofed `Host` matching a spoofed `Origin` | **Allowed** | Browsers can't set `Host`, so this isn't a cross-site attack. DNS rebinding can produce a matching pair, but the victim's cookies for the real host aren't sent, so the attacker gains nothing they couldn't get with `curl`. **Low.** |
| `X-Forwarded-Host: evil.example` | 403 (ignored) | No bypass this way. |
| Scheme mismatch, `http://host` Origin on an `https` site | **Allowed** | The scheme is never compared. A browser treats http→https as cross-origin, so the preflight still blocks it. **Low.** |
| Different port | 403 | Correct. |
| `Host: buglab.example:443` vs Origin `https://buglab.example` | **403 for a legitimate user** | The default port isn't normalised. Availability bug. |
| Uppercase `Host` | **403 for a legitimate user** | Not lower-cased. Availability bug. |
| Proxy rewrites `Host` to the upstream (`127.0.0.1:3000`) | **403 for a legitimate user** | `X-Forwarded-Host` isn't consulted. Breaks behind such proxies. |
| `Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site` with no Origin | Allowed | Fetch-metadata headers (`Sec-Fetch-*`) are ignored. |
| Trusted proxy setting (`BUGLAB_TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS`) | No effect | It only controls the per-IP limiter, which is off by default. |
| `Secure` cookie flag | Depends on `X-Forwarded-Proto` | Over plain HTTP the client can set it; behind a TLS-terminating proxy that doesn't send it, the cookie loses `Secure`. **Low to medium.** |

## Is it exploitable now?

No, not by a cross-site attacker with the current configuration. Three independent defences stop a forged browser submission before the Origin check matters:
1. **JSON-only content type.** The browser-safe types (`text/plain`, form-encoded, multipart) all get **415**, so a cross-site JSON POST needs a CORS preflight.
2. **The preflight fails.** Next answers `OPTIONS` automatically with `204` and `Allow: OPTIONS, POST`, but no `Access-Control-Allow-*` headers, so the browser never sends the POST. I confirmed this over real HTTP.
3. **`SameSite=Strict` cookie, and nothing to steal.** A cross-site request wouldn't carry the victim's attempt cookie, and responses can't be read cross-origin. Nothing a victim has would be affected: attempts are counted per cookie, and the IP limit is off.

What depends on the deployment:
- whether legitimate users get **false 403s** (a proxy that rewrites `Host`, an explicit `:443`, uppercase hosts);
- whether the cookie gets **`Secure`**.

The weak check becomes important if any of these changes:
- CORS headers are added, or non-JSON bodies are accepted;
- Habin's session cookie uses `SameSite=Lax` or `None`;
- completion and attempts become tied to a session that a forged request could affect.

So it should be fixed **before** the session wiring lands.

## Smallest secure fix

The 403 status and body, the 415 check running first, and every other response stay exactly as they are.

1. **Check `Sec-Fetch-Site` first.**
   - If it's present, require `same-origin`; any other value gets 403.
   - Web pages can't forge this header, it doesn't depend on any proxy, and all current browsers send it.
2. **Compare full origins, not hosts.**
   - Use `new URL(origin).origin` against the expected origin, so scheme, port and case are all checked and normalised. This also stops the false 403s for `:443` and uppercase hosts.
   - Reject anything that isn't a bare `http:` or `https:` origin.
3. **Expected origin:**
   - **Production:** add an optional `BUGLAB_PUBLIC_ORIGIN` (e.g. `https://buglab.example`), checked at startup. When it's set, it's the only accepted origin and the `Host` header is never used. This removes the spoofed-Host weakness and the proxy-rewrite 403s.
   - **When it isn't set:** behave as today for local dev, but compare scheme and normalised host. The fallback should log, or be documented, as development-only.
4. **Missing Origin and missing `Sec-Fetch-Site`:** keep allowing them, for non-browser clients and older browsers. Document why. Rejecting them could break legitimate browsers that send neither header, and wouldn't stop `curl`.
5. **`Secure` cookie (optional, same review):** when `BUGLAB_PUBLIC_ORIGIN` is `https:`, always set `Secure`, instead of depending on `X-Forwarded-Proto`.

**Affected files:**
- a new `src/server/diagnosis/origin.ts`: pure, server-only, in the style of `client-ip.ts`;
- `src/server/diagnosis/handler.ts`: replaces `isSameOrigin` and adds one `expectedOrigin` option;
- `src/app/api/challenges/[challengeId]/diagnosis/route.ts`: reads the environment variable;
- `tests/diagnosis/fixtures.ts`: a test-handler option.

`src/types/diagnosis.ts`, the client, the evaluator and the rate limits are unchanged.

## Regression tests needed

**New `tests/diagnosis/origin.test.ts`, for the pure functions:**
- **`Sec-Fetch-Site`:** `cross-site`, `same-site` and `none` are rejected; `same-origin` is accepted; an absent header falls through to the Origin check.
- **Origin:**
  - `null`, malformed, non-HTTP schemes, a path, user info and a foreign scheme are rejected;
  - uppercase and default-port forms are accepted;
  - a different port is rejected.
- **Expected origin:**
  - with `BUGLAB_PUBLIC_ORIGIN` set, `Host` and `X-Forwarded-Host` are ignored, so a spoofed `Host` plus matching `Origin` is rejected and a proxy-rewritten `Host` with the real Origin is accepted;
  - with it unset, the development fallback is scheme-aware.
- **Config parsing:** an invalid `BUGLAB_PUBLIC_ORIGIN` (a path, `ftp:`, a trailing slash, garbage) fails at startup, so it never silently allows everything.

**In `tests/diagnosis/handler.test.ts`:**
- every rejection still returns exactly the 403 body and status, with `Cache-Control: no-store`, **no `Set-Cookie`** and **no change to the attempt or IP counters**;
- 415 is still returned before 403;
- a missing `Origin` and `Sec-Fetch-Site` is still accepted;
- the existing `http://localhost:3000` same-origin test still returns 200;
- with an `https` public origin, the cookie carries `Secure` even when `request.url` is `http:`.

**Unchanged and must still pass:** `tests/diagnosis/client.test.ts` and the `response`, `confidentiality` and `evaluate` tests.

**In a real browser:** a Challenge 001 submission from `/` in headless Chrome against `next start` must get a normal 200 response (the browser sends `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin`), and a cross-site page's attempt must fail at preflight.

## State

| Checkout | State |
|---|---|
| `fix/diagnosis-origin-validation` | clean at `2461037`; only ignored build output |
| Main checkout | unchanged; only your session log modified |
| Integration, `2461037` | clean |
| Challenge library, `901e64f` | clean, not touched |
| Multi-language, `89a622f` | clean |

- I stopped only the server I started on port 3419. Servers from other worktrees are still running.
- My probe script is at `C:\Users\LAPTOP~1\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\C--Users-Laptop-Zone-by-JK-buglab\6a0a38fb-42bd-4091-8443-e500d4f86a7d\scratchpad\sec\origin-probe.ts`. To get the installed packages and test loader, it ran in the integration worktree, without modifying it.

## Decisions needed

1. Should `BUGLAB_PUBLIC_ORIGIN` be optional, as proposed, or required in production builds?
2. Should the `Secure` cookie change go in this fix or be reviewed separately?

I'll wait for your approval.

I implemented the Origin fix in the security worktree: an optional `BUGLAB_APP_ORIGIN` that, when set, is the only accepted origin. All 671 tests, typecheck, lint and the production build pass, and a live browser check confirms real submissions still work. Nothing is committed.

## Changed files

| File | Change |
|---|---|
| `src/server/diagnosis/origin.ts` (new, 95 lines) | The origin rules as pure server-only functions: `normalizeOrigin`, `parseAppOrigin`, `isSameOriginRequest`, `shouldSetSecureCookie`. The header comment documents the policy and the fallback's limitations. |
| `src/server/diagnosis/handler.ts` | Removes the old `isSameOrigin`, uses the new check, and adds a required `appOrigin` option. The attempt cookie's `Secure` flag now comes from `shouldSetSecureCookie`. |
| `src/app/api/challenges/[challengeId]/diagnosis/route.ts` | Reads `BUGLAB_APP_ORIGIN` through `parseAppOrigin` |
| `tests/diagnosis/fixtures.ts` | Test handler accepts `appOrigin`, exposes its limiters, and `post` accepts a `baseUrl` |
| `tests/diagnosis/origin.test.ts` (new) | 23 unit tests |
| `tests/diagnosis/handler-origin.test.ts` (new) | 6 handler-level tests |
| `docs/architecture/diagnosis-origin-validation.md` (new) | Configuration, rules, missing-Origin policy and fallback limitations |

## How the new check works

1. **`Sec-Fetch-Site`:** if the browser sends it, it must be `same-origin`. Web pages can't set this header.
2. **`Origin`:** if present, it must be exactly a bare `http`/`https` origin and equal the expected one: same scheme, hostname and effective port. Hostnames are compared case-insensitively and default ports are normalised.
3. **Expected origin:**
   - **Configured:** exactly `BUGLAB_APP_ORIGIN`. `Host`, `X-Forwarded-Host` and `X-Forwarded-Proto` are never consulted.
   - **Unset (local development):** the `Host` header under the Origin's own scheme, as before but normalised.
4. **`Secure` cookie:**
   - **Configured:** set when the configured origin is `https`, regardless of what any proxy or client sends.
   - **Unset:** follows the request URL, as before.

**Missing-Origin policy:** a request with neither `Origin` nor `Sec-Fetch-Site` is still accepted. Only non-browser clients send that combination, and they can forge any header, so rejecting them would protect nothing and would break curl, scripts and monitors. Those requests still go through validation and rate limits.

## Before and after

| Request | Before | After, unset | After, configured |
|---|---|---|---|
| Same origin (curl, and a real browser `fetch`) | allowed | allowed | allowed |
| Cross-origin `https://evil.example` | 403 | 403 | 403 |
| `Origin: null` or malformed | 403 | 403 | 403 |
| Origin with path or user info, e.g. `https://x@host/p` | **allowed** | 403 | 403 |
| Scheme mismatch, `https://` Origin against an `http` app | **allowed** | allowed (known limitation) | **403** |
| Spoofed `Host` and `Origin` together, e.g. `evil.example` | **allowed** | allowed (known limitation) | **403** |
| Real Origin, with `Host` and `X-Forwarded-Host` spoofed | 403 | 403 | **allowed** (correct) |
| Proxy rewrote `Host` to `127.0.0.1:3000` | **403 for real users** | 403 | **allowed** (correct) |
| Uppercase hostname, or explicit `:443` | **403 for real users** | allowed | allowed |
| `Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site`, `same-site` or `none` | **allowed** | 403 | 403 |
| No `Origin` and no `Sec-Fetch-Site` | allowed | allowed | allowed |
| `Secure` cookie, TLS proxy omitting `X-Forwarded-Proto` | **missing** | missing (as before) | **set** |

Everything else is unchanged:
- A rejected request still returns `403 {"status":"error","message":"Cross-origin submissions are not allowed."}` with `Cache-Control: no-store`.
- It sets no cookie and touches no rate-limit counter.
- The 415 content-type check still runs first.
- All other responses, the client, and the learner-session code are untouched, and there are no new routes.

## Test results

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Diagnosis tests | 340/340 pass (311 existing + 29 new) |
| Full suite (`npm test`) | 671/671 pass (642 before + 29), 0 failed, 0 skipped |
| `npm run typecheck` | exit 0. The first run caught a typing mistake in my new handler test; fixed, then everything was re-run from scratch. |
| `npm run lint` | exit 0 |
| `npm run build` | exit 0; same three routes |

**The new tests catch regressions.** I temporarily put back five weaknesses, one at a time, and each made between 2 and 6 tests fail:
- comparing hosts only, ignoring the scheme;
- ignoring `Sec-Fetch-Site`;
- trusting `Host` even when an origin is configured;
- taking `Secure` from the request URL only;
- dropping the scheme and trailing-slash checks.

`origin.ts` was restored byte for byte.

**Live check against the production build:**
- I ran the curl cases from the table with `BUGLAB_APP_ORIGIN` unset and with it set to `http://localhost:3420`. The results match the table.
- In headless Chrome, a page on the app's own origin submitted with its own `fetch` and got through the check (400 means it reached validation).
- A page on a different origin (`http://127.0.0.1:3420`) was stopped at the CORS preflight.
- With an invalid value (`https://buglab.example/`), the diagnosis route returns 500 and logs `BUGLAB_APP_ORIGIN must be an origin…`, while `/` keeps working. A typo fails closed instead of accepting everything.

## Deployment configuration required

- **Set `BUGLAB_APP_ORIGIN` in every deployment.** Use the exact public origin, e.g. `BUGLAB_APP_ORIGIN=https://buglab.example`, with no path and no trailing slash.
- **Multiple hostnames:** if the app is served under more than one, pick one canonical origin and redirect the others. A list of origins isn't supported.
- **Unchanged:** `BUGLAB_TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS` still controls only the per-IP limit.

## Remaining risks and compatibility

- **Without `BUGLAB_APP_ORIGIN`:** a non-browser client can still spoof `Host` and `Origin` together, and a scheme mismatch isn't caught for clients that don't send `Sec-Fetch-Site`. Neither is a cross-site browser attack, and both are documented. Nothing enforces setting the variable in production; requiring it would be a separate decision.
- **Invalid value:** the diagnosis route returns Next's default empty 500 page instead of the JSON error shape. This only happens when misconfigured.
- **Older browsers:** they may not send `Sec-Fetch-Site`. They still send `Origin`, which is checked as before.
- **Same-origin requests elsewhere:** a browser request is allowed only with `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin`. Same-site subdomains are rejected, which is intended. This needs to be kept in mind if a future feature legitimately needs another origin.
- **Cookie attributes:** `SameSite=Strict`, `HttpOnly` and `Path=/api` are unchanged. Habin's future session cookie should use the same `shouldSetSecureCookie` logic.
- **Line endings:** the new files are LF, which git normalises to CRLF on commit, as before.

## Git status

| Checkout | State |
|---|---|
| `fix/diagnosis-origin-validation` | at `2461037`: 3 modified and 4 new files, not committed; `git diff --check` clean |
| Integration | `2461037`, clean |
| Challenge library | `901e64f`, clean |
| Multi-language | `89a622f`, clean |
| Main checkout | unchanged; only your session log modified |

I stopped only the server I started on port 3420 and the headless Chrome instances. Servers from other worktrees were left running.

I'll wait for your review.

Milestone 2 is done: a public-only catalog adapter that lists Challenge 001 and looks up its scenario by ID. All 495 tests, typecheck, lint and the production build pass, and nothing is committed.

## Changed files

Two new files; no existing file changed.

| File | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `src/lib/challenge-catalog.ts` (new) | The catalog adapter (details below) |
| `tests/challenge/challenge-catalog.test.ts` (new) | 10 tests |

`src/lib/challenge-catalog.ts`:
- **Contents:** holds only `challenge001`. A duplicate ID would throw as soon as the module loads.
- **`listChallengeSummaries(): ChallengeSummary[]`:** returns a new array on every call, ordered by challenge number (compared as numbers, so `"2"` comes before `"10"`), then by ID.
- **`getPublicScenario(challengeId): WorkspaceScenario | null`:** exact match only, through a `Map`, so keys like `__proto__` or `constructor` can't match anything.
- **`sortChallengeSummaries()`:** exported so the ordering can be tested with test-only summaries while the real catalog stays at one challenge.
- **What it imports:** only `@/data/challenge-001` and the workspace types. It is in `src/lib`, not `src/server`, because pages may import it; the confidentiality test only lets server-only files import from `src/server`.
- **Header comment:** says it is public data only, never to import answer keys, and to call it from server components rather than client components, since a client import would bundle every scenario.

The homepage, workspace, diagnosis handler, navigation, `ChallengeSummary` and `WorkspaceScenario` are unchanged. Nothing calls the adapter yet.

## Test results

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| New catalog tests | 10/10 pass |
| All `tests/challenge` tests | 24/24 pass (14 from Milestone 1 + 10 new) |
| Full suite (`npm test`) | 495/495 pass (485 before + 10), 0 failed, 0 skipped |
| `npm run typecheck` | exit 0 |
| `npm run lint` | exit 0 |
| `npm run build` | exit 0; same three routes as before |

**What the catalog tests check:**
- **Known ID:** returns exactly the existing `challenge001` object.
- **Unknown IDs:** return `null` for 12 cases, including `""`, `"001"`, a different case, leading or trailing spaces, a trailing `/`, a path, and the prototype keys `__proto__`, `constructor`, `toString` and `hasOwnProperty`.
- **Listing:**
  - Challenge 001 is the only entry, and every summary matches its scenario's challenge with unique IDs.
  - It's deterministic: changing the returned array doesn't affect the next call, and each call returns a new array.
  - Ordering is numeric with an ID tie-break, the same whatever the input order, and the input array isn't changed.
- **Public data only:**
  - The serialised catalog output contains no answer-key field names and not the resolution text.
  - Scenarios have exactly the eight `WorkspaceScenario` fields.
  - The module's code (comments excluded) imports only public data and types, and has no server, `server-only`, fetch, session, cookie or storage code.
- **The tests catch regressions:** I temporarily made five changes, one at a time. Each made 1 or 2 tests fail, and the file was restored byte for byte.
  - case- and space-tolerant ID matching;
  - non-numeric ordering;
  - sorting the input array in place;
  - importing the answer key;
  - listing an extra challenge.

**Test fix along the way:** my first run flagged the adapter's own header comment, because it mentions `*.server.ts`. I changed the source guards to ignore comments; the adapter itself didn't change.

## Note for review

Callers get a new array each time, but the summary objects inside it are the same ones Challenge 001 uses elsewhere. Code that edited a summary's fields would change it everywhere; nothing does that today. If you'd like that ruled out, freezing the summaries or returning copies is a one-line change.

- **Contents:** only the two Milestone 2 files, staged by path: `src/lib/challenge-catalog.ts` and `tests/challenge/challenge-catalog.test.ts` (227 lines added).

## Changes from the review

**Summaries are now independent copies.**
- `listChallengeSummaries()` returns a fresh `structuredClone` of each summary on every call, so the nested `tags` array isn't shared either. A shallow spread would have left `tags` shared.
- New regression test: a caller changes the title, the tags and the estimated time on one result, and the next call, Challenge 001's own data and `getPublicScenario` all stay unchanged.
- One existing test compared summaries by identity; it now compares by value.
- `getPublicScenario()` still returns the scenario object itself, as before. Its existing test checks that it is exactly the `challenge001` object.

**Source guards ignore comments but still catch imports in code.**
- They now use two helpers: `codeOnly`, which removes block comments and whole-line `//` comments, and `moduleSpecifiers`.
- `moduleSpecifiers` finds every import form: `from`, side-effect `import "x"`, `export … from`, dynamic `import()` and `require()`. The old check only caught `from`.
- Code followed by a trailing `//` comment is still checked, so the guards can only flag too much, never too little.
- Three new tests run the guards on synthetic sources:
  - forbidden names that appear only in comments are ignored;
  - all seven forbidden forms in code are caught;
  - a forbidden import sitting between comments is still found.

**The tests catch regressions.** I temporarily made three changes, one at a time:
- returning the shared object;
- a shallow copy that still shares `tags`;
- a dynamic import of the answer key in the catalog's code.

Each made one test fail. Adding the same forbidden names in a comment only did not cause a failure. The catalog file was restored before committing.

**Unchanged:** the catalog is still public-only with Challenge 001 as its only entry. The homepage, workspace, diagnosis handler, navigation and types are untouched.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
- **Stack:** Next.js 16.4.3 (Turbopack, `cacheComponents`, partialPrefetching), React 19.3, Tailwind v4, TypeScript.

  - copied to `public/monaco/0.57.0/vs`;
  - `MONACO_VS_PATH` env;
  - workers started from same-origin `blob:` URLs;
  - model URIs are `file:///challenges/<challengeId>/<path>`.
- **Diagnosis API:** `POST /api/challenges/:id/diagnosis`.
  - Check order: 415 (content type) → 403 (origin) → 404 → 413 → 400 → attempt cookie → limiters → evaluation.
  - Cookie: `buglab_attempt` UUID, `Path=/api; Max-Age=86400; HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict[; Secure]`.
  - `BUGLAB_TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS` controls the IP limiter only (off by default).
- **Next.js internals:**
  - Under `next start`, `request.url` is built from the bind host/port, with the protocol from the TLS socket or the client-supplied `X-Forwarded-Proto`.
  - The Host header is passed through unchanged.
  - Auto OPTIONS returns 204 with `Allow` only.
- **Engine:** `ChallengeSessionProvider` (in-memory reducer), `sessionConfigForChallenge`, `investigationStarted` fixed to true (TEMPORARY).
- **PR #2 session store:**
  - 256-bit base64url tokens, SHA-256 hashes;
  - idle 30d / absolute 90d / touch 1d / purge 7d;
  - `MemorySessionStore` is dev-only;
  - no attempt or completion storage and no revoke.
- **Merge simulation:** `git merge-tree --write-tree` (with `--merge-base`).
- **Browser verification:** headless Chrome over CDP, using scratchpad scripts.

- **`src/server/diagnosis/origin.ts`** (new). Exports:
  - `normalizeOrigin(value)`: exact http(s) origin, rejects a trailing "/", requires `url.href === url.origin + "/"`.
  - `parseAppOrigin(value)`: unset or blank → null; otherwise throws `BUGLAB_APP_ORIGIN must be an origin such as "https://buglab.example" (no path or trailing slash), got "..."`.
  - `isSameOriginRequest(request, appOrigin)`:
    - `Sec-Fetch-Site`, if present, must be "same-origin";
    - no Origin → true;
    - an Origin that won't normalize → false;
    - expected = `appOrigin ?? originFromHost(request, originScheme)`, where the fallback uses the Host header or the URL host.
  - `shouldSetSecureCookie(request, appOrigin)`: `(appOrigin ?? new URL(request.url).origin).startsWith("https:")`.
- **`src/server/diagnosis/handler.ts`:**
  - removed `isSameOrigin`;

I added the `/challenges/[challengeId]` route, and the library card now links to `/challenges/challenge-001`. All checks pass, but testing turned up a real problem. On a Windows machine, a single request with the wrong letter case can make the real challenge page return 404, and it stays that way after restarts until you rebuild. Nothing is committed; HEAD is still `8a4867b`.

## The problem to decide on

Next.js keeps a cache of rendered challenge pages as files on disk (`.next/server/route-cache`), named after each URL. Windows filenames ignore case, and so do macOS disks by default.

So in production mode (`next start`), here is what happened:
1. Requesting `/challenges/CHALLENGE-001` saved a "not found" page into the same file that holds `/challenges/challenge-001`.
2. From then on the real challenge page returned 404, including after restarting the server.
3. Only deleting that cache folder fixed it. I rebuilt to clear it, then confirmed the real page served 200 again.

**Who it affects:** anyone running `next start` on Windows, or on macOS with its default disk format. A Linux deployment wouldn't be affected, since Linux filenames are case-sensitive. The repo has no deployment config, so I can't tell where this will run.

A related point: every unknown ID someone requests (`challenge-999`, `__proto__`, and so on) also gets its own 404 cache files on disk. On a self-hosted server, anyone can grow that folder by requesting random URLs.

**My recommendation:** add a small `src/proxy.ts` that redirects any `/challenges/…` URL containing capital letters to the lowercase form. That runs before the cache, so a wrong-case entry can never be saved. All catalog IDs are already lowercase. I didn't add it because it changes how every request is handled, which goes beyond this milestone's scope. The disk-growth issue mainly matters if you self-host; it needs a deployment decision rather than a code change here.

## What I built

| File | Change |
|---|---|
| `src/app/challenges/[challengeId]/page.tsx` (new) | Looks the challenge up with `getPublicScenario()` and renders the existing `ChallengeWorkspacePage`; unknown IDs get `notFound()`. |
| `src/lib/challenge-routes.ts` (new) | `challengePath(id)` builds `/challenges/<id>` from a catalog ID. |
| `src/app/challenges/page.tsx` | Library links now come from `challengePath`, replacing the temporary mapping to `/`. |
| `tests/challenge/challenge-route.test.ts` (new) | 10 tests. |
| `tests/challenge/challenge-library.test.ts` | Updated for the new link destination. |

How the new route works, following the installed Next.js 16.4 docs:
- It reads the requested ID before rendering anything, so unknown IDs get a real 404 status.
- It prerenders the catalog's challenge IDs at build time (only `challenge-001` today).
- The page title is the challenge's, for example "BugLab · Challenge 001: Broken User Sessions".
- It sets `ensureStatic = "navigation"`, which makes the build fail if the route ever starts reading per-request data.

`src/app/page.tsx`, `AppHeader`, and the diagnosis, engine, session and backend code are unchanged, and the workspace isn't duplicated.

## Validation

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| All challenge tests | 50/50 |
| Full suite | 521/521, 0 failed, 0 skipped |
| Typecheck / lint | both pass |
| Production build | passes; `/challenges/challenge-001` is prerendered as a static page |
| Homepage | byte-identical (73,044 bytes) |

- **Known and unknown IDs:** `/challenges/challenge-001` renders exactly the same workspace markup as `/`. Unknown IDs, including `CHALLENGE-001`, `__proto__` and a trailing space, call `notFound()`. Removing that call makes the unknown-ID test fail, so the test does catch it.
- **Diagnosis ID:** the page passes the workspace `challenge-001`, which is the ID the diagnosis request is sent for.
- **No answer-key data:** none of it appears in the rendered page, the prerendered page files, or any browser JavaScript bundle.
- **Browser smoke test** (fresh build, headless Chrome):
  - From `/challenges`, Tab then Enter opens `/challenges/challenge-001`.
  - The editor loads, and the overview, all 5 root-cause options and the submit button are present.
  - A diagnosis request sent from that page passes the origin check; it got the normal "invalid answer" response, not a cross-origin block.
  - No third-party requests and no console errors.
  - `/`, `/challenges` and `/challenges/challenge-001` return 200; unknown IDs return 404.

## Other concerns

- **Two URLs for one workspace:** `/` and `/challenges/challenge-001` now show the same challenge. Each page load starts its own in-memory session, so nothing breaks, but you'll eventually want one to redirect to the other or name the main one.
- **The static-route guard will need loosening later:** `ensureStatic = "navigation"` will fail the build once per-learner data such as attested status (via Habin's session work) is added to this route. That part will need to be wrapped in `<Suspense>`, or the setting lowered.
- **Header on `/challenges/challenge-001`:** `AppHeader` marks "Workspace" as the current page here, which is correct. It's still wrong on `/challenges`, as noted in the last milestone.

I stopped only the server I started, on port 3164, and nothing outside this worktree was touched. Screenshots are in `C:\Users\LAPTOP~1\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\C--Users-Laptop-Zone-by-JK-buglab\6a0a38fb-42bd-4091-8443-e500d4f86a7d\scratchpad\m3\browser-m4\`.

The fix you suggested doesn't work. With `export const dynamicParams = false` added to `src/app/challenges/[challengeId]/page.tsx`, the production build fails:

```
Error: Route segment config "dynamicParams" is not compatible with `nextConfig.cacheComponents`. Please remove it.
  ./src/app/challenges/[challengeId]/page.tsx:9:14
```

The bundled Next.js 16.4 docs say the same: `dynamicParams` "is not available when Cache Components is enabled" (it was removed in v16.0.0). This project has `cacheComponents: true`.

Because the build failed, I didn't run sequences A–E or the extra validation, as Step 3 asks. The cache collision is still there.

**State of the worktree:**
- I removed the line; the page is byte-identical to the Milestone 4 version you reviewed.
- I rebuilt, and the build passes again. `/challenges/challenge-001` is prerendered and the route cache is empty.
- The same five Milestone 4 files are uncommitted on HEAD `8a4867b`.
- I added no proxy, middleware or rewrite, and touched no other worktrees.

## Options for review

None of these has been tested.

1. **Render this one route on demand instead of caching it (my recommendation).** Remove `ensureStatic` and `generateStaticParams`, and call `await connection()` before the lookup.
   - The route would then be fully dynamic, like the diagnosis API, so it would never write route-cache files. That removes both the wrong-case poisoning and the disk growth from unknown IDs.
   - It only affects this route, and unknown IDs should still get a real 404, because the lookup runs before anything is rendered.
   - The cost: the challenge page is rendered on every request instead of served as a prebuilt static page.
   - This is the narrowest fix I can see.
2. **A proxy that redirects capitalised challenge URLs to lowercase.** This stops the poisoning, but unknown lowercase IDs would still each write cache files. It also changes global request handling, which you've asked to review first.
3. **Look up challenge IDs regardless of case.** Then a collision would only ever store the same correct page. But it changes the catalog's exact-match lookup, and unknown IDs would still write cache files.

If you approve option 1, I'll apply it and run sequences A–E against a fresh production build, followed by the full validation.

No supported route-level setting meets all the requirements, so a narrowly scoped `src/proxy.ts` is the smallest fix that does. I haven't implemented it.

The worktree is back to the validated Milestone 4 state:
- HEAD is `8a4867b`, with the same five uncommitted files.
- The page is byte-identical to the version you reviewed.
- It has been rebuilt, and the route cache is empty.

## Why it happens

On every request, Next.js reads `.next/server/route-cache/…/challenges/<id>.html` from disk before any page code runs. If it has to render the page, it writes the result there, including 404s. Windows ignores case in filenames, so `CHALLENGE-001.html` and `challenge-001.html` are the same file.

Two consequences:
- Code inside the page can't stop wrong-case URLs being read, because the cache is checked first.
- The real page is overwritten whenever a wrong-case 404 is written to that file.

## Route-level alternatives considered

| Option | Result |
|---|---|
| `dynamicParams = false` | Build fails: "not compatible with `nextConfig.cacheComponents`". |
| Current version (`ensureStatic = "navigation"`) | Unknown IDs return a real 404, but each one is written to disk. Wrong-case IDs overwrite the real page, and it stays broken across restarts. |
| `await connection()` for unknown IDs only (tried on fresh builds, then reverted) | Fixes the overwrite but breaks the 404 status. Details below. |
| Other `ensureStatic` levels, `instant`, `prefetch` | None of them stop unknown IDs being cached while still returning a 404 status. |

What the `connection()` experiment did:
- **What worked:**
  - No more overwriting: the real challenge returned 200 in every sequence and after a restart.
  - Unknown IDs wrote no entries of their own; only one shared placeholder shell was saved.
- **What failed:**
  - Unknown IDs returned **HTTP 200**. That 200 response is the placeholder shell, and the "not found" page streams in afterwards with a `noindex` tag.
  - On Windows, once the real page was cached, `/challenges/CHALLENGE-001` returned **200 with the real Challenge 001 page**, because of the case-insensitive read.

So the route can avoid caching unknown IDs, or it can return a proper 404 for them, but not both.

## Proposed proxy

The proxy runs before Next.js looks for the page or its cache, using the default Node runtime, so it can reject bad IDs before anything is read or written.

**Can it use the public catalog? Yes.**
- It would build the set of allowed paths once at startup from `listChallengeSummaries()` and `challengePath()`.
- That means no second list of challenges.
- Challenge 001's public scenario gets included in the proxy's server code, but it is public data and never reaches the browser.
- It imports no answer keys or anything from `@/server/*`.

**Matcher:** `export const config = { matcher: "/challenges/:challengeId" }`.
- It matches exactly one path segment after `/challenges/`.
- It doesn't touch `/challenges`, `/`, `/api/*`, `/_next/*`, `/monaco/*` or other routes.

**Request behaviour:**
- **Allowed:** if the requested path exactly matches a catalog path (currently only `/challenges/challenge-001`), the request passes through untouched. That includes the requests Next.js makes for client-side navigation and prefetching, which use the same path.
- **Rejected:** anything else gets a 404:
  - wrong case;
  - unknown IDs;
  - other spellings of a valid ID, such as `challenge%2D001`, since the proxy compares the path without decoding it.

  To serve the 404, I'd rewrite to the app's existing static not-found page. Then nothing new is rendered or cached per ID.
- **The page keeps its own `notFound()` check** as a second line of defence.

## Concerns

- **Security:** low risk.
  - The proxy only checks the path against a fixed set; it doesn't decode or interpret it, so path-trick URLs can't get through.
  - It reads no cookies or headers and logs nothing.
  - Learner data never reaches it.
- **Caching:** checks to run once it's implemented:
  - the 404 rewrite keeps a 404 status, including for client-side navigation;
  - unknown IDs leave no route-cache entries.
- **Deployment:**
  - The overwrite only happens on Windows or macOS disks that ignore case; Linux is unaffected.
  - The disk growth from unknown IDs happens everywhere `next start` runs; on a managed host it becomes cache entries instead.
  - The proxy fixes both, and it also runs in `next dev`.
- **Maintenance:** the allowed set is fixed at startup. If Habin's registry later loads challenges at runtime, the proxy will need to read from that registry.
- **Testing:**
  - The proxy function can be unit-tested directly.
  - Next 16.4 only ships `unstable_doesMiddlewareMatch` for testing matchers, so the live sequences on a fresh build are the real check.

## Recommended smallest fix

Add `src/proxy.ts`, about 20 lines, as described above. Keep the route exactly as reviewed, with `generateStaticParams()`, exact-match lookup and `ensureStatic = "navigation"`. Then run your sequences A–E on fresh builds, and include the full validation and the browser flow.

The scripts and logs from this investigation are in `C:\Users\LAPTOP~1\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\C--Users-Laptop-Zone-by-JK-buglab\6a0a38fb-42bd-4091-8443-e500d4f86a7d\scratchpad\m3\`. Nothing is committed, and no other worktree was touched. I stopped only my own server on port 3164.

The proxy is in place and the route-cache collision is fixed. On fresh production builds:
- `/challenges/challenge-001` returned 200 in every sequence, including after restarting without rebuilding.
- Wrong-case and unknown IDs returned 404.
- Rejected IDs left nothing in the route cache.

All checks pass, and nothing is committed (HEAD is still `8a4867b`).

## Changed files

Milestone 4 now has seven files, all uncommitted.

| File | Status |
|---|---|
| `src/proxy.ts` | new |
| `tests/challenge/challenge-proxy.test.ts` | new, 10 tests |
| `tests/challenge/challenge-library.test.ts` | the list of files allowed to import the catalog now includes `proxy.ts`, which runs only on the server |
| `src/app/challenges/[challengeId]/page.tsx`, `src/lib/challenge-routes.ts`, `tests/challenge/challenge-route.test.ts`, `src/app/challenges/page.tsx` | unchanged since the reviewed Milestone 4 version |

How the proxy works:
- **Allowed paths:** built once at startup from `listChallengeSummaries()` and `challengePath()`, so there's no second list of challenges.
- **Matching:** the requested path must equal an allowed path exactly. It's compared as received, without decoding or changing case.
- **Valid paths:** pass through untouched.
- **Everything else:** gets a direct `404` with the text "Challenge not found." and `Cache-Control: no-store`. It is never redirected or rewritten.
- **Scope:** the matcher is `"/challenges/:challengeId"` only.
- **Imports:** only `next/server`, the public catalog and the path helper.

The route itself, `generateStaticParams()` and the page's own `notFound()` check are unchanged.

## Tests

- **Proxy request handling** (unit tests):
  - Challenge 001 and every catalog path pass through.
  - Query strings on valid paths pass through, including `?_rsc=`.
  - These get a 404 and are never redirected or rewritten:
    - wrong case (`CHALLENGE-001`, `Challenge-001`, `chALLenge-001`);
    - unknown IDs (`challenge-999`, `__proto__`, `constructor`);
    - other encodings (`challenge%2D001`, `challenge%2d001`, `%63hallenge-001`, `challenge-001%20`, `challenge-001%00`);
    - invalid IDs with query strings.
- **Matcher scope:** the proxy does not run on `/`, `/challenges`, the diagnosis API (in either case), `/_next/static`, `/_next/image`, `/monaco/…`, `/favicon.ico` or similar-looking paths.
- **The tests catch regressions.** I temporarily made four deliberate changes, one at a time, and each made tests fail:
  - ignoring case;
  - decoding the path;
  - widening the matcher;
  - redirecting instead of returning 404.

  The file was restored byte for byte afterwards.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| All challenge tests | 60/60 |
| Full suite | 531/531, 0 failed, 0 skipped |
| Typecheck / lint | both pass |
| Production build | passes; output now lists "ƒ Proxy (Middleware)", and `/challenges/challenge-001` is still prebuilt static |
| Homepage | byte-identical (73,044 bytes) |
| Diagnosis, engine, session, backend, data, types, `next.config.ts` | unchanged |

## Live HTTP behaviour

| Sequence | Result |
|---|---|
| A: fresh build, wrong case, then valid | 404, then 200, 200 |
| B: fresh build, valid, two wrong-case variants, valid | 200, 404, 404, 200 |
| C: fresh build, 7 unknown or alternately encoded IDs, then valid | 404 ×7, then 200 |
| D: restart without rebuilding, A–C repeated plus extra paths | Rejected IDs 404 and valid ones 200, as in A–C. Extra paths: `/`, `/challenges`, `/challenges/` and `?x=1` → 200; `/CHALLENGES/challenge-001` and `/challenges/challenge-001/extra` → Next's normal 404 page. |

- **Client navigation requests** (the RSC requests the router sends): the valid ID returns 200 `text/x-component`; wrong-case and unknown IDs return a plain 404.
- **Browser flow:**
  - From `/challenges`, Tab then Enter opens `/challenges/challenge-001` with the editor, overview, 5 root-cause options and the submit button.
  - No third-party requests and no console errors.
- **Diagnosis:** a request from the challenge page got the same "invalid answer" 400 as before; the proxy never matches the API.

## Cache behaviour

After all the sequences, the route cache held only the fixed set of entries:
- `/`, `/challenges` and `/challenges/challenge-001`;
- one shared not-found page, from the unrelated paths that didn't match any route.

There was no wrong-case entry, no entry per unknown ID, and the real challenge's entry stayed at status 200.

## Remaining risks

- **Bad links get a bare page.** An invalid challenge URL shows a plain-text 404 instead of the styled not-found page. Clicking a link to one in the app makes the browser load that plain 404 page in full. This was your stated preference; a rewrite to the styled page would need its own status check.
- **The allowed list is fixed at startup.** It's built from the catalog when the server starts, so a registry that adds challenges at runtime will need the proxy updated.
- **The proxy runs before every challenge request.** It's one exact lookup in a set. It does bundle Challenge 001's public scenario into the server-side proxy code; that data never reaches the browser.
- **Wrong-case paths that don't match the proxy.** `/CHALLENGES/challenge-001` isn't matched, but it falls to Next's shared static 404 page, so it adds no per-path cache entries.
- **Still needs separate handling:** the `/` vs `/challenges/challenge-001` duplicate URL, the future learner-status work against `ensureStatic = "navigation"`, and `AppHeader` on `/challenges`.

I stopped only my own server on port 3164 and touched no other worktree.

Your browser is reaching the wrong server. Port 3000 is the dev server running from your main checkout (`C:\Users\Laptop Zone by JK\buglab`), and that checkout has no `/challenges` page. The Challenge Library route itself isn't failing; it lives in its own worktree, which nothing serves on port 3000.

## What I found

**The server on port 3000**
- It's process 956, started on 2 November at 21:14 as a `next dev` child of process 18228.
- Both processes run from `C:\Users\Laptop Zone by JK\buglab\node_modules\next\...`, so they serve the main checkout.
- `/` returns 200 and `/challenges` returns 404.

**The main checkout** (`C:\Users\Laptop Zone by JK\buglab`)
- It's on branch `feature/landing-page` at `22e3903`.
- `src/app` contains only `api`, `welcome`, `layout.tsx` and `page.tsx`. There's no `src/app/challenges/page.tsx`.
- The library commit `eeb6ea0` isn't part of this branch, because the library work was never merged anywhere, as instructed. So a 404 there is expected.

**The Challenge Library worktree** (`C:\Users\Laptop Zone by JK\buglab-challenge-library`)
- It's on branch `feature/challenge-library-ui` at `eeb6ea097e8c5da23cd554edcffe36a7f7e47c1d`, with a clean status.
- It contains `src/app/challenges/page.tsx` and `src/app/challenges/[challengeId]/page.tsx`.
- Its `.next` production build is from after the last code change, and that build passed all the live checks earlier.
- No server is running for this worktree. I stopped my own test server on port 3164 after validation, as usual.

**Other servers running:** ports 3150, 3151 and 3152 belong to the `buglab-multilanguage-workspace` worktree, which doesn't have the library either.

## How to open the Challenge Library

Start a separate server from the library worktree on a free port, and leave port 3000 alone. In your own terminal (it keeps running, so not through me):

```
cd "C:\Users\Laptop Zone by JK\buglab-challenge-library"
npx next start -p 3160
```

Then open **http://localhost:3160/challenges**. The card leads to http://localhost:3160/challenges/challenge-001.

- **Why this way:** `next start` serves the existing, already-validated production build without changing anything, and port 3160 is free. Your main dev server on port 3000 keeps running.
- **For live editing instead:** use `npx next dev -p 3160` from the same folder.
- **To stop it:** press Ctrl+C in that terminal.

I didn't modify, stop or restart anything.

The Challenge Library redesign is done, and Challenges is now a working link in the header. All tests, typecheck, lint, the production build and the browser checks at three widths pass. Nothing is committed; HEAD is still `eeb6ea0`.

The one visible change to `/` is that "Challenges" in its header is now a link instead of a "Soon" label. Everything else on that page is unchanged.

## Changed files

| File | Change |
|---|---|
| `src/components/layout/AppHeader.tsx` | Takes a required `current` page (`"challenges"` or `"workspace"`) and handles each nav item's state (rules under Navigation). |
| `src/components/challenge/ChallengeWorkspacePage.tsx` | Passes `current="workspace"` to the header. |
| `src/components/layout/SkipLinks.tsx` | Accepts optional links; with none given, the workspace keeps its two existing skip links. |
| `src/components/challenge/ChallengeLibrary.tsx` | New page layout (described below). |
| `src/components/challenge/ChallengeCard.tsx` | Visual polish, an optional `action` slot, a `titleAs` heading level, and hover/focus styles that follow the link around it. |
| `src/components/ui/icons.tsx` | Adds `ClockIcon` and `ArrowRightIcon`. |
| `tests/ui/app-header.test.ts` (new) | 3 tests. |
| `tests/challenge/challenge-library.test.ts`, `tests/challenge/challenge-card.test.ts` | Updated for the new markup; one new card test. |

Unchanged: Challenge 001's data, the catalog, the routes, the proxy, and the diagnosis, engine, session and server code.

## Before and after

| Before | After |
|---|---|
| No app header, only a small "BugLab" label | The same BugLab header as the workspace, with "Challenges" marked as the current page |
| A small heading, one sentence and a count, with empty space on the right | A larger heading and introduction, beside a "How a challenge works" panel on desktop |
| The count sat under the heading | An "Available challenges" section header with the count on the right |
| A plain card that only changed border colour on hover | The improved card below |
| Card titles were `h2`s under the `h1` | A proper outline: `h1`, then `h2` sections, then `h3` card titles |

The "How a challenge works" panel has three steps: read the incident, investigate, diagnose. Each one describes something the workspace actually does, and nothing in it is made up.

The improved card has:
- the challenge number and difficulty badge;
- a larger title;
- the incident block and topic tags;
- a footer with a clock icon, "~30 min" and "Open workspace →".

On hover, the border turns accent-coloured, the background lightens, the title turns accent and the arrow slides slightly (it stays still for users who prefer reduced motion). Keyboard focus shows an accent outline and an accent border.

The grid has 3 columns on desktop, 2 on tablet and 1 on mobile. With one challenge it shows one card and nothing invented. An empty catalog shows a message instead.

| Page | Challenges | Workspace | Learn |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/challenges` | current page, links to `/challenges` | not clickable, tooltip "Open a challenge to use the workspace" | "Soon", not clickable |
| `/challenges/challenge-001` and `/` | link back to the library | current page | "Soon", not clickable |

- **Keyboard order on `/challenges`:** "Skip to challenges", then the Challenges link, then the Challenge 001 card. Pressing Enter on the card opens the workspace.

## Changed files

| File | Change |

| `src/components/challenge/ChallengeLibrary.tsx` | rewritten as the explorer layout |
| `src/components/challenge/ChallengeCard.tsx` | restyled as a workspace panel |

The rest of Milestone 5A is kept as it was validated: the header with its mobile fix, the skip links, the icons, the workspace page's header setting and the header tests. The `/` page renders exactly as it did after the mobile fix.

## Design changes

| Before | After |
|---|---|
| Large intro: eyebrow, 4xl heading, lead paragraph | A compact "Challenge Explorer" bar, shaped like the workspace's challenge overview bar: same border, padding, mono label, `text-xl` title and short description |
| "How a challenge works" panel | Removed |
| Large heading above a divider for the list | "AVAILABLE CHALLENGES" with a folder icon in the workspace panels' 11px uppercase style, with the count in mono on the right |
| A tall card in a 3-column grid, leaving two-thirds of desktop empty | One full-width card per challenge in a list, so a single challenge reads as a deliberate row |
| A rounded marketing-style card | A workspace-style panel card (described below) |

The new card:
- has a 36px `bg-surface-raised` title strip with a bug icon, mono "Challenge 001" and the difficulty badge, the same strip as the Explorer and Editor panels;
- below the strip, shows the title and incident on the left, and the tags, "~30 min" and "Open workspace →" on the right;
- arranges itself by its own width, not the screen's: two columns when it's at least 672px wide, stacked when narrower. So it would also work in a grid later.

The page also uses the workspace's own container: `max-w-[1600px]`, `px-4 py-4`, `gap-3`.

Unchanged: hover (accent border and accent title, arrow nudge with reduced-motion support), the focus outline and border, the skip link, and no invented challenges, statistics, progress, search or filtering.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Challenge and header tests | 64/64 |

## How logs are captured

- **Hook:** `~/.claude/settings.json` runs `python "C:/Users/Laptop Zone by JK/.claude/extract-log.py"` on every prompt and every response. There's no `.claude/` folder inside the repository.
- **What the script does:** it rebuilds the whole session log from Claude Code's transcript each time and writes it to `.claude-logs/` under the directory the session is working in. Every session so far ran from the main checkout, so all logs land in `buglab\.claude-logs\`.
- **Completeness:** each log matches its transcript in `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Laptop-Zone-by-JK-buglab/`:

  |---|---|---|
  | `57558499` | 1 prompt | 1 exchange |

  | `6a0a38fb` (this session) | 98 prompts | 96 exchanges, generated at 09:39 |

  The gap of two is just the newest prompts; the file is rebuilt on the next prompt.
- **Covers all worktrees:** the work in every worktree was done from this one session using full paths, so the single `6a0a38fb` log covers all of it.
- **One stray partial copy:** `buglab\node_modules\next\dist\docs\01-app\.claude-logs\2026-11-01_15-01-52_6a0a38fb….md`. It's 21 exchanges from 1 November, written while the session was briefly working inside that folder. It's ignored (it's under `node_modules`), and the main log supersedes it.
